- [x] FIXED — timezone bug in shift persistence. Root cause: `combineDateAndTime` (`server/src/parsing/normalize.ts`) called bare `dayjs(...)`, which parses/formats wall-clock strings in the host OS's local zone; `parseDateCell`/`parseTimeCell`'s `Date`-instance branches had the same defect on the input side (SheetJS `cellDates:true` returns UTC-based `Date`s, but `dayjs(date).format()` re-renders them in host-local time). Fix: added `dayjs/plugin/utc` + `dayjs/plugin/timezone`; `combineDateAndTime(isoDate, hhmm, timezone, nextDay?)` now takes an explicit IANA timezone (via `dayjs.tz(...)`, default export `DEFAULT_VENUE_TIMEZONE = 'Asia/Dubai'`) instead of relying on host-local parsing; `parseDateCell`/`parseTimeCell` now read `Date` instances with `dayjs.utc(value)` instead of `dayjs(value)`. `persistShifts.ts` now fetches the target `Location.timezone` from the DB and threads it through both `combineDateAndTime` calls (start/end), falling back to `DEFAULT_VENUE_TIMEZONE` only if a location record somehow has no timezone set. Verified: (1) isolated unit test proved the fix is not accidental — passing `"Asia/Dubai"` vs `"UTC"` for the identical wall-clock `17:00` produced two different, individually correct UTC instants (`13:00Z` vs `17:00Z`), and the no-arg default correctly matched the `Asia/Dubai` case; (2) re-ran the full live upload→preview→confirm flow against Supabase (same fixture/seed data as before) and independently re-queried Postgres — stored UTC instants for all 3 shifts matched the correct Dubai-offset conversion; (3) `server:typecheck`, root `typecheck`, `lint`, and the full `npm test` engine suite (8/8) all pass clean with no regressions. Test shifts deleted after verification; dev server stopped, port 4000 confirmed closed.
- [x] Prisma/PostgreSQL schema authored at `prisma/schema.prisma` (7shifts-benchmarked, Dubai-adapted): Organization→Location multi-tenancy, Role + User with fixed `baseMonthlySalary` and `isActive` turnover tracking, Shift model (date/start/end/break + `managerNotes` floor directives), `ShiftSwapRequest`/`TimeOffRequest` request engine (type, target user, `expiresAt` weekly window, `RequestStatus`, `managerNote` override audit), `AuditLog`, `AttendanceLog`, and `MonthlyAttendanceSummary` for WPS/accounting rollups. `@prisma/client`+`prisma` added to package.json, `prisma:generate`/`prisma:migrate`/`prisma:studio` scripts added, `.env.example` added. Validated via `npx prisma validate` and `npx prisma generate` (both passed).

- [x] ROOT-CAUSED and FIXED the "correct shift count but shifts missing from the grid" bug, live-tested against the real Gattopardo PDF via a throwaway diagnostic script (`extractPdfText` → `parseRosterText` → live `parseRosterImage`/Gemini, deleted after use). Root cause: Andrea/Roberto/Alessandro (the unlabeled management rows with no section header above them in this file) were getting `roleName: "COVERS"` — the VLM borrowed a stray label from the unrelated covers/pax-count panel elsewhere on the sheet. `"COVERS"` isn't in `ROLE_ALIASES` and no such `Role` exists in the DB, so `resolveRows.ts` flagged it a blocking error and `persistShifts.ts` silently skipped those 15 shifts at confirm time — while the initial parse still reported a near-correct row count. Fix: `server/src/parsing/vlmPrompt.ts` role-extraction instruction now explicitly forbids borrowing a role/section label from a different part of the sheet (covers panel, legend, notes column, totals row) — a header only counts if it sits directly above a contiguous staff block in the same column; unlabeled top rows now correctly emit `role: null`. Also fixed a related bug this uncovered: `parseVision.ts` previously **dropped** any row with a null/missing role entirely (silent data loss, worse than a wrong guess); it now flows through to DB resolution and lands in the existing "Unmatched role" manual-review queue instead. `resolveRows.ts` gives a clearer message for a genuinely blank role vs. a real spelling mismatch. Live re-verified against the same PDF: Andrea/Roberto/Alessandro now correctly resolve to `"MANAGEMENT"`. Noted but not yet addressed: the same file produced 94 rows on one Gemini call and 164 on another — real run-to-run variance worth cross-validating (see Next Sprint Goals).
- [x] FIXED a second, independently-discovered hardcoded-week bug in `src/App.tsx`: the day-grid column filter used a literal `daySuffix()` map (`Mon → '08-17'`, ..., `Sun → '08-23'`) — one specific calendar week only. Any other week's roster would render **zero shifts in every day column** despite the data being present in `mergedRoster`; this was invisible only because "today" happened to fall inside that exact week. Replaced with `weekdayOf(isoDate)`, which computes the weekday label directly from each shift's own date instead of a fixed lookup.
- [x] ADDED grid-format Excel/CSV ingestion — previously `parseWorkbookBuffer` only matched 3 fixed long-format ("one row per shift") templates and hard-rejected (422) any day-of-week-grid-shaped spreadsheet (the same layout already supported for PDFs/images), even though `deterministicParser.ts`'s `parseRotaFile` existed for exactly this shape. `server/src/parsing/parseWorkbook.ts` gained `buildMergeExpandedGrid()` (reads `!merges` and copies the top-left cell's value into every covered cell, reads the range from the sheet's own `!ref` instead of a trimmed/inferred range, applied before `sheet_to_json`) and `gridToTsvText()`; `parseWorkbookBuffer` itself now uses the merge-expanded grid too (fixes vertically-merged name/role cells in long-format sheets as a side benefit). `server/src/parsing/parseVision.ts` gained `parseRosterGrid()` — mirrors `parseRosterImage`'s retry/fallback/sample structure, but sends the merge-expanded grid as a tab-separated text block to Gemini (`callGeminiWithGridText`) instead of image bytes, reusing the exact same `vlmPrompt.ts` system prompt/schema (tweaked to describe itself as accepting either an image or a text grid) — no new LLM vendor needed. On no-API-key/error it falls back to `deterministicParser.ts`'s exported `processRowsIntoRoster()` run directly on the merge-expanded grid. `server/src/routes/schedules.ts`'s Excel/CSV branch now: try `parseWorkbookBuffer` first (unchanged behavior for real long-format templates) → on `TemplateDetectionError`, build the merge-expanded grid and route through `parseRosterGrid` instead of hard-rejecting. Verified via a synthetic merged-cell `.xlsx` built with the `xlsx` library (day headers merged across AM/PM sub-columns, `SUPERVISORS`/`RUNNERS` role-section headers merged across the full staff-block width) run through the actual pipeline: `parseWorkbookBuffer` correctly rejected it (no long-format match), the merge-expansion correctly filled every merged cell, and the live Gemini call correctly extracted all 11/11 expected shifts with the right dates/times/AM-PM periods and section-header role inheritance, 0 anomalies. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, frontend tests (11/11), and server tests (16/16) all clean — no regressions.

- [x] ROOT-CAUSED and FIXED the 94-vs-164 row-count variance on the Gattopardo PDF. Ran the real PDF through live `parseRosterImage` 5x (script deleted after use): mean 152.2 rows, stdev 27.8, one catastrophic outlier (97 rows, 66 anomalies) — NOT smooth temperature noise (temperature was already 0 in `callGemini`'s config). Diagnosis: the source PDF prints overnight shift end times as raw hours-past-midnight ("18 26" = 18:00 to 2am next day); in the bad run the model echoed "26:00"/"25:00"/"27:00" literally into the `endTime` field instead of converting to true wall-clock time, and the pipeline's time validator correctly rejects any hour ≥24 — which explains the near-uniform ~50% per-employee count drop (every overnight shift that run became an anomaly instead of a row) and the anomaly spike. Fix: added a regex `pattern` (`^$|^([01][0-9]|2[0-3]):[0-5][0-9]$`) to `startTime`/`endTime` in `ROSTER_VLM_GEMINI_SCHEMA` (`server/src/parsing/vlmPrompt.ts`) — Gemini's structured output enforces schema compliance during generation, so an hour ≥24 is now structurally impossible to emit, not just discouraged by prompt wording. Also added an explicit "OVERNIGHT-ROLLOVER RULE" paragraph to the SHIFT INTERPRETATION section explaining the conversion, and applied the same pattern to the dead/unused OpenAI-schema variant for consistency. Re-ran the same 5x live test after the fix: mean 167.6, stdev 2.9, **0 anomalies across all 5 runs**, 11/16 employees with byte-identical shift counts on every single run (the remaining 5 employees varied by only 1 shift, consistent with a few genuinely ambiguous cells like unmarked "Close" times, not a systemic failure). `npm run server:typecheck` clean.
- [x] BUILT and ran 3 synthetic stress-test rosters 3x each (in addition to the days-as-columns/merged-headers shape already validated with 11/11 correct) to check the grid-ingestion pipeline's format generality, each with hand-defined ground truth and an automated pass/fail diff: (B) days-as-rows/staff-as-columns with name+role combined in the header cell — **2/3 runs pass**; (C) flat one-row-per-shift list with headers deliberately not matching any of the 3 long-format templates, forcing the grid-text VLM fallback — **2/3 runs pass**; (D) no header row + irregular date formats ("17/8", "21 Aug") plus one deliberately invalid date ("31/2") to check it's flagged rather than silently hallucinated into a real shift — **3/3 runs pass**, including the invalid date correctly surfacing as an anomaly with a clear reason instead of becoming a phantom shift. The B/C failures were investigated directly (not assumed): re-running Test C's exact failing input in isolation immediately afterward succeeded fully both with and without the new `startTime`/`endTime` schema pattern constraint (`finishReason: STOP` both times, i.e. a clean/voluntary completion, not truncation) — confirming the schema fix above did NOT cause these failures. The B/C failures are themselves smaller-scale instances of the same run-to-run non-determinism investigated in the entry above (the model occasionally stops after the first employee/row-group on a fresh call, unrelated to input structure), not a structural inability to parse the days-as-rows or flat-list shapes specifically. This raises the priority of the double-pass cross-validation idea in Next Sprint Goals — it would catch exactly this failure mode on any format, not just the one PDF.

- [x] PIVOTED the roster-parsing architecture: deterministic parsing is now the PRIMARY path for Excel/CSV, LLM (Gemini) is the fallback/last-resort instead of the default — per-upload decision to stop depending on single-shot LLM reliability for the majority of uploads (Excel exports, digital files), which have real cell-grid structure a model shouldn't need to "read" at all. Scoped to stay Node/TypeScript-only (no Python/openpyxl/pdfplumber subprocess) — SheetJS already gave full cell-grid+merge access via last session's `buildMergeExpandedGrid()`; a Python bridge would have added a second runtime/deployment story for no functional gain on the Excel side. PDF gets a Node-native positional table reconstruction (pdfjs-dist) as a separate follow-up, since Node has no pdfplumber-equivalent with built-in table detection.
  - New `server/src/parsing/deterministicGridParser.ts` (`parseExcelGrid`) — reads a merge-expanded grid directly (no network call, fully reproducible) for day-grid rosters (day-of-week columns, optional AM/PM sub-columns, role-section header rows). Encodes both real bugs found in the LLM path as structural rules instead of prompt hopes: (1) a role/section header only ever applies to the staff rows that follow it in the same staff-name column — detects BOTH merge-expansion shapes (label only in column 0, or repeated into every column by a full-row merge) — a staff row with no header above it gets `roleName: ""`, surfaced downstream as "needs manual role assignment," never a borrowed/guessed label (the "COVERS" bug, now structurally impossible). (2) any parsed hour >=24 (the "hours past midnight" overnight notation, e.g. "26:00" or the real reference venue's literal decimal form "18.5") is always normalized by `% 24`, never passed through as an invalid literal (the overnight-hour bug, now structurally impossible). Also handles: the real reference venue's literal cell format (4 space-separated numbers = AM start/end, PM start/end, e.g. "11 17 18 25"), 2-number single-shift cells, hyphen/multi-segment single-cell ranges ("9-13", "10am/3pm-7pm/12am"), leave/absence codes (OFF/PH/AL/Sick/etc → `LeaveRecord`, not a shift or anomaly), and skips numeric-only first-cell rows (totals/headcount summary rows, a real garbage-row pattern seen in earlier live testing) instead of misreading them as staff names.
  - `server/src/parsing/deterministicGridParser.test.ts` — 7 unit/integration tests, including one exercising the REAL `buildMergeExpandedGrid()` pipeline against an actual `.xlsx` buffer with genuine `!merges` ranges (not a pre-expanded array), reproducing the exact shape already validated live against Gemini last session (11/11 correct). All 7 pass.
  - `server/src/routes/schedules.ts` router updated: Excel/CSV upload now tries (1) `parseWorkbookBuffer` — the 3 fixed long-format templates, unchanged — then (2) `parseExcelGrid` — the new deterministic day-grid parser, PRIMARY for this shape, zero network calls — then (3) `parseRosterGrid` (Gemini grid-text) only as a last-resort fallback when the deterministic parser doesn't recognize the shape at all (e.g. days-as-rows), preserving the format coverage already validated for those shapes rather than hard-rejecting. This is a deliberate deviation from the literal pivot spec (which routed all Excel/CSV to deterministic-only, no LLM branch) — kept the safety net since it was cheap (code already existed) and avoids regressing already-working coverage.
  - Verified: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, frontend tests (11/11), server tests (23/23, up from 16) all clean.
  - NOT yet done (explicitly deferred per the user's own sequencing — Excel first, validate, then PDF): (1) PDF positional table reconstruction via pdfjs-dist for text-layer PDFs (the real reference venue's actual file is a PDF, not Excel — this is the next piece needed before the pivot covers that file), (2) the router's PDF branch still uses the old text-extraction-then-Gemini-image path unchanged, (3) the Ollama/local-vision-model swap for the image/scanned-PDF LLM path, (4) the completeness safety-check + auto-retry on that Ollama path. All still Gemini-based / not started.
- [x] BUILT PDF positional table reconstruction (pdfjs-dist), completing the deterministic-primary pivot for the real reference-venue file (a PDF, not Excel). Checked first, as asked, whether the real PDF even has a text layer before writing any code: confirmed via both `pdf-parse` and `pdfjs-dist` (1722 chars extracted, real staff names + shift-number patterns present, 864 positioned text items on page 1) — genuinely a text-layer PDF, not scanned, so positional reconstruction is the right approach (Ollama vision would only be needed for a scanned/photographed roster, which this isn't).
  - New `server/src/parsing/pdfTableExtractor.ts` — `extractPdfGrid()`/`hasPdfTextLayer()`. Reads every text item's real x/y via `pdfjs-dist/legacy/build/pdf.mjs` (added as an explicit dependency; was previously only transitive via `pdf-parse`), row-clusters by y-proximity using a GAP from the previous row (not a fixed grid, so inconsistent row spacing doesn't break it), then derives column anchors from the header row's items specifically — NOT from every item on the page. That distinction mattered: a data row has far more positioned text items than visual columns (each raw number in a multi-value shift cell, e.g. "11 17 18 25", is its own item), so an initial attempt at clustering from all items fragmented one logical day-column into ~6 spurious ones (44 columns instead of ~8) and produced 0 usable rows. Anchoring on the header row's items alone fixed it in one pass — went from 0 rows to 160. A trailing anchor catches whatever sits right of the last day column (a notes/legend column on the real file) so that text doesn't bleed into the last day's cell and break its parsing. A continuation-line merge pass (single populated column + gap tighter than the page's typical row spacing) reassembles a role header wrapped across two lines. The reconstructed grid is fed into the exact same `parseExcelGrid()` already built for Excel — no second parsing engine, per the explicit instruction.
  - Fixing the real file's exact 21-employee layout surfaced two more real bugs in `deterministicGridParser.ts`'s role-header detection, both now fixed (shared engine, so Excel gets these fixes too): (1) the role-header rule assumed the label always sits in column 0; on this file the section labels (SUPERVISORS/HEAD WAITERS/WAITERS/RUNNERS) are visually centred and land in whichever middle day-column their x-position is closest to, so they were never being detected at all. (2) that same "row full of blanks + one label" heuristic was misfiring on real staff members who simply have zero shifts that week (on leave/PH the whole week, e.g. Sintia/Tomas/Irma/Tatenda/Rowel) — their own name was the "lone value," so it got used as the role for every employee listed after them. Fixed by reusing the existing `ROLE_ALIASES` vocabulary (`canonicalRoleName` from `resolveRows.ts`) as the disambiguating signal: a lone value only becomes the current role if it actually resolves to a known role keyword; a shift-less employee's own name doesn't, so it's correctly treated as a zero-shift staff row instead of a header, leaving `currentRole` untouched for whoever comes next. Also reordered role-keyword detection to run BEFORE the numeric-totals-row skip, since a real section-header row on this file happened to share its reconstructed line with an unrelated stray headcount number in column 0.
  - Result against the real file: **160 shift rows**, all 16 labeled/unlabeled-correctly employees matching the ground truth independently established during the earlier VLM investigation (Andrea/Roberto/Alessandro → `""`, correctly unresolved and flagged for manual review, never a guessed label; Pratik/Derrick → `SUPERVISORS`; Rojina/Rafael/Robert → `HEAD WAITERS`; Hefny → `WAITERS`; Bashkar/Ismail/Ranis/Mohamed/Oumaima/Subash/Zakir → `RUNNERS`), and exactly 2 anomalies — both the non-employee "COVERS" covers-count row's two unparseable annotation cells, correctly flagged rather than silently mishandled. 160 sits inside the live-Gemini post-fix baseline's tight cluster (162–170 across 5 runs, mean 167.6) — with the deterministic path there is now zero run-to-run variance at all, since no model call is involved. Known gap: the trailing notes/legend column's per-employee annotations (Holiday/Closing/Request/Sick/PH/Training/BDP) aren't interpreted as leave records the way the VLM path does — employees who are on leave the entire week (Sintia, Tomas, etc.) currently produce zero output rather than a `LeaveRecord`, since that data lives outside the 7 day columns this parser reads. Not a regression (those employees produced 0 shift rows either way) but a real feature gap versus the VLM path, worth a follow-up if leave visibility for all-leave weeks matters.
  - `server/src/parsing/pdfTableExtractor.test.ts` — 3 tests: (1) the real reference-venue PDF (`server/test-fixtures/real-roster.pdf`, already a committed fixture from an earlier session) through the full `extractPdfGrid` → `parseExcelGrid` pipeline, asserting the exact 160-row/role-by-employee/anomaly result above; (2) a synthetic PDF (built with `pdf-lib`, added as a devDependency) with a role header wrapped across two lines, verifying the continuation-merge reassembles it correctly; (3) a synthetic PDF with deliberately irregular row-to-row spacing (20pt/35pt/14pt gaps), verifying rows stay correctly separated rather than being spuriously merged. All 3 pass. The synthetic fixtures produce a benign pdfjs font-substitution console warning (pdf-lib's non-embedded Helvetica doesn't match pdfjs's fallback font file) — doesn't affect correctness, doesn't occur on the real PDF (which has an embedded font), not worth chasing further. Also fixed a similar warning on the real path by pointing pdfjs at its bundled `standard_fonts` directory via `standardFontDataUrl`.
  - `server/src/routes/schedules.ts` PDF branch restructured into the same layered fallback shape as Excel/CSV: `hasPdfTextLayer` check first (no positional reconstruction is attempted at all for a scanned/image-only PDF) → deterministic `extractPdfGrid`+`parseExcelGrid` as PRIMARY for a text-layer PDF, zero network calls → falls back to the existing line-oriented `parseRosterText` for a flat-list-shaped text PDF the grid reconstruction correctly declines to recognize → falls back to the existing Gemini vision path only as the last resort (scanned PDFs, or a layout neither deterministic path handles).
  - Verified: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, frontend tests (11/11), server tests (26/26, up from 23) all clean.
  - NOT yet done: the Ollama/local-vision-model swap for the remaining LLM path (still Gemini), and the completeness safety-check + auto-retry on that path — both explicitly out of scope for this step per the user's own sequencing.
- [x] ADDED leave-annotation capture to the shared deterministic grid parser (`deterministicGridParser.ts`, so both the Excel and PDF paths get it): when an employee has zero shift/leave data across every day column, any OTHER non-blank column on their row (a notes/comments column outside the day-date range) is checked for a known leave code (`LEAVE_CODES`, same vocabulary `parseCellValue` already uses per-cell); a match becomes an explicit `LeaveRecord` dated at `weekStart` instead of the employee silently vanishing with zero output. Deliberately narrow: only fires when the day columns were completely empty, so a real working week with unrelated trailing text (e.g. a "Closing" note next to someone who worked normally) is never misread as an absence — verified directly with a control case (`Andrea` worked + had a "Closing" note → 0 leave records; `Sintia`/`Tomas` had zero shifts + a leave-code note → 2 leave records; `Irma` had zero shifts + no note → correctly nothing).
  - Real-file finding worth knowing: the reference-venue PDF's trailing column that this feature reads from is actually a **static legend/key box** (11 fixed entries: Holiday/1920/Closing/Request/Horizon/Sick/Unpaid/PH/Training/BDP/Gatto Floor — independently confirmed against the legend array Gemini extracted from the same file in an earlier session), not genuine per-employee annotations — it only produces real leave records for Sintia ("PH") and Tomas ("Request") on this file because their zero-shift rows happen to coincide, row-for-row, with two of the legend's 11 lines. Even the Gemini vision path (reading actual cell colours, which text extraction can't see) produced 0 leave records for this exact file, meaning there was no confidently-visible leave marking on it either. Surfacing the coincidence anyway was a deliberate choice: a manager seeing "Sintia — PH" for review can dismiss a wrong guess in seconds, which is strictly better than the prior silent-zero-rows behavior, and on a real file where a manager genuinely writes "Sick" next to a blank row (the common case this feature targets) it's exactly correct.
  - `server/src/parsing/deterministicGridParser.test.ts` gained 1 test (control case above). `server/src/parsing/pdfTableExtractor.test.ts`'s real-PDF test updated to assert the exact `Sintia:PH` / `Tomas:Request` leave records, with the coincidence documented inline.
  - Verified: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, frontend tests (11/11), server tests (27/27, up from 26) all clean. No change to shift-row count (still 160) or anomaly count (still 2) on the real file — purely additive.
- [x] SWAPPED the Gemini vision last-resort fallback for a local Ollama vision model, closing the pivot's last item — the image/scanned-PDF path (now genuinely only reached when neither deterministic path applies) no longer depends on an external API at all. Checked local setup first: Ollama was installed and running (v0.32.14) but had no vision-capable model pulled (only `llama3`, `qwen2.5-coder`) — asked which one to pull given it's a real multi-GB download; picked **qwen2.5vl:7b** (~6GB) over `llava`/`llama3.2-vision` for being specifically strong at dense document/table/OCR-style reading, closer to this task than general-purpose captioning.
  - New `server/src/parsing/parseVisionOllama.ts` (`parseRosterImageOllama`) — POSTs to the local Ollama `/api/chat` endpoint (`OLLAMA_HOST` env, default `http://127.0.0.1:11434`; `OLLAMA_VLM_MODEL` env, default `qwen2.5vl:7b`), reusing the exact same `ROSTER_VLM_SYSTEM_PROMPT` and `mapVlmResponseToResult` mapping as the Gemini path — only the transport and the JSON-schema shape passed for structured output differ (Ollama's native `format` parameter takes a plain JSON Schema, so the previously-unused `ROSTER_VLM_JSON_SCHEMA` — originally written for OpenAI, dead code since the Gemini switch — became genuinely useful again instead of needing a third schema variant). Exported `VlmResponse` from `parseVision.ts` (was a local-only type) so this module could reuse it without duplicating the shape.
  - Completeness-retry check, as specified: a cheap, narrow "list only the staff names you see" call runs first as an independent baseline (much smaller output, far less likely to itself truncate than the full extraction) — if the full structured extraction is missing any name that baseline call found (checked across shift rows, anomalies, and leave records, not just rows), it retries the full extraction once, silently, before ever returning to the caller. Still-missing names after the retry are logged server-side (not blocking) so a manager reviewing the preview will still visibly notice whoever's absent.
  - `server/src/routes/schedules.ts` — both Gemini `parseRosterImage` call sites (the direct image-upload branch, and the PDF-fallback-to-vision branch) replaced with `parseRosterImageOllama`. Deliberately scoped narrow: `parseRosterGrid` (the Gemini **text**-mode fallback for an Excel/CSV grid shape the deterministic parser doesn't recognize, e.g. days-as-rows) was left on Gemini — the request specifically named "vision model" and "images/scans," and swapping a text-only call to a vision model doesn't fit; flagged this scoping choice rather than assuming. No Gemini fallback-of-the-fallback was added if Ollama itself fails (not running / model not pulled) — surfaces as a clear `VisionIngestionError` instead, per "I don't want an API dependency even for the rare fallback case" (a silent fallback to Gemini would have reintroduced exactly that dependency).
  - `server/src/parsing/parseVisionOllama.test.ts` — 2 mocked tests (via `ollamaHttp.fetch`, see below) verifying the retry logic deterministically: retries exactly once and returns the complete result when a name is missing; does NOT retry when nothing is missing. Both pass.
  - LIVE-TESTED against the real reference-venue roster image (`server/test-fixtures/real-roster.png`) once qwen2.5vl:7b finished pulling, and hit two real bugs in the process (both fixed): (1) **400 "exceeds context size"** — Ollama defaults to a 4096-token context window regardless of what the model supports unless explicitly overridden per-request; the real prompt+schema+image needed ~5973. Fixed by setting `options.num_ctx` (env `OLLAMA_NUM_CTX`, default 16384) on every call. (2) **A premature timeout that fired independently of the code's own AbortController** — Node's built-in global `fetch` (undici under the hood) has its own internal `headersTimeout` defaulting to ~300s that killed the request at ~357s even with a 600s AbortController configured. Fixed by routing through the `undici` npm package directly (added as an explicit dependency) with a custom `Agent` whose `headersTimeout`/`bodyTimeout` actually match `OLLAMA_TIMEOUT_MS` — exposed as an exported `ollamaHttp = { fetch }` object specifically so the existing mocked tests could keep mocking a stable target instead of the ambiguous global.
  - Real-hardware finding, discussed and a decision made rather than assumed: even after both fixes and raising the timeout to 10 minutes, the full structured extraction on this specific 21-employee/160+-shift reference image still hadn't completed on this machine (RTX 4050, 6GB VRAM — the 6.9GB model doesn't fully fit, so Ollama runs a ~50/50 CPU/GPU split, and CPU-assisted generation of a large structured JSON response is slow). Decision: **ship as-is** — this is the rare last-resort path (Excel and text-layer PDFs never reach it after the earlier pivot work), the reference file is an unusually dense stress-test case, and staying fully local with no API dependency was the explicit point of this swap. Real photo uploads from smaller venues are expected to be lighter and may well complete comfortably, but that's unverified until real usage confirms it.
  - Given that finding, the live integration test is now gated behind `RUN_OLLAMA_LIVE_TEST=1` (skipped by default with a clear reason) rather than running — and potentially failing on slow hardware — as part of the normal fast test suite; run it manually when checking actual throughput on a given machine/model.
  - Verified: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, frontend tests (11/11), server tests (29/30 — the live test correctly self-skipped by design, not a failure) all clean, full suite runs in ~4s (not 10+ minutes).
- [x] GENERALIZED role/section-header detection in `deterministicGridParser.ts` — it was entirely vocabulary-coupled (`canonicalRoleName`/`ROLE_ALIASES`), meaning any venue's own section wording not already in that hospitality-role word list (e.g. "FOH TEAM", "BAR CREW") would never be recognized as a header at all and its staff would just fall through as unlabeled. Added a second, pattern-based signal: `isRoleHeaderLabel()` now also recognizes a lone ALL-CAPS label as a header **once at least one real staff row has already been seen** in the parse — the position gate is what tells a genuine role-section header (which always sits inside the staff listing, splitting one group from the next) apart from some other all-caps sheet caption before the listing starts (a covers-count panel title, a legend heading) that has nothing to do with role grouping. First attempt without that position gate immediately regressed the original "COVERS" bug (an unrelated all-caps caption at the top of the real reference file got treated as a header again) and broke ALL-CAPS leave codes ("OFF", "UNPAID") colliding with the new pattern — both caught by re-running the existing test suite, not just the new tests, and fixed: leave codes are now checked and excluded first, unconditionally.
  - 2 new tests added: a synthetic grid using section labels genuinely absent from `ROLE_ALIASES` ("FOH TEAM", "BAR CREW") confirms the generalization actually extends coverage, not just re-detecting words already in the vocabulary; a second reproduces the "COVERS"-before-any-staff-row shape directly to guard against the exact regression that surfaced during development. All 10 grid-parser tests + all 3 PDF-extractor tests (including the real reference file, still exactly 160 rows/correct roles) pass with zero change to existing behavior on Gattopardo — the generalization is additive, invisible on a file whose vocabulary was already fully covered.
  - **Honest coverage picture, as asked for** — what's still NOT handled after this pass: (1) a venue that writes every staff name in the sheet in capital letters (a style choice, not a header signal) will still misfire, same failure class as a coincidental vocabulary match before, just a different trigger; (2) a genuinely novel-vocabulary section header that is the very first labelled group in the sheet, with no unlabeled staff rows above it — the position-gate requires at least one prior staff row, so a bare pattern match won't catch that specific case (only the vocabulary-match path would, if the word happens to be a known one); (3) **not yet validated against a second real file** — the user's own explicit ask was "add Bar des Pres's actual file as a second permanent fixture and get both passing," and that file wasn't available locally (searched Downloads and the user profile, not found) — asked the user to provide it; this generalization is evidence-based only against the one real file already in the repo (Gattopardo) plus hand-built synthetic cases, not yet against a second independently-sourced real-world layout. Do not describe this as "now handles any file" until that validation happens.
  - Also **not addressed in this pass** (out of scope, not attempted): the header-row-detection side (`findHeaderRows`) still assumes the AM/PM sub-header row sits immediately below the day-name row with nothing in between, and still only recognizes one overall header shape (day-columns, optional AM/PM row) — a file with a materially different header structure (e.g. a spacer/venue-name row between the date row and the AM/PM row, or no AM/PM row concept at all) has not been tested and may not be recognized. Role-header detection was the specific, scoped ask; broader header-shape generalization is a separate, larger piece of work.
- [x] REMOVED the paste-text parser tab from the UI entirely, per explicit request — only file upload (Excel/CSV/PDF/image) remains. `src/App.tsx`: removed the `parseRosterText` call, the `text`/`setText` state, the `SAMPLE` constant, the "Parser" `<textarea>` section, and the now-always-empty "Warnings"/"Unparsed lines" sections that only ever surfaced issues from that removed text parser. `roster` (the base `mergedRoster` starts from before any upload) is now a small locally-constructed empty shell (`{id, venueId, weekStart, employees: [], shifts: [], createdAt}`) instead of the text-parser's output — `Dashboard` still needs a valid `weekStart` for its date-range display, which this preserves. Cleaned up the now-fully-unused `.parser`/`.warnings`/`.paste-area` CSS rules from `global.css` (`.roster` and `.hint`, which the removed sections shared class names/groupings with, are still used elsewhere and were kept). Note: `src/engine/parser.ts` (the underlying text-parsing engine) and its own test suite (`src/engine/parser.test.ts`) were NOT touched — only the App.tsx UI wiring that called it was removed; the engine module itself still exists and is still unit-tested, just no longer reachable from the UI.
  - Verified: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, frontend tests (11/11) all clean.
- [x] ADDED Bar des Pres as the second permanent fixture and closed 4 real format gaps it surfaced, plus 1 more found while building the reference test. Real file provided by the user ("April 13-19.pdf") — checked first (as established practice this session) whether it has a text layer: **it does not** (0 positioned text items via pdfjs-dist; pdf-parse extracted 16 characters of pure boilerplate, no roster content) — almost certainly an Excel sheet exported/printed as a flat image, structurally unlike Gattopardo's genuine text/vector PDF. This means it can't run through `extractPdfGrid`/`hasPdfTextLayer` the way Gattopardo does — no automated "real file → grid" extraction test exists for it. Per the user's explicit direction, added it anyway as a **hand-transcribed documented reference fixture** (`server/src/parsing/barDesPresReference.test.ts`) — the real, independently-sourced content read directly off the rendered image, testing the interpretation side (`parseExcelGrid`) against real complexity rather than synthetic approximations. 5 tests, all pass.
  - **(1) Per-row title column.** This file gives an explicit title per person (RM, AM, JAM, Supervisor, Head waiter 1-5, Waiter 1-4, Runner 1-5, Chef de pass) in its own column before the name — a structurally different shape from Gattopardo (role only ever via section header). `parseExcelGrid` now infers a title column from where the day columns actually start (`firstDayColIndex >= 2` → column 0 is a per-row title, the column just before the first day column is the name) rather than assuming column 0 is always the name — general by position, not hardcoded to this venue. Per-row title takes precedence over section-header-derived role when present, falls back to it when blank. Required a genuine restructure of the row-classification logic (single-title-column and two-title-column shapes discriminate "is this a header row?" differently — content-based vs. position-based) with a shared `processStaffRow` inner closure so both paths reuse the same per-cell logic instead of duplicating it. Re-ran the full existing suite (not just new tests) after the restructure specifically because the first version silently regressed the earlier PDF-reconstruction stray-number fix; caught and fixed before it shipped.
  - **(2) "UL" added to `LEAVE_CODES`** as Unpaid Leave, confirmed with the venue.
  - **(3) Shorthand codes** ("10IN", "12CL", bare "IN"), exact meanings confirmed with the venue: open-ended (real start, no end), until-closing (real start, ends at an unspecified closing time), and fully flexible (no fixed start or end) respectively. **Scoped down from the literal ask**: requested as "not an anomaly," implemented as a distinct `CellParseResult` kind (`'flagged'`) routed through the *existing* `anomalies` array with a clear structured reason and higher confidence (0.7, vs. 0.2 for genuinely unparseable garbage) — signalling "understood, needs a decision" rather than "couldn't read this." A true separate "open-ended shift" concept in the DB/UI would need `ParsedShiftRow.startTime`/`endTime` to stop being both-required (`Shift.endTime` is a required DateTime column), which is a real schema change across the DB, persistence, and UI layers — out of scope for a parser-generalization pass. Flagged this deviation explicitly rather than silently reinterpreting the ask.
  - **(4) Hyphen-chained 4-number split shifts** ("10:30-4:00-8:00-12", no slash) now parse as two back-to-back shifts, distinct from the existing slash-separated multi-segment form. Bare numbers are read as literal 24h hours, consistent with every other numeric cell this parser already handles — for this specific example that means "4:00" reads as 04:00, not the almost-certainly-intended 16:00 (10:30am-4:00pm is a far more natural lunch shift than a 10:30pm-4:00am one). Not fixed with an unverified AM/PM-guessing heuristic; documented as an open interpretation risk in both the code and the reference fixture rather than silently guessed.
  - **(5, found building the reference test, not part of the original 4-item ask):** this file stacks *two* header rows — numeric dates ("13-Apr") then weekday names ("Monday") directly below, with no AM/PM row at all. `findHeaderRows` only knew how to skip past an AM/PM row; without a fix the weekday-name row would have been mistaken for the first staff row. Fixed generally: after the date row, if the next row isn't an AM/PM row, check whether it independently resolves >=2 more dates itself (same test the date row passed) and skip it too before data starts.
  - **Real findings surfaced by testing against actual messy data, not fixed in this pass:** an employee whose entire transcribed week is leave codes and/or flagged shorthand (Robert Orgovan/RM, Shovit Shrestha/AM, George Karanja/Chef de pass — all real, all in the reference file) ends up with **zero** `ParsedShiftRow` entries, and their role is recorded nowhere in the output at all — `AnomalyRecord` and `LeaveRecord` both carry no role field. Their name still appears (in anomalies/leave records), but a manager reviewing the preview has no way to see what their job title even is unless they also show up with a real shift some other week. Not fixed here — flagged for a decision on whether it's worth threading `roleName` through both types.
  - Full suite re-verified after all 5 changes: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, frontend tests (11/11), server tests (41/42 — the 1 skip is the gated Ollama live test, not a failure, up from 27) all clean. Gattopardo's real-file test unchanged throughout at exactly 160 rows/correct roles — every Bar des Pres-driven change was additive there too.
  - **Honest overall coverage picture, as explicitly asked for — do not describe this as "handles any roster file":** validated against exactly 2 real files (Gattopardo: genuine text-layer PDF, day-columns-only, section-headers-only; Bar des Pres: image-only PDF tested via hand transcription only, per-row-titles-plus-section-headers). Known NOT handled: (a) a venue that writes every staff name in ALL CAPS (style choice, not a header signal) will misfire the role-header pattern; (b) a novel-vocabulary section header that's the very first labelled group with no unlabeled staff above it won't be caught by the pattern signal; (c) the header-shape detection still only recognizes "day-columns, optional single AM/PM row, optional single stacked weekday-name row" — a materially different header structure hasn't been tested; (d) the AM/PM ambiguity in bare hyphen-chained numbers is unresolved, documented not fixed; (e) role is invisible for an employee whose whole week is leave/flagged codes.
- [x] BUILT the Staff Directory + Management tier — the last piece of the original 3-item ask (numbered #1/#2 in the request, done last per the user's own priority order). Directly closes the exact gap the parser generalization work exposed: Andrea/Roberto/Tomas/Alessandro (Gattopardo's unlabeled top rows) correctly get `roleName: ""` from an upload — the parser declining to guess is the *right* behavior — but the frontend grid previously had no way to know they're actually management, so they fell into "Other." The Staff Directory lets a venue say so once, manually, and the grid now trusts that over whatever (if anything) a given week's upload resolved.
  - **Backend, persisted (not frontend-only/localStorage):** staff already exist as `User` records per `Location` with a real Postgres-backed DB, so this extends that rather than inventing a parallel store. `prisma/schema.prisma`: added `User.jobTitle String?` (distinct from `role`/`Role`, which is the shift-scheduling category like Waiter/Runner — `jobTitle` is the venue's own specific title, e.g. "Restaurant Manager" vs "Assistant Restaurant Manager"). Migration `add_user_job_title` applied to the live Supabase DB. New `server/src/routes/staffDirectory.ts`: `GET /api/staff-directory/:locationId` (list), `POST /api/staff-directory` (add), `PATCH /api/staff-directory/:userId` (edit name/title) — no DELETE endpoint, kept deliberately out of scope for "simple." Mounted in `app.ts`.
  - **Frontend:** `src/api/staffDirectory.ts` (client, mirrors `schedules.ts`'s conventions) and `src/components/StaffDirectory.tsx` — a collapsible panel below Upload Roster: a table of current staff with an inline-editable job-title field (saves on blur) plus a small add-staff-member form. Matches the existing dark-luxury design system (`.preview-table`/`.btn`/`.hint` classes reused; a handful of new `.staff-directory-*` rules added to `global.css`).
  - **Management tier:** `App.tsx`'s existing `ROLE_SECTIONS` already had a `manager` tier positioned first (above Supervisor) — relabeled "Manager" → "Management" to match the ask, and added `sectionForEmployee()`, which checks the Staff Directory FIRST (by exact case-insensitive name match) before falling back to the existing parsed-role matching. Management-title detection is pattern-based (`/manager|management/i` on the job title), not a fixed word list — deliberately consistent with this session's broader "pattern over hardcoded vocabulary" direction, so it works for whatever specific title wording a venue actually uses without needing every variant enumerated.
  - **Verified:** `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, full frontend+server test suites (11/11 + 41/42, no regressions) all clean. Live-tested the backend against the real Supabase DB (not just typechecked): `GET` returned the real seeded Gattopardo staff, `POST` created a test entry, `PATCH` updated its job title and a follow-up independent `GET` confirmed the change actually persisted (not just echoed back), then the test record was deleted via a direct Prisma call to avoid leaving fake data in the DB, and the dev server was stopped afterward. **Not visually verified** — no browser/screenshot tool available (per the project's own established gotcha); the panel's actual rendering, collapse/expand behavior, and inline-edit UX have not been seen, only reasoned through against the existing component patterns.
  - **Known scope/precision limits, stated plainly:** (1) name matching between the roster grid and the Staff Directory is exact-normalized-string (lowercase+trim) — "Andrea" in an upload must match "Andrea" (or the venue must enter it that way) in the directory; no fuzzy matching. (2) No delete/deactivate UI for a directory entry. (3) The Management-tier cross-reference only affects grid *grouping* — it doesn't change what role gets persisted to the `Shift` record in the DB (that's still whatever `resolveRowsAgainstDatabase` resolved at confirm time), so a manager pulling data directly from Postgres would still see the upload's own resolved role, not the Staff Directory's.

As a real **STAFF** session (the real seed user Ahmed Khan): `MARK_AVAILABILITY` (`"mark me unavailable on September 5th 2026"` → parsed correctly → executed → a real `AvailabilityMark` row created) and `REQUEST_SWAP` (`"request a swap with Fatima Al Suwaidi for my shift on September 2nd 2026"` → parsed correctly, `shiftId`/`targetUserId` both matched the real intended shift/person → executed → a real `PENDING ShiftSwapRequest` created) both ran parse-then-execute end to end and were independently confirmed, via a direct Prisma query (not just the API response), to be scoped to Ahmed's own `userId` — never a client-suppliable value. All 3 resulting `AuditLog` rows were independently re-queried directly from the DB and confirmed to carry the real `[voice] "<verbatim transcript>"`-prefixed note and the correct actor id (the manager's id for the approve, Ahmed's own id for both self-scoped staff actions). Confirmed the `GEMINI_API_KEY`-unset path surfaces a real, typed `503` (`"GEMINI_API_KEY is not configured on the server — voice intent parsing is unavailable."`) rather than a bare 500, by starting a second dedicated server instance with `GEMINI_API_KEY` blanked only in that process's own environment (the real `.env` was never touched). Did not re-run Task 6's own adversarial cross-tenant/malformed-input attacks (already exhaustively covered across its 2 fix rounds) — this pass targeted proving the whole pipeline genuinely works end-to-end for a legitimate user in both roles, which it does.

Both dedicated server instances (ports 4010/4011) were stopped by PID (`Stop-Process -Id 19212,14748 -Force`, both confirmed to be this task's own `tsx server/src/index.ts` processes via their real `CreationDate` before being touched) — real `netstat`/`tasklist` output afterward confirms no LISTENING socket remains on either port (only harmless `TIME_WAIT` entries) and both PIDs are gone from `tasklist`. Neither `/api/voice/transcribe` nor `/api/voice/parse-intent` has any rate limiting, despite both calling paid external Gemini endpoints behind only a session-gate. Missing regression test coverage, named specifically rather than left implicit: `REQUEST_SWAP`'s shift-ownership check (a session user naming a shift they don't own), and `/transcribe`/`/parse-intent`'s `401` (no session) and `503` (`GEMINI_API_KEY` unset) paths — all real, all worth a follow-up, none blocking.
- [x] CLOSED the AI Voice Intent phase's **final whole-branch fix wave** (2 Important + 8 Minor cross-task integration issues that no single task's scoped review could see — `.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-27-ai-voice-intent/fixwave-report.md`).
  - **[IMPORTANT] Venue-timezone correctness in the voice prompt context.** `parseIntent.ts`'s `buildContext` computed `today` as `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` — the **UTC** calendar date. For an `Asia/Dubai` (UTC+4) venue, 00:00-04:00 local (exactly when a closing shift ends) is still the previous day in UTC, so a spoken "tomorrow"/"this Friday" resolved **one calendar day early**; the result is still a syntactically valid date, so `/execute`'s round-trip validity check correctly let it through and silently wrote an `AvailabilityMark` for the wrong day. Same root cause rendered `callerShifts`/`pendingSwapRequests` times as raw UTC. Fixed by mirroring the convention `server/src/routes/shifts.ts` already uses: 2 new helpers in `server/src/lib/venueTime.ts` (`venueToday(tz)`, `venueTimezoneFor(locationId)`) alongside the existing `formatVenueTime`, which now formats every time field in the prompt. `Shift.date` deliberately left alone — it is stored as UTC-midnight-of-the-venue-local-day, so slicing it was already correct. Verified empirically: at `2026-08-27T21:00Z` the old code said "today is 2026-08-27" when Dubai was already on the 28th, and rendered a 01:00 shift end as "21:00".
  - **[IMPORTANT] Mic-permission double-tap leaked a live `MediaStream`.** `startVoiceRecording` awaited `getUserMedia` before ever setting `voiceOn`, so during the browser's permission prompt the button stayed tappable with `voiceOn` still false; two recorders started, `mediaRecorderRef` held only one, and the loser's stream stayed open with nothing able to stop it (mic indicator lit until reload). Fixed with a **synchronous ref** guard (`voiceStartingRef` — a state check cannot catch a same-tick second tap), cleared in a `finally` so a denied prompt doesn't wedge the button, plus a new `starting` prop on `RadialDock` so the keystone button is visibly disabled during the prompt.
  - **Also fixed:** voice could re-decide an already-DECLINED swap request (`decideSwapRequest`'s `'conflict'` only ever means "a *different* request already reassigned this shift" — `isRequestLocked` returns `false` for any non-PENDING request — so the "already decided" case was never guarded at all; the `sr` lookup now selects `status` and 409s accurately, with a new regression test); `UNRECOGNIZED`'s `reason` was computed but never rendered; the transcript itself was never shown in the confirm sheet (the whole point of confirm-before-execute is catching a mishearing, which a paraphrased summary cannot reveal); Cancel/backdrop stayed clickable while the mutation was already in flight; no max recording duration (now 45s auto-stop through the same path a manual second tap takes — the only prior backstop was multer's 10MB cap surfacing as a confusing "File too large" *after* discarding the recording); the `GEMINI_API_KEY is not configured…` 503 message reached the end-user banner verbatim (now logged server-side, generic message on the wire); `RadialDock`'s idle mic label still read `'Toggle AI voice assistant'` (an always-on listener) rather than tap-to-record; `VOICE_MODEL`'s default was duplicated as two independent literals (extracted to `server/src/voice/model.ts`); and `audio/mp4` sat above the webm fallback despite the list's own "Gemini-documented first" rationale being what demoted webm (mp4 is not in Gemini's documented list either — now grouped with webm as an equally-unconfirmed fallback, comment made to match).
  - **Verification, all clean:** `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings), `npm run build` (same pre-existing chunk-size warning), `npm test` (23/23), `npm run test:server` (**91 total: 89 pass / 2 skipped by design**, up from 90 by exactly the 1 new already-decided-swap regression test). No dev server was started for this wave — the timezone fix was proven with a standalone dayjs script instead.
  - **Explicitly parked, unchanged:** decide-intents do not re-validate that a swap's target user is still active/same-location before reassigning (a real gap, but symmetric with pre-existing REST behavior and not unique to this plan); the non-atomic double-audit-row write; and rate limiting on the Gemini-calling routes. All 3 remain open follow-ups.
- [x] **ACTOR IDENTITY ENFORCEMENT + SIGN-IN GATE phase (2026-08-31)** — closes the STANDING SECURITY GAP above, before pilot testing with real Dubai venue managers. Worked as fresh subagent per route, each reviewed individually, then a mandatory final whole-branch review before merge, per standing pattern.
  - **`server/src/middleware/requireSession.ts`** gained `requireManager` — a small shared 403 gate (`req.user.systemRole === 'STAFF'` → 403) mirroring the STAFF-vs-everyone-else binary `voice/intentSchema.ts`'s `allowedIntentsFor` already used, applied here to the legacy REST routes voice.ts's own role check never covered.
  - **`swapRequests.ts`**: `GET`/`POST`/`PATCH` all session-gated. `POST /` (create) got a deliberate actor/target split, not a blanket self-only rule: a STAFF session's `requestedById` is always `req.user.id`, ownership-checked exactly like `voice.ts`'s `REQUEST_SWAP` (`shift.userId !== actorId` → 404); a MANAGER/OWNER session may still name a *different* employee via `requestedById` in the body — preserving `SchedulingRoute.tsx`'s existing "arrange cover for the employee selected in the Viewing dropdown" capability — but the `AuditLog` actor is always `req.user.id` regardless, with the note recording who it was filed on behalf of. Also added the `SWAP_REQUESTED` audit write this REST path was missing entirely (voice.ts's equivalent path already had it). `PATCH /:id` (decide) is MANAGER/OWNER-only, `reviewedById` never read from the body, location-scoped before `decideSwapRequest` runs (a check the REST route lacked entirely before).
  - **`join.ts`**: `/request-otp`/`/verify-otp` correctly left untouched (pre-auth by nature — the identity-establishing moment itself). `GET /:locationId/pending` and `PATCH /:requestId` are now MANAGER/OWNER-only, session-scoped to the caller's own venue, `reviewedById` never read from the body.
  - **`staffDirectory.ts`**: no actor field existed here before (unlike the other three) — the gap was `locationId` trusted from body/params with zero scoping and zero audit trail on any mutation. `POST`/`PATCH` now MANAGER/OWNER-only, `locationId` derived from session only, `PATCH`'s previously-nonexistent cross-location check now 404s indistinguishably from "doesn't exist." Two new `AuditAction` values added (`STAFF_CREATED`, `STAFF_UPDATED` — additive, same shallow pattern as `FloorSection.notes`) since none of the existing ones fit staff-directory CRUD.
  - **`floorPlan.ts`**: all 9 routes gated (2 GETs session-only, 7 mutations MANAGER/OWNER-only). Every `actorId`/`createdById` site now reads `req.user.id`. Closed two real pre-existing gaps the phase found along the way, not introduced by it: `PATCH`/`DELETE /sections/:id` had **zero** ownership check at all (any caller who knew a `sectionId` could edit or delete any venue's section), and `POST /assignments` checked its two referenced entities (`staffId`, `sectionId`) against *each other* but never against the caller's own session venue.
  - **`server/src/routes/locations.ts`** (built in the immediately-preceding onboarding-wizard phase, not one of the four originally named) had the identical vulnerability — zero auth at all — and was missed by every per-route review specifically *because* it wasn't one of the four named routes. Caught by the mandatory final whole-branch review, not a per-task one; fixed the same way (session + manager-only on `PATCH`).
  - **Schema/migration reconciliation.** `venueType` and the two new `AuditAction` values had been applied via `prisma db push` with no committed migration (a real drift from this project's actual migration history — 10 real migrations exist, contrary to an earlier assumption mid-phase that none did). `prisma migrate dev` was attempted to regenerate one and prompted an interactive **"reset the database, all data will be lost"** — not run. Fixed instead by hand-writing the migration SQL and running `prisma migrate resolve --applied`, reconciling history with zero data loss.
  - **Frontend**: every one of `src/api/{swapRequests,join,staffDirectory,floorPlan,locations}.ts` now takes a `token` and attaches `withAuth(token)` (the same pattern `availability.ts` established), threaded through `AppStateContext.tsx`, `StaffDirectory.tsx`, `PendingApprovals.tsx`, and `FloorPlan/{AssignmentBoard,FloorPlanTab,SectionEditor}.tsx`. Fixed one real bug this surfaced: `AppStateContext.tsx` never cleared `swapRequests`/`staffDirectory` state on logout — since no route required a session to render, signing out on the shared venue device this app targets left the previous person's staff directory (names/phones/preferred language) and swap-request history visible to whoever used the device next. Now cleared on `session → null`.
  - **Sign-in gate.** Since every one of the above routes now requires a session but nothing in the frontend router ever did, an unauthenticated visit to `/scheduling`, `/floor-plan`, or `/people` would previously have rendered a page that silently showed nothing. `src/router.tsx` gained a `RequireSession` wrapper, redirecting to `/join?mode=login` (a new `?mode=login` param on `JoinRoute.tsx`/`JoinFlow.tsx` that lands directly in login mode rather than the default join/self-registration mode). Originally applied to exactly those three routes plus `/schedule` and `/onboarding` left ungated on the reasoning that "neither touches the four hardened routes" — **that reasoning was wrong for `/onboarding` and was corrected in the very next phase's mandatory final review, not caught here.** `/onboarding`'s venue-setup step calls `/api/locations/:id`, which this same phase DID harden (see the `locations.ts` finding two paragraphs up) — the exclusion contradicted the phase's own work. `/onboarding` is now gated too. `/schedule` remains correctly excluded — it only touches `shifts.ts`, which stayed unauthenticated.
  - **Verified live, not just statically**: a real script exercised every route above against the real running server + real Supabase DB with two real minted sessions (one MANAGER, one STAFF) obtained through the real `/api/identity` OTP flow — every spoofing attempt (naming a different user's id in `requestedById`/`reviewedById`/`actorId`/`createdById`/`locationId`) confirmed ignored by independently re-querying `AuditLog` afterward, every STAFF attempt on a manager-only action confirmed 403, a manager from one venue targeting a different venue's floor-plan section confirmed 404. All test rows deleted and independently re-queried to zero. Static verification also clean throughout: `typecheck`, `server:typecheck`, `build`, `lint` (0 errors), `npm test` (23/23), `npm run test:server` (89/89, 2 skipped by design).
  - **Mandatory final whole-branch review** (`code-review`, high effort) caught 4 things no per-route review could have: the schema/migration drift above; the `locations.ts` gap above; the `AppStateContext.tsx` logout-doesn't-clear-state bug above; and (flagged, not fixed — a maintainability note, not a live bug) the `locationId !== req.user.locationId` check is now hand-copied at ~9 call sites across 5 files with slightly different 403-vs-404 shapes instead of a shared helper alongside `requireManager` — every instance individually verified correct, but a future new route in this family could easily be added without it, the way `locations.ts` just was.
  - **Confirmed, not assumed**: exactly one `Location` row exists in the entire database (`seed-location`) and all 23 real `User` rows are scoped to it (queried directly, twice, across two separate reports) — the hardcoded `'seed-location'` littered through this app's frontend is correct for this single-venue pilot as it stands. It stops being correct the moment a second real venue needs to exist; nothing before that.
- [x] **AI VOICE: CREATE_SHIFT/EDIT_SHIFT/ASSIGN_SECTION + confidence gate + VoiceInteractionLog (2026-09-09)** — extends the existing MARK_AVAILABILITY/REQUEST_SWAP/APPROVE-DECLINE_SWAP/APPROVE-DECLINE_JOIN voice pipeline with 3 new manager-only intents, following the exact same architecture (voice calls the same mutator the REST UI uses, never a parallel data path). Built via subagent-driven-development on branch `feat/voice-shift-section-tools` — 13 planned tasks, each with a fresh implementer + independent task review, plus a mandatory final whole-branch review + one bundled fix wave. Full spec/plan/ledger at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-09-ai-voice-shift-section-tools-design.md` / `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-09-ai-voice-shift-section-tools.md`.
  - **Mutator extraction** (`server/src/lib/actions/{shiftActions,sectionActions}.ts`, new, mirroring the pre-existing `swapActions.ts`/`joinActions.ts` split): `createShift`/`updateShift`/`upsertSectionAssignment` are pure Prisma writes with zero validation — validation stays in each caller (REST route, voice `/execute`), matching this codebase's established precedent. `routes/shifts.ts` and `routes/floorPlan.ts` now call these same functions instead of inlining the write; zero behavior change, proven by the pre-existing `shifts.test.ts`/`floorPlan.test.ts` passing unmodified throughout.
  - **`confidence` field** (0–1, self-reported by Gemini) added to every voice intent except `UNRECOGNIZED`, in both `intentSchema.ts` and its `src/api/voice.ts` mirror. `CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD = 0.6` in `parseIntent.ts`: a real intent below threshold gets coerced to a client-facing UNRECOGNIZED-shaped response, but the true attempted intent (with its real confidence) is preserved separately for logging via a `VoiceIntentResolution` (`{response, attempted}`) wrapper. A missing/non-numeric/out-of-range confidence fails CLOSED to `0`, never trusted as "certain."
  - **`VoiceInteractionLog`** (new table, additive-only migration): one row per `/parse-intent` call (`resolvedIntent` always the true model output, `outcome` one of `PENDING_CONFIRMATION`/`LOW_CONFIDENCE`/`UNRECOGNIZED`), updated by `/execute` to its final outcome (`EXECUTED`/`REJECTED_VALIDATION`/`REJECTED_PERMISSION`/`ERROR`). Round-tripped via a `voiceLogId` the client passes back on confirm — **found and fixed during Task 9's own review**: `updateInteractionOutcome` originally updated by `id` alone with no ownership check, letting any authenticated session overwrite another user's/location's audit-trail row; now scoped via `updateMany({ where: { id, actorId } })`.
  - **Two more real bugs caught and fixed inside Task 9's review loop** (the largest, highest-stakes task — a full `/execute` rewrite): `ASSIGN_SECTION` originally hardcoded `touchDutyLabel: true`, which would have silently wiped an existing duty label on every voice reassign with no spoken label (the exact failure mode `floorPlan.ts`'s REST route was written to prevent — a spoken "assign X to section Y" normalizes to `dutyLabel: null`, the common case); fixed to `touchDutyLabel: intent.dutyLabel !== null`. The rewrite's replacement code had also dropped the `/execute` handler's security-rationale comments (including the "THIS IS THE ONLY REAL SECURITY BOUNDARY..." docblock) — restored.
  - **Audio pipeline hardening**: push-to-talk cap dropped 45s→10s (`AppShell.tsx`); `getUserMedia` now requests `{ noiseSuppression, echoCancellation, autoGainControl }` instead of bare `audio: true`; `/transcribe` now fetches the venue's staff/section/role names and feeds them to Gemini as a vocabulary-bias hint (capped/deduped to 150 entries after the final review flagged the uncapped version as an unbounded-prompt-size risk for a large venue, and wrapped in its own try/catch so a DB blip degrades to unbiased transcription instead of a 500).
  - **Mandatory final whole-branch review** (opus) re-traced all 3 new intents end-to-end confirming they really do call the same extracted mutators the REST routes call (not a parallel implementation), re-verified both Task 9 security fixes directly in the final file state (not just trusting the commit messages), and caught 3 more things no single task's scoped review could see: a `voiceLogId: string | null` vs `string` type lie spanning the Task-8/Task-10 seam (server can return null on a log-write failure; both frontend types still declared it required); comment loss wider than Task 9's round caught (also hit `prompts.ts` and several more `/execute` branches); and a missing `EDIT_SHIFT` integration test (the plan's own Task 13 brief only planned 5 of the spec's 8 test cases — a plan gap, not an implementer one). All fixed in one bundled final fix wave + one scoped re-review, then closed out with a real live-Gemini smoke test (not simulated) confirming the confidence gate actually receives and correctly gates on a real `confidence` value in practice, not just in the schema definition.
  - **Verification, all clean**: `npm run server:typecheck`/`npm run typecheck` clean throughout every task; `voice.test.ts` grew from 19→25 tests (6 pre-existing intents' tests never modified, proving zero regression), full server suite serial run 159/0/2-skip; live Gemini smoke test (`MARK_AVAILABILITY`, confidence `0.95`, `outcome: PENDING_CONFIRMATION`) confirmed post-merge behavior for real, not assumed.
  - **Explicitly parked at the final review, not silently dropped** (see the SDD ledger for full rulings): unit-testability refactor for the confidence gate (export `normalizeParsedIntent`/`outcomeAtParseTime`, add synthetic tests not requiring a live Gemini call); cross-location 404 tests for the 3 new intents specifically (the underlying validation was independently verified as a faithful line-by-line mirror of the already-tested REST-route validation, so risk from deferring is low); `respond()` awaiting the log write instead of fire-and-forget (perf-only, `.catch()`'d so it can't fail the request); a `VoiceInteractionLog` read endpoint (no gap — never in scope, but the natural next slice, since the table is currently write-only); a "stopped at 10s" UX indicator when the recording cap fires mid-command.

Spec/plan/ledger at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-voice-query-my-schedule-design.md` / `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-voice-query-my-schedule.md`. Built via subagent-driven-development, 7 tasks, each independently reviewed, plus a mandatory final whole-branch review + one bundled fix wave.
  - **A broader "Full Tool Coverage" v2 brief was substantially corrected against actual repo state before any code was written**: `REQUEST_SWAP`/`APPROVE_SWAP`/`DECLINE_SWAP` were listed as "new" but had shipped before v1 even started; `TimeOffRequest` has a Prisma model but zero REST mutator anywhere (time-off voice tools dropped from the roadmap entirely, not just deferred, until that mutator exists as its own piece of work); `RotaPublish`/`RotaTemplate`'s apply-mutator both need the v1-style `lib/actions/` extraction first (sequenced as Slice 3, not built here). Only the smallest, lowest-risk piece — `QUERY_MY_SCHEDULE` — was actually specced and built this pass.
  - **Architecturally new pattern**: every intent before this one is a write, resolved by `/parse-intent` then committed by a separately-confirmed `/execute` call. `QUERY_MY_SCHEDULE` terminates entirely inside `/parse-intent` — no `/execute` call, no confirm step, since nothing is written. `summary` is repurposed as the literal spoken-style answer for this one intent instead of a description of a pending action. New `VoiceInteractionOutcome` value `ANSWERED` (additive migration) logged instead of the otherwise-default `PENDING_CONFIRMATION`. `/execute` deliberately got **zero new code** — a hand-crafted request for this intent still passes the role/permission checks (it's a real, permitted intent) but has no `case` in either `validateIntentShape` or the main switch, so it correctly falls to the pre-existing `default: 400 'Unknown intent.'` branch. `AppShell.tsx` needed **zero changes at all** — `handleVoiceCancel`/`handleVoiceConfirm` were already fully generic, and hiding the Confirm button in `VoiceCommandSheet.tsx` for this intent is what makes `executeVoiceIntent` structurally unreachable through the real UI (independently re-verified by the final review by reading `AppShell.tsx` directly, not trusting the plan's claim).
  - **The final whole-branch review caught a real gap no single task's scoped review could see**: the spec originally claimed the "never another person's schedule" guarantee was uniformly structural (the data simply isn't in context). True for STAFF callers — but for MANAGER/OWNER callers, `buildContext` already populates the full venue-wide shift list (`weekShifts`, everyone's name) in the same prompt for other intents (EDIT_SHIFT/ASSIGN_SECTION), so for that tier the guarantee was actually resting on the prompt instruction alone, not data absence — exactly the enforcement mechanism the spec said it wasn't relying on. Not a data leak (a manager already sees the full roster via the REST UI) but a real audit-trail-accuracy risk: a manager's question about a third party could misroute to this intent and log misleadingly as a legitimate self-query. Fixed in the final bundled fix wave: the spec was corrected to state the tier split honestly, the prompt instruction was strengthened to explicitly forbid answering from the venue-wide list for this intent (mandating UNRECOGNIZED for a named-third-party question instead), and a genuine MANAGER-tier test was added (`systemRole: 'MANAGER'`, asserting `weekShifts` really does co-populate) rather than only ever testing STAFF.
  - **Two more findings from the same final review, also fixed in the same wave**: `callerShifts`'s only rendering gate in the prompt-builder checked `allowed.includes('REQUEST_SWAP')` with no mention that `QUERY_MY_SCHEDULE` also depends on it — an invisible coupling that would have silently broken this intent (a wrong "you have no shifts" answer, not an error) if `REQUEST_SWAP` were ever removed from `STAFF_INTENTS` for unrelated reasons; fixed by widening the gate's `||` condition with a comment documenting the dependency. Two other prompt/schema instructions still described `summary` as "what will happen if confirmed" with no carve-out for this intent's repurposed meaning — fixed with a short parenthetical exception in both places.
  - **Verification**: two new pure-function/unit-level tests (`parseIntent.test.ts` proving `buildContext` never leaks one caller's shift into another's context, bidirectionally; `interactionLog.test.ts` covering all 4 branches of the outcome-classification logic including the ordering-sensitive case — a `QUERY_MY_SCHEDULE` attempt coerced to `UNRECOGNIZED` by the confidence gate must log `LOW_CONFIDENCE`, not `ANSWERED`) need no live Gemini call and always pass deterministically. 3 new integration tests appended to `voice.test.ts` (zero pre-existing tests modified — confirmed via diff stat, a pure append): 2 live-Gemini round trips (STAFF + MANAGER) and one proving `/execute`'s fail-closed 400. This session's shared Gemini free-tier key (20 requests/day) hit exhaustion partway through from concurrent multi-worktree usage across this plan and the still-open v1 plan sharing one `.env` — the resulting intermittent 503/429s were independently corroborated as external (not a code defect) by observing the same failures land on already-reviewed, untouched pre-existing tests in the same run.
  - **Explicitly parked, not silently dropped**: a one-line stale doc-comment (`PromptContext.callerShifts`'s "only relevant/populated for REQUEST_SWAP" note, now inaccurate after the coupling fix); a terminal `ANSWERED` log row can still be overwritten by replaying its own `voiceLogId` to `/execute` (self-inflicted only, no cross-user impact — `updateInteractionOutcome`'s `actorId` scoping still holds); the 2 live-Gemini tests use a bare early `return` instead of `t.skip(...)` when no API key is configured (matches this test file's existing convention throughout, not a regression); `callerShifts` is `take: 10` with no date upper-bound and the model isn't told the list is truncated (pre-existing shape built for `REQUEST_SWAP`, where 10 is plenty — this is the first intent where truncation could produce a confidently wrong answer like "am I working next month").
