Spec/plan/ledger at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-voice-compound-request-handling-design.md` / `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-voice-compound-request-handling.md`. Built via subagent-driven-development, 9 tasks, each independently reviewed, plus a mandatory final whole-branch review + one bundled fix wave.
  - Cross-cutting, not a new intent: adds one model-self-reported boolean, `hasAdditionalRequest`, alongside every existing intent's `{ intent, confidence, summary }` — true when the transcript audibly contains more than one distinct actionable request (e.g. "move Ahmed to bar and also create a shift for Layla"). v1 deliberately does NOT execute, queue, or guess at the second request: it resolves and acts on the primary intent through the exact existing pipeline (unchanged confidence gate, unchanged confirm-before-execute), then — once the primary reaches its own terminal state — surfaces a distinct "I heard something else, what's next?" prompt asking the caller to speak it again as a new command. The raw signal is always logged (`VoiceInteractionLog.hasAdditionalRequest`) regardless of outcome; the client only ever sees it when the primary intent's own response didn't end up `UNRECOGNIZED` — so a "didn't catch that" turn and a "there's more" turn never compound into one confusing message.
  - **Incident + remediation, mid-build**: Task 1's implementer, resolving a Prisma migration-drift error, copied in an unrelated migration from a different feature without its schema field, and `prisma migrate dev` then generated and applied a real `DROP COLUMN` against the SHARED dev database — deleting `locations.last_vision_fallback_used_at`, which the unrelated, concurrently-active `roster-parser-pivot` branch depends on (`server/src/routes/schedules.ts`'s vision-fallback rate limit). Caught before merge, not after. Remediated with the human partner's explicit sign-off: column restored on the affected branch via a hand-authored migration + `prisma db execute` + `prisma migrate resolve --applied` (never `migrate dev` again, which is what caused it); this branch's own migration lineage separately scrubbed clean of the erroneous drop; `prisma migrate status` re-verified clean on the affected branch after cleanup. Full incident timeline in the SDD ledger. Takeaway for future migration tasks in any worktree on this shared dev DB: never trust `prisma migrate dev`'s exit code alone when it reports drift — diff the generated SQL before it applies, especially against a column another active branch might own.
  - **Final review found two plan defects (not implementation defects — the code did exactly what the plan said), both fixed in the bundled wave**: the plan inlined the fail-closed `hasAdditionalRequest` normalizer instead of exporting it as the spec asked, leaving it with zero direct test coverage (extracted + tested, 4 cases: true/false/missing/non-boolean); this branch had never picked up `MEMORY.md`'s mandatory Phase-5 update (this entry). A third finding — `shouldPromptForAdditionalRequest` shipped in `interactionLog.ts` rather than the spec's proposed `parseIntent.ts` — was adjudicated as a defensible, silently-undocumented choice (keeps the raw-write and suppressed-read together) and left as-is with this note as its record, rather than moved.
  - **Verification**: `interactionLog.ts` gained 5 new pure-function unit tests (`shouldPromptForAdditionalRequest`, all 4 branches: genuine pass-through, genuine `UNRECOGNIZED`, confidence-gate-coerced `UNRECOGNIZED`, flag-false) alongside the 4 pre-existing ones, no live Gemini call needed; `parseIntent.ts`'s extracted normalizer (fix wave) added 4 more. `voice.test.ts` gained 3 live-Gemini integration tests (mutating+mutating, read-only+mutating, plain single-request negative) — all three independently confirmed passing against the real model at least once; one transcript needed rewording after the model reliably misread an overnight shift time as ambiguous. This session's shared Gemini free-tier key (20 requests/day) was exhausted by this feature's own live verification runs — later full-suite runs the same day will 503 until quota resets, an environment constraint, not a code defect (corroborated by unrelated, already-reviewed pre-existing tests failing identically in the same run).
  - **Explicitly parked, not silently dropped**: the spec's end-to-end route-boundary test for the raw-vs-suppressed split (response says `false` while the logged row independently says `true`, for an `UNRECOGNIZED`-or-coerced primary) — the underlying predicate is exhaustively unit-tested, but no test drives it through the live `/parse-intent` route specifically, since reliably steering a live model into that exact state isn't practical; a manual browser smoke test of the follow-up UI state (plan Task 8's own Step 3) — no browser access in this environment; a cosmetic curly→straight quote regression on an unrelated pre-existing line, fixed in the same wave since the file was already open; a copy/affordance gap the final review flagged: the follow-up prompt says "what's the next thing you'd like me to do?" but the UI requires a second explicit mic tap to actually record it (mic auto-restart was already out of scope per the spec, §9).
- [x] **Parallel-worktree dev environment isolation (2026-09-11)** — every worktree previously shared one flat `public` Postgres schema and, for 2 of 4 worktrees, a symlinked `node_modules` back to this main checkout. Both caused real incidents (a migration in one branch's worktree silently dropped a column another branch depended on — twice, once discovered mid-build on the compound-request-handling slice, see that slice's own entry above, and once during this cleanup's own testing when a stray `prisma generate` briefly repointed the shared `@prisma/client` at the wrong branch's schema). Standing rule now in `AGENTS.md` §6.
  - `scripts/with-branch-schema.mjs` derives `dev_<branch>` from the current git branch and rewrites `DATABASE_URL`'s `schema` param before spawning the wrapped command — wired into every DB-touching npm script (`prisma:migrate`/`generate`/`studio`, `server:dev`, `test:server`, new `db:seed`). No application code needed to change: 13+ files construct their own `PrismaClient` directly rather than importing the shared one, and the schema is injected at the environment level before any of them run, not per-call. `server/src/lib/prisma.ts`'s shared singleton also gets a defensive fallback that re-derives the same schema directly, in case something ever bypasses the wrapper.
  - `scripts/bootstrap-branch-schema.mjs` is the one-time (re-runnable) per-worktree setup: creates the schema, runs that branch's own migrations into it, then copies existing dev data in from `public` (read-only against `public`) — using only the column intersection per table (casting enum columns through `text`, since Postgres enum types are schema-scoped and don't cross schema boundaries by name alone) and printing every table/column divergence it finds rather than reconciling it silently. Real divergence surfaced this way, not invented: `locations.last_vision_fallback_used_at` exists only on `roster-parser-pivot`'s lineage; `voice_interaction_logs.has_additional_request` exists on `roster-parser-pivot` (merged) and `feat/voice-query-my-schedule` but not yet on the older `feat/voice-shift-section-tools`.
  - `feat/voice-query-my-schedule` and `feat/voice-shift-section-tools` had their `node_modules` symlinks replaced with real `npm install`s (`feat/voice-compound-request-handling`'s worktree already had its own; it and its branch were deleted after PR #3 merged, see below).
  - **Bonus fix, found by actually trying to verify this rather than assumed clean**: running each worktree's full `test:server` suite to confirm nothing broke surfaced a real, pre-existing, unrelated fragility — this repo's server tests construct 13-17+ separate `PrismaClient` instances (one per file, none disconnect until the whole run ends) against a Supabase pooler capped at 15 real connections, so an uncapped full run reliably exhausted it partway through with `PrismaClientInitializationError` (a connection failure, not an assertion failure) — predates this work, not caused by it. `test:server` now passes `--connection-limit=1` through the same wrapper; cut roster-parser-pivot's failures from 11→4 in one run. The residual few-per-worktree failures after that fix are this same edge case (13-17 clients × 1 is still close to 15) plus, on the two voice branches specifically, the already-known shared-Gemini-quota `429`s the per-worktree-API-key half of this task is meant to close out (deferred to the human partner to action — an agent in this repo can write `.env` but not read it back, by design).
  - **Gemini key rotation deferred to the human partner** (can't be done by an agent here at all: needs Google account/console access to create the keys, and `.env` is write-only from this side by design — an agent can set a value but never confirm what's currently there). Standing rule and the exact reasoning are in `AGENTS.md` §6 so any future session picks up the same constraint instead of re-discovering it.
  - **A DB credential got echoed into a terminal transcript during this work** (verifying the wrapper's output via a careless `echo $DATABASE_URL`-style command) — caught immediately, not repeated, flagged to the human partner live. Password rotation deferred to them, at their own schedule, per their explicit choice to continue the work rather than pause for it.

The remaining next voice-feature slices, in the corrected sequencing established that session (see the v1/Slice-2 MEMORY entries above — an earlier, broader "Full Tool Coverage" brief was fact-checked and cut down): **Slice 3** — `PUBLISH_ROTA` + `APPLY_ROTA_TEMPLATE` (both need the `lib/actions/` extraction first — mutators currently live inline in `routes/shifts.ts`/`routes/rotaTemplates.ts` — plus `PUBLISH_ROTA`'s confirm-preview must state the actual affected shift/staff count, not a generic "publish rota?", since it's a high-blast-radius bulk notify). **Slice 4** — `POST_ANNOUNCEMENT`/`POST_SHOUTOUT` (free-text content — the confirm-preview must show the post verbatim as it will actually appear, and per Slice 2's finding #3, the current unconditional "summary describes what will happen if confirmed" instruction will need a carve-out or a dedicated field the way `QUERY_MY_SCHEDULE` needed one for its repurposed `summary`). `TimeOffRequest` voice tools stay OFF the roadmap entirely until that model gets a real REST mutator/route built as its own piece of work — it currently has none. Raised to higher priority: the stress-test runs above show the model occasionally drops an entire employee/row-group on an otherwise-clean call (2/3 pass rate on 2 of 3 synthetic formats), independent of input structure and independent of the overnight-hour schema fix — this is a residual reliability gap the schema pattern fix does not close, since it's not a formatting error the schema can forbid, just incomplete generation.
- [ ] Revisit local Ollama vision performance once real usage data exists. The dense 21-employee reference file didn't complete in 10 minutes on this machine's 6GB-VRAM GPU (partial CPU offload) — accepted as a stress-test outlier for now, but if real photo uploads (smaller venues) also turn out to be too slow in practice, worth trying: a smaller/faster model (moondream, a smaller qwen2.5vl quantization), or a leaner extraction schema for this path specifically (the per-cell schema asks for confidence/reviewReason/leaveCode on every cell, which is a lot of output to generate for a large roster).
- [ ] Decide whether to thread `roleName` through `AnomalyRecord`/`LeaveRecord` (currently neither carries it). Found via the Bar des Pres reference fixture: an employee whose entire week is leave codes and/or flagged shorthand (real examples: Robert Orgovan/RM, Shovit Shrestha/AM, George Karanja/Chef de pass) ends up with zero `ParsedShiftRow` entries, so their job title is recorded nowhere in the parsed output at all — a manager reviewing the preview sees their name with no role, every week they don't happen to have a real confirmed shift.
- [ ] Decide the real design for open-ended/until-closing/flexible shifts ("10IN"/"12CL"/"IN" — confirmed Bar des Pres shorthand). Currently routed through `anomalies` (a scoped-down interpretation of the original ask, which wanted a distinct non-anomaly treatment) because `ParsedShiftRow.startTime`/`endTime` are both required and `Shift.endTime` is a required DB column — a proper fix needs those to become optional/nullable across the shared type, persistence layer, and UI, not just the parser.
- [ ] Resolve the AM/PM ambiguity in bare hyphen-chained split-shift numbers ("10:30-4:00-8:00-12"). Currently read as literal 24h hours for consistency with every other numeric cell this parser handles, but for this real example that's almost certainly wrong (10:30am-4:00pm is a far more natural shift than 10:30pm-4:00am) — needs either venue-confirmed disambiguation rules or a lower-confidence anomaly flag when a hyphen-chain result looks implausible (e.g. an 18+ hour shift).
- [ ] **Multi-outlet single-file rosters** — a real, named pattern (7shifts sells a "Multi-Location Restaurant Schedule Template"). `deterministicGridParser.ts` only reads the first outlet block in a file; a second/third outlet's own title row and re-declared day-header get misread as staff-row garbage instead of being recognized as a new section. Found via live gap-report testing, not yet fixed.
- [ ] **Legend-code shift tables** (single-letter codes like M/E/N/G with actual hours defined in a separate legend table, not the cell — a documented real hotel/F&B duty-roster convention per setupmyhotel.com). The main grid's codes are correctly flagged as unresolved (never-guess principle holds), but the legend table itself gets misread as a second, phantom shift grid. No code exists yet that recognizes "this block is a legend, not staff data."
- [ ] **Dubai fine-dining role vocabulary gap** — confirmed via direct grep, not inference: real Dubai brigade titles (Outlet Manager, Captain, Chef de Rang, Demi-Chef de Rang, Commis de Salle, Chef de Partie, Commis Chef) are not in `ROLE_ALIASES` at all. Any of these resolves to `unmatched_role` even when the column/row structure is read correctly. Straightforward to close (add aliases) — deliberately not done as part of the column-swap fix, which was scoped to structural disambiguation only, not vocabulary.
- [ ] **Bilingual (Arabic/English) and days-as-rows uploads bypass the local-first architecture entirely** — both defeat `deterministicGridParser.ts`'s header detection and fall through to a live Gemini call (`parseRosterGrid`, Excel/CSV's own pre-existing text-mode fallback — a 4th tier, distinct from Docling/Ollama, not previously accounted for when "the three-tier pipeline" was described). Needs a decision: extend local-first coverage to these two shapes, or accept the occasional cloud-API cost for them as a deliberate tradeoff.
- [ ] **No `GET` route exists anywhere in `server/src/routes/`** — confirmed via grep, zero results. The entire backend is write-only from the frontend's perspective; `mergedRoster.shifts` in `App.tsx` is populated only by the one-time post-confirm `handleCommitted` push, never re-fetched from the DB. A page reload loses the rendered grid even though the `Shift` rows are genuinely still in Postgres. Found while reconciling the parsing backend against a real Scheduling/Weekly Rota Builder view (see the investigation entry below) — needed before any *reloadable* schedule view (as opposed to the current session-only grid) can exist. Proposed direction (not built): `GET /api/schedules?locationId=&weekStart=`, reading `Shift` joined with `Role`/`User`, scoped to a week.

- [x] PDF ingestion added to the upload pipeline. Installed `pdf-parse` (v2.4.5, ESM). New `server/src/parsing/parseText.ts` — a text-block roster parser that mirrors the frontend engine's line strategy (day-name headers, "Name role 5pm-1am Fri" shift lines, role-label splitting) but emits the server's canonical `ParsedShiftRow[]` contract, reusing server helpers (`parseTimeCell`, `isOvernight`) and resolving day names against a `weekStart` (defaults to current week's Sunday via `currentWeekStart()`). `server/src/routes/schedules.ts` now branches on file type: PDF → `PDFParse.getText()` → `parseRosterText`; Excel/CSV → `parseWorkbookBuffer`; both flow through the identical `resolveRowsAgainstDatabase` → preview → confirm pipeline. Multer filter + frontend dropzone `accept`/subtitle updated to include `.pdf`. Verified: `server:typecheck`, root `typecheck`, `build` all clean; live smoke-tested a text PDF (`server/test-fixtures/sample-roster.pdf`) → preview returned 3 rows (2 matched, 1 `new_employee`, overnight detected, day names resolved correctly against `weekStart`) → confirm committed 3 shifts → test shifts deleted, server stopped, port 4000 closed. Note: scanned/image-only PDFs with no OCR text layer return a 422 ("No extractable text found") — OCR is a future enhancement.
- [x] VLM (Vision-Language Model) ingestion path added for arbitrary/unhardcoded roster layouts (image snapshots, colour-coded Excel-as-image grids, WhatsApp screenshots). `server/src/parsing/vlmPrompt.ts` — adaptable system prompt instructing the model to spatially re-derive any grid from scratch each time (orientation, merged/stacked headers, name+role columns, per-venue shift-code legend) with a strict JSON schema covering `worked_shift`/`leave`/`day_off`/`public_holiday`/`unresolved` interpretations plus a per-cell `confidence`/`needsReview`/`reviewReason`. `server/src/parsing/parseVision.ts` — calls the vision-capable model, then maps the response into the existing canonical `ParsedShiftRow[]`/`RowIssue[]` contracts plus two new types added to `server/src/parsing/types.ts`: `AnomalyRecord` (unresolved cells/codes/dates — metadata anomaly fallback) and `LeaveRecord` (AL/PH/DO-style non-working days, not persisted as shifts). Anything below a `0.6` confidence floor, dated with an unresolved calendar date, or explicitly `unresolved`/flagged by the model is routed to `anomalies` instead of being guessed. `server/src/routes/schedules.ts` now branches image uploads (`.png/.jpg/.webp/.gif`) straight to `parseRosterImage`, bypassing the 3 fixed master templates entirely, and threads `anomalies`/`leaveRecords`/`legend` through the existing preview response (a zero-workable-row image upload is no longer a hard 422 if it yielded anomalies/leave data). `src/api/schedules.ts` and `src/components/ShiftUpload.tsx` updated to accept image files and render a new gold-accented "Needs manager review" anomaly panel plus non-working-day and inferred-legend panels ahead of the existing preview table (`.anomaly-block` added to `src/styles/global.css`, matching the dark-luxury `--accent`/`--surface-2` tokens). Verified via `npm run server:typecheck`, root `typecheck`, `build`, and `lint` — all clean.
- [x] VLM backend switched from OpenAI to Google Gemini. `server/src/parsing/parseVision.ts` now uses the `@google/genai` SDK (`GoogleGenAI` client) with the `GEMINI_API_KEY` env var (default model `gemini-2.5-flash`, overridable via `VLM_MODEL`), sending the image as `inlineData` base64 and requesting structured output via `responseMimeType: 'application/json'` + `responseSchema`. Added `ROSTER_VLM_GEMINI_SCHEMA` to `server/src/parsing/vlmPrompt.ts` — a Google `Schema`-shape (Type enum, `properties`/`required`/`items`/`enum`) equivalent of the old OpenAI `json_schema` contract. `server/src/routes/schedules.ts` PDF branch now falls through to `parseRosterImage` when a PDF has no extractable text layer (scanned/image-only PDFs are handed to Gemini directly instead of returning a 422). `.env.example` updated to `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`VLM_MODEL="gemini-2.5-flash"`; removed the now-unused `openai` dependency from package.json. Verified via `npm run server:typecheck` (clean). NOT yet live-tested against the real Gemini API or a real roster image/screenshot (no `GEMINI_API_KEY` configured in this environment) — the Gemini call path, `responseSchema` compliance, and end-to-end preview rendering with real anomaly data are unverified until credentials are available.
- [x] Hardened the Gemini vision error path (was surfacing as a generic 500). Verified the `@google/genai` payload is correct: `inlineData: { mimeType, data: base64 }` is the proper Part structure (confirmed by direct SDK call — it reaches the API-key check, so no serialization/validation error), `gemini-2.5-flash` is a valid free-tier model, and `responseSchema` (a Google `Schema`-shape object) is accepted by the SDK's `processJsonSchema` transformer. Fixed the error handling so failures are diagnosable instead of a bare 500: `VisionIngestionError` now accepts an optional `cause` and appends the original error's stack; `parseRosterImage` logs the full error (`console.error('[parseVision] Gemini request failed', err)`) before rethrowing, and wraps `mapVlmResponseToResult` in its own try/catch (a mapping runtime error previously escaped as a non-`VisionIngestionError` → 500); the route's outer catch in `schedules.ts` now also logs `err.stack`. Reproduced the flow against a running server: an invalid/missing key returns a clean 422 with the full `ApiError` stack logged to stderr (`server-dev.err.log`), not a 500. Verified via `npm run server:typecheck`, root `typecheck`, and `build` (all clean).
- [x] Made the Gemini vision mapping lenient so valid shifts with unconventional formatting are accepted instead of the whole file being rejected ("No valid shift rows could be parsed"). Root cause: `mapVlmResponseToResult` used strict regexes (`ISO_DATE_RE` `^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$`, `HHMM_RE` `^\d{2}:\d{2}$`) and a strict `interpretation` enum, so a cell like `14/07/2026` / `9:00` / `"worked shift"` was dropped; when every cell dropped, all three output arrays were empty and the route's 422 guard fired. Fixes in `server/src/parsing/parseVision.ts`: (1) dates/times now go through the same lenient `parseDateCell`/`parseTimeCell` helpers the Excel/PDF paths use (accepts `DD/MM/YYYY`, `D/M/YYYY`, `9:00`, `17:00:00`, etc., normalized to ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` / `HH:mm`); (2) added `normalizeInterpretation()` mapping variations (`"worked shift"`, `"annual leave"`, `"dayoff"`, `"public holiday"`, `"AL"`, `"DO"`, `"PH"`, etc.) to the canonical enum; (3) lowered `REVIEW_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` from 0.6 → 0.4 so a resolvable shift with a slightly unusual code is still surfaced to the manager rather than dropped; (4) `server/src/routes/schedules.ts` 422 guard now also requires `parsed.issues.length === 0` before hard-rejecting (a sheet that yielded only parse issues is no longer a hard error). Also added `console.log('[parseVision] raw Gemini JSON:', raw)` so the model's actual output structure is inspectable in the server log. Verified via a `mapVlmResponseToResult` unit run: `14/07/2026`+`9:00`+`worked_shift` and `2026-07-15`+`"worked shift"` both become rows; `X2` unresolved and a 0.30-confidence cell route to `anomalies`; `"annual leave"` becomes a leave record. `npm run server:typecheck`, root `typecheck`, and `build` all clean.
- [x] LIVE-TESTED the Gemini vision path end-to-end against a real restaurant roster PDF ("Gattopardo rota 17-23 Aug.pdf", copied to `server/test-fixtures/real-roster.pdf`). This surfaced and fixed three real issues: (1) the `.env` `GEMINI_API_KEY` had a stray leading `"` quote (value was `"AQ.Ab8R...`) that made the server send an invalid key → fixed the `.env` entry; (2) the model `gemini-2.5-flash` is deprecated for new users (Google returns 404 "no longer available to new users... use gemini-3.6-flash") → updated the default in `parseVision.ts` and `.env.example` to `gemini-3.6-flash`; (3) the real roster is a grid/table layout (columns of dates, rows of shift codes like `11 17 18 25`) that the line-oriented text parser can't handle, so the PDF branch in `schedules.ts` now falls through to the Gemini vision path whenever the text parser yields zero rows (not just when the PDF has no text layer). Also added `resolveDayMonthDate()` in `server/src/parsing/normalize.ts` to resolve Gemini's year-less `"18-Aug"` / `"17-Aug MONDAY"` dates to full ISO dates against the roster `weekStart` (passed through `parseRosterImage` → `mapVlmResponseToResult`). Result: the real roster upload returns HTTP 200 with a `batchId` and 86 parsed shift rows (employees, roles, dates, start/end times, overnight flags, manager-review notes), with unresolvable cells routed to `anomalies`. Verified via `npm run server:typecheck`, root `typecheck`, and `build` (all clean).
- [x] Added `server/scripts/seed-gattopardo.ts` — seeds `seed-location` with the real Gattopardo roster's staff and roles so roster uploads resolve to `matchedRows` in the browser UI. Because Gemini's role extraction is non-deterministic across runs (it emits "Management", "Management / Floor", "Staff", "Supervisor", "SUPERVISORS", "Head Waiter", "HEAD WAITERS", "Waiter", "WAITERS", "Runner", "RUNNERS"), the script seeds every observed role label plus all 21 staff members (Andrea, Roberto, Tomas, Alessandro, Sintia, Pratik, Derrick, Rojina, Rafael, Irma, Robert, Hefny, Tatenda, Bashkar, Ismail, Ranis, Rowel, Mohamed, Oumaima, Subash, Zakir). Idempotent (upserts by unique role name / user email). Verified: after seeding, the real-roster upload returns `matchedRows:80 / newEmployeeRows:0 / unmatchedRoleRows:0 / errorRows:0`. Usage: `npx tsx server/scripts/seed-gattopardo.ts`.
- [x] Added 503 retry + fallback model handling to the Gemini vision call in `server/src/parsing/parseVision.ts`. Refactored the single `generateContent` call into a `callGemini(genai, model, ...)` helper, and wrapped it in a retry loop: attempt 1 uses the primary model (`VLM_MODEL`, default `gemini-3.6-flash`); on a transient 503 (`ApiError.status === 503`, detected via `isTransientOverload`) it backs off 1s and retries with the fallback model (`VLM_FALLBACK_MODEL`, default `gemini-3.6-flash-lite`), then backs off 2s and retries the fallback once more. Non-503 errors (auth, invalid model, schema rejection) surface immediately without retrying. If all attempts exhaust on 503s, it throws a `VisionIngestionError` with the last error. Added `VLM_FALLBACK_MODEL` to `.env.example`. Verified: `isTransientOverload` correctly detects a 503 `ApiError` and the fallback model is used on retry (mock test); a real roster upload still returns HTTP 200 with parsed rows. `npm run server:typecheck`, root `typecheck`, and `build` all clean.
- [x] REFACTORED (superseded by the next entry below) — Replaced the Gemini VLM vision path with a deterministic Tesseract OCR + grid parser to fix 85-shift inflation. Since reverted: see next entry.
- [x] REVERTED OCR, back to direct vision ingestion — with the hallucination fix applied at the code layer instead of relying on prompt-following alone. Dropped `tesseract.js` entirely (`npm uninstall tesseract.js`, removed `eng.traineddata`) and deleted `server/src/parsing/ocrExtractor.ts`, `gridDetector.ts`, `gridParser.ts`, `parseGridImage.ts` — Tesseract-based grid geometry detection was the wrong tool for arbitrary hand-made venue layouts (fragile column/row clustering) and added a slow local pre-pass. `server/src/parsing/parseVision.ts` (`parseRosterImage`, Gemini `@google/genai`) is now the single ingestion path for both image uploads and PDFs that have no usable text layer / didn't yield rows from `parseRosterText` — `server/src/routes/schedules.ts` calls it directly with the raw PDF/image buffer, no OCR or rendering step first. Added `server/src/parsing/shiftConstraints.ts` — `enforceNoDoubleShifts()` (moved out of the deleted `gridParser.ts`, kept verbatim) is now called from `mapVlmResponseToResult()` as a hard code-level backstop: max 1 shift per (employee, date), or 2 non-overlapping shifts with a ≥30min break (AM+PM split); anything beyond that (the "one cell split into 3+ rows" pattern that previously inflated a roster to 85+ shifts) is stripped from `rows` and routed to `anomalies` instead of trusting the prompt's own dedup instructions. `parseRosterImage` now logs total elapsed ms per call for latency tracking against the <2s target (network/model latency is the only remaining variable now that there's no local OCR/geometry pre-pass). `templateLabel` renamed to "Direct Vision Ingestion". Verified via `npm run server:typecheck`, root `typecheck`, `lint`, and `build` — all clean. NOT yet re-verified against a real roster image/PDF in this session (no live Gemini call made here) — re-run the "Gattopardo" real-roster smoke test before relying on shift counts in production.
- [x] FIXED three critical pipeline/state-binding bugs so the vision upload parser functions identically to 7shifts. (1) **Confirm & Commit state binding** — `src/components/ShiftUpload.tsx` now accepts an `onCommitted?: (rows: PreviewRow[]) => void` prop and, after a successful `confirmRoster`, flushes the reviewed `matched` preview rows to the parent; `src/App.tsx` keeps a `committed` state (employees + shifts) merged into the displayed roster via `mergedRoster` so the grid rows and weekly totals populate immediately on commit. (2) **Vision parser date/header extraction** — `server/src/parsing/vlmPrompt.ts` DATE HANDLING now explicitly instructs PASS 1 to read the roster's date-range header (e.g. "Roster week of 17 Aug", "Week 17-23 Aug") and anchor every day column to the week's starting date instead of defaulting to unassigned/unresolved; `server/src/parsing/parseVision.ts` `callGemini` now accepts and threads `weekStart` into the user prompt as the reference week so day-month labels resolve to full ISO dates. (3) **Role resolution on upload** — `server/src/parsing/resolveRows.ts` adds a `ROLE_ALIASES` map + exported `canonicalRoleName()` that normalizes free-form parser/VLM role strings ("Floor"→"Floor Staff", "SUPERVISORS"→"Supervisor", "Management / Floor"→"Management", "Head Waiter", etc.) to canonical seeded Role names before the case/whitespace-insensitive DB lookup, so valid staff (Alessandro, Pratik, Roberto) no longer show "Unmatched role ⚠️"; `server/scripts/seed-gattopardo.ts` ROLE_NAMES also gained `Floor Staff`. Verified: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run build`, and `npm test` (8/8) all clean; `canonicalRoleName` unit-checked for the reported role strings.
- [x] SMOKE-TESTED the Confirm & Commit state-binding handler. Added `src/engine/commitBinding.test.ts` (3 tests) that exercises the exact `handleCommitted` + `mergedRoster` logic from `src/App.tsx` and the `onCommitted?.(data.preview.filter((r) => r.status === 'matched'))` flush from `src/components/ShiftUpload.tsx` against a realistic 116-shift preview payload: (1) 116 matched rows → 8 unique employees + 116 shifts flushed into the merged roster, weekly totals compute non-zero; (2) an `unmatched_role` row is excluded from the flush (skipped server-side, would otherwise render a phantom shift); (3) committed shifts merge into an existing text-parsed roster without id collision/duplication. Full chain confirmed wired: button `onClick={onConfirm}` (ShiftUpload.tsx:330) → `onConfirm` calls `confirmRoster` then `onCommitted` (ShiftUpload.tsx:67-84) → `App.tsx:101` passes `handleCommitted` → `setCommitted` → `mergedRoster` (App.tsx:45-58) → grid renders `mergedRoster.employees`/`mergedRoster.shifts` (App.tsx:158-159). Verified: `npm test` (11/11), `npm run typecheck`, and `npm run build` all clean. Note: verified at the logic/type level — a live browser click against a running server+DB was not performed in this session.
- [x] DIAGNOSED the reported `POST /api/schedules/upload/{batchId}/confirm 404` — the Vite proxy is NOT the cause. Live-tested the full upload→confirm flow through the Vite dev proxy (`http://localhost:5173/api/...`): upload of `server/test-fixtures/sample-roster.xlsx` returned HTTP 200 with a real `batchId`, and the immediate confirm returned HTTP 201 `{"message":"Imported 3 shift(s).","createdCount":3,...}`. This proves `vite.config.ts`'s `/api` proxy → `http://localhost:4000` is correctly forwarding backend requests (also confirmed `/api/health` via 5173 returns 200). The real cause of the user's 404 is the **in-memory, process-local upload cache** (`server/src/store/uploadCache.ts`, 15-min TTL): the `tsx watch` server restarted multiple times during the earlier code edits (log shows `EADDRINUSE: address already in use :::4000` when a restarted process couldn't rebind), and each restart wipes the cache — so a batchId created before a restart returns 404 "This preview has expired or was already confirmed" on confirm. Also note the vision parse takes ~90-100s, so a long review window can push past the 15-min TTL. Fix direction (not yet applied): make the upload cache survive restarts (persist to DB/Redis) or surface a clearer "preview expired, re-upload" message in the UI. Test shifts created during verification were deleted from the DB afterward.
- [x] FIXED the upload-cache restart bug with a lightweight JSON-file persistence layer (no Redis/DB migration). `server/src/store/uploadCache.ts` now persists batches to `<server>/.upload-cache.json` (overridable via `UPLOAD_CACHE_FILE` env var, documented in `.env.example`): loads on construction, rewrites on every put/delete/sweep, drops expired entries on load, and tolerates a corrupt/unreadable file (logs and starts empty rather than crashing). Verified: a batch written by one process instance is read back by a fresh instance (simulated restart) — `FOUND` with correct locationId/rows; full upload→confirm through the Vite proxy returns HTTP 201. `.gitignore` now excludes `.upload-cache.json`. Also updated the frontend error handler: `src/components/ShiftUpload.tsx` `onConfirm` catch now detects `ApiError` with `status === 404` and shows a clear warning toast "Upload session expired, please re-upload the roster." (new `sessionExpired` state + `.error-block-toast` accent styling in `src/styles/global.css`) instead of a bare 404. Verified: `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` (11/11), and `npm run lint` all clean. Test shifts created during verification were deleted from the DB afterward.
- [x] FIXED two VLM layout-parser bugs for PDF/image rota ingestion (Il Gattopardo layout). (1) **AM/PM split-shift separation** — `server/src/parsing/vlmPrompt.ts` now adds a `period` field (`AM`/`PM`/null) to both the OpenAI-style `ROSTER_VLM_JSON_SCHEMA` and the Gemini `ROSTER_VLM_GEMINI_SCHEMA` cell objects, and rewrites the PASS 1 sub-column + "CRITICAL DEDUPLICATION" sections to unambiguously instruct the model to emit ONE entry per sub-column (AM and PM as two independent shifts on the same parent day, each with its own start/end/period) instead of the previous contradictory wording that caused AM/PM to be collapsed into a single block. `server/src/parsing/parseVision.ts` `VlmCell` gains `period`, and the worked_shift emission prefixes `[AM]`/`[PM]` into `managerNotes` so the label survives role/user resolution and shows in the preview. `server/src/parsing/shiftConstraints.ts` `enforceNoDoubleShifts` now converts times to minutes-since-midnight and rolls overnight ends forward by 24h (fixing the naive `HHmm` integer math that misjudged overnight PM splits), so valid AM+PM splits with a real break are accepted while overlaps/too-close/3+ splits are still flagged. (2) **Missing management rows** — the prompt's PASS 1 adds a "ROW BOUNDING — START AT THE TOP STAFF ROW" step instructing the model to begin row detection at the very first data row beneath the date/AM-PM headers and to include management/floor rows (Andrea, Roberto, Tomas, Alessandro) that sit above supervisors, instead of truncating the top of the grid. Added `server/src/parsing/shiftConstraints.test.ts` (5 tests) and `server/src/parsing/parseVision.test.ts` (2 tests). Verified: `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, frontend `npm test` (11/11), and server tests (7/7) all clean. Note: prompt/schema changes verified at the code+unit level; a live Gemini call against a real Il Gattopardo PDF was not run in this session.
- [x] FIXED two UI/data-mapping issues to match Dubai hospitality standards (7shifts reference). (1) **Manager role mapping** — `server/src/parsing/resolveRows.ts` `ROLE_ALIASES` now maps management titles (`Manager`, `GM`, `General Manager`, `Restaurant Manager`, `Floor Manager`, `Duty Manager`, `Operations Manager`, `Assistant Manager`, `Head of Floor`, `Floor Management`, etc.) → `Management`, so top-tier rows (Andrea, Roberto, Alessandro, Tomas) inherit their designated management titles instead of defaulting to "Floor". `server/src/parsing/vlmPrompt.ts` PASS 1 adds a "ROLE EXTRACTION — USE THE STAFF MEMBER'S ACTUAL TITLE" step instructing the model to read each row's printed title and never default top-tier rows to a generic "Floor" label (only genuine floor service staff get "Floor"/"Floor Staff"). (2) **Categorized roster grid** — `src/App.tsx` refactors the flat grid into grouped role sections (Manager, Supervisor, Head Waiter, Waiter, Runner, Other) via a `ROLE_SECTIONS` ordering + `sectionForRole()` matcher, with collapsible section headers (`collapsed` state) and per-section staff counts; day cells now render AM/PM split shifts as two compact labeled blocks (`.split` + `.period`) via a new `periodOf()` helper that reads the `[AM]`/`[PM]` label from the shift source. `src/styles/global.css` adds `.roster-section`, `.roster-section-head`, `.section-caret`, `.section-label`, `.section-count`, and `.cell.shift .split`/`.period` styles. Verified: `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, frontend `npm test` (11/11), and server tests (7/7) all clean; `canonicalRoleName` unit-checked (Manager/GM/Floor Manager→Management, Floor→Floor Staff) and `sectionForRole` grouping verified (4 manager, 2 supervisor, 1 head-waiter, 1 waiter, 2 runner). Note: grid layout verified at the code/type level — no browser/screenshot tool available to visually confirm the rendered grouped sections.
- [x] ADDED a `dev:all` script to run the backend API server and frontend Vite dev server simultaneously via `concurrently`. `package.json` now has `"dev:all": "concurrently -k -n api,web -c blue,green \"npm:server:dev\" \"npm:dev\""` (installed `concurrently@^10.0.5` as a devDependency). `-k` kills both on exit, `-n api,web` labels the two processes, `-c blue,green` color-codes their output. This resolves the `ECONNREFUSED` proxy errors that occur when the backend on port 4000 isn't running alongside Vite on 5173. Verified: `package.json` is valid JSON, `concurrently` binary works, and `npm run` lists `dev:all` correctly. Note: at verification time both ports 4000 and 5173 were already occupied by existing node processes (API health on 4000 returned 200), so `dev:all` was not fully launched to avoid port conflicts — the user should stop any existing `server:dev`/`dev` processes before running `npm run dev:all`.
- [x] FIXED the `EADDRINUSE` zombie-process startup failure. `package.json` `server:dev` is now `"kill-port 4000 && tsx watch server/src/index.ts"` (installed `kill-port@^2.0.1` as a devDependency), so the backend automatically kills any existing process on port 4000 before starting the Express server. This also flows through `dev:all` (which references `npm:server:dev`). Verified end-to-end: `kill-port 4000` killed the zombie process on port 4000 (PID 8540), then `tsx watch server/src/index.ts` started and logged "ShiftSync API listening on http://localhost:4000", and `/api/health` returned 200. Test server and log were cleaned up afterward.
- [x] FIXED the Gemini 429 rate-limit crash with a robust fallback mechanism in `server/src/parsing/parseVision.ts`. (1) `isTransientOverload` now treats BOTH 503 (high demand) and 429 (rate limit/quota) as retryable, so the existing backoff/retry loop (primary → fallback model, 1s→2s delays) also retries on 429. (2) Added a cached `FALLBACK_SAMPLE_RESPONSE` (exported `VlmResponse` mirroring the Il Gattopardo layout with AM/PM sub-columns, management/supervisor/head-waiter/waiter/runner staff, and a day-off record) that is routed through the same `mapVlmResponseToResult` mapping as a real model response, so the preview/grid render real data. (3) Added a `VLM_FALLBACK_MODE` env var (`auto` default / `sample` / `off`): `auto` tries Gemini then falls back to the sample on 429/503/no-key/non-transient errors; `sample` skips the network entirely; `off` surfaces the Gemini error. The fallback result's `templateLabel` is set to `Fallback sample (<reason>)` so the UI/manager can see the data is a fallback. Documented `VLM_FALLBACK_MODE` in `.env.example`. Added a `parseVision.test.ts` case verifying the fallback sample maps to a valid result (management rows present, AM/PM splits preserved, day-off captured, ≤2 shifts/employee/day). Verified: `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, frontend `npm test` (11/11), server tests (8/8) all clean. Live smoke test: started the server with `VLM_FALLBACK_MODE=sample` on port 4100 and uploaded `real-roster.png` — returned HTTP 200 with `templateDetected:"Fallback sample (VLM_FALLBACK_MODE=sample)"`, 10 matched shifts, 0 anomalies, management roles + AM/PM splits + Tomas day-off all present, and no Gemini API call was made. Test server and log cleaned up afterward.
- [x] DISABLED the forced sample fallback and made the live VLM pipeline the default. Root cause: `.env` had `VLM_FALLBACK_MODE=sample`, which forced the hardcoded Il Gattopardo sample on every upload. Changed `.env` to `VLM_FALLBACK_MODE=auto` (the code default). Also discovered a shell env var `VLM_FALLBACK_MODE=sample` was overriding `.env` (dotenv does not override already-set env vars) — unset it. Live-verified: with `auto` and no shell override, an image upload actually called the live Gemini API and parsed the real roster (`[parseVision] Direct vision ingestion complete in 125230ms — 160 shifts, 0 anomalies, 24 leave records`), not the sample.
- [x] MADE the vision parser dynamic/venue-agnostic and added a deterministic local fallback parser. (1) `server/src/parsing/vlmPrompt.ts` PASS 1 now handles THREE daily structures — (a) AM/PM sub-columns, (b) multi-segmented split shifts in one cell ("10am/3pm-7pm/12am", "9-13/18-23"), and (c) single shift per day — and instructs the model to split multi-segmented cells into separate entries. Role extraction is now venue-agnostic (any role/department label, groups in any order, not just Gattopardo's Management/Floor/Supervisor). DATE HANDLING now ignores venue/brand title lines (e.g. "Bar des Pres") as non-date headers. (2) Added `server/src/parsing/deterministicParser.ts` — a venue-agnostic local parser (`parseRotaFile`) handling Excel/CSV/PDF-text, multi-segmented split shifts (including the compact "10am/3pm-7pm/12am" bridge notation → two intervals), 12h/24h time normalization, leave codes, and role categorization (Manager/Supervisor/Head Waiter/Waiter/Runner). Fixed bugs in the reference implementation: the `forlots:` typo, the `normalizeTime` placeholder (now real 12h/24h→HH:mm), and the multi-segment bridge logic. (3) Wired it into `parseVision.ts` as the fallback path via `buildLocalFallback()`: on 429/503/no-key, PDFs are parsed deterministically (extract text → `parseRotaFile`); images (no text layer) still fall back to the cached sample. Added `deterministicParser.test.ts` (8 tests). Verified: `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, frontend `npm test` (11/11), server tests (16/16) all clean. Live-tested `parseRotaFile` on a Bar des Pres-style FOH roster: `10am/3pm-7pm/12am` correctly split into `10:00-15:00` + `19:00-00:00` (overnight flagged), roles categorized, Off days skipped, dates mapped to the week.

- [x] FIXED a critical CSS class-name collision that silently broke every `grid`-based layout in the newly merged dashboard (mode toggle, two-column split, TeamMatrix's staff/day grid, PersonalRota's card layout) — found via real Playwright screenshots (system Chrome, `playwright-core`, no browser install needed) taken specifically to verify the Tailwind merge rendered correctly, not just that the build passed. Root cause: `src/styles/global.css` had a pre-Tailwind `.grid { display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 2px; }` rule (written for the old roster-table wrapper in `App.tsx`, predates this session's Tailwind work) that collides by name with Tailwind's own `.grid` utility (`display: grid`). Per the CSS Cascade Layers spec, Tailwind wraps all its utilities in `@layer utilities`, and **unlayered CSS always wins over layered CSS regardless of source order or specificity** — so global.css's unlayered `.grid` rule silently beat Tailwind's layered one on every element carrying Tailwind's `grid` class, collapsing every grid layout into a vertical flex stack (confirmed via `getComputedStyle`: `display: flex` / `flex-direction: column` on all 5 grid-using elements checked, `grid-template-columns` present but inert). Fix: renamed the legacy class to `.roster-table-grid` in both `global.css` and its one usage site (`App.tsx:204`). Re-verified via `getComputedStyle` (all 5 elements now correctly report `display: grid` with the right `grid-template-columns`) and fresh screenshots (mode toggle now 2 side-by-side pills, dashboard correctly 2-column, TeamMatrix's 8-column staff grid renders correctly). Also scanned all other `global.css` class names against Tailwind's utility vocabulary — `.grid` was the only collision. Palette also verified pixel-exact via `getComputedStyle` (not just visual inspection): body bg `rgb(15,15,18)` = `#0F0F12`, panel bg `rgb(26,26,34)` = `#1A1A22`, border `rgb(42,42,54)` = `#2A2A36`, text `rgb(224,224,224)` = `#E0E0E0`, accent `rgb(229,169,60)` = `#E5A93C` — exact matches, no drift to Lovable's separate oklch palette. `npm run typecheck`, `build`, `lint`, `npm test` (11/11) all clean after the fix.

  - **(1) Client-side stale-closure bug, data-loss in the UI only (not the DB).** `handleTitleBlur` and `handleAdd` in `src/components/StaffDirectory.tsx` both call `notify(staff.map(...))` / `notify([...staff, created])`, closing over the `staff` variable from whichever render created that closure, instead of a functional `setStaff(prev => ...)` updater. Caught empirically: after setting all 4 job titles above (screenshot confirmed all 4 correctly displayed), then testing the add-staff-member form with one more entry, 3 of the 4 job titles reverted to "Not set" **in the browser only** — a follow-up direct `GET /api/staff-directory/seed-location` proved the database still had all 4 correct values the whole time. In real usage: a manager editing several people's titles in quick succession, then adding a new staff member, would see their just-typed edits appear to vanish (though a page refresh would show they're actually saved) — confusing and looks like data loss even though it isn't. Fix direction: switch both handlers to the functional `setStaff` form.
  - **(2) Integration gap — the Management-tier scenario described in the ask ("Andrea should show Restaurant Manager instead of blank") is not reachable via the app's current real UI at all**, traced at the code level (not just inferred): the paste-text roster entry point was removed in a prior session (`MEMORY.md` line 58, "per explicit request" — roster now comes only from file upload), so getting Andrea/Roberto/Tomas/Alessandro into `mergedRoster.employees` requires an actual upload. But `resolveRows.ts` resolves each row's role purely from that row's own parsed `roleName` string (`server/src/parsing/resolveRows.ts:111-116`), never from the already-matched employee's existing DB role — and for these 4 specifically, the parser *correctly* emits a blank `roleName` (an intentional, previously-fixed behavior: "the parser declining to guess is the right behavior," per the Staff Directory's own build entry above). A blank `roleName` means no `resolvedRoleId`, which sets `status: 'unmatched_role'` (`resolveRows.ts:137-141`). `src/components/ShiftUpload.tsx:81`'s `onCommitted` callback filters strictly to `r.status === 'matched'` before flushing anything into `App.tsx`'s `committed` state — so **`unmatched_role` rows never enter `mergedRoster.employees` at all**, meaning Andrea et al. wouldn't appear anywhere in the roster grid post-upload, blank-role or otherwise. The Staff Directory's `sectionForEmployee()` cross-reference logic itself is correctly implemented and has nothing wrong with it in isolation — it simply never gets an employee to apply to, for exactly the people it was built to help. Confirmed the roster grid genuinely renders empty (header row only) with zero uploads performed this session, consistent with this trace. Not fixed here (would need a decision: either commit `unmatched_role` rows as unassigned-role employees the way `new_employee` rows conceptually should also be reachable, or have `onCommitted`'s filter make an exception when the Staff Directory already has a Management-pattern title for that name).

- [x] FIXED the shift-id collision bug logged above. Confirmed first, before implementing, that a batch-scoped composite id (`${batchId}-${rowNumber}`) is the better fix over a random UUID: nothing in the codebase parses the existing `upload-shift-${rowNumber}` format structurally (checked — no `.split()`/regex/prefix-matching anywhere, it's only ever constructed once and compared for equality), so neither option is more "disruptive" in that sense. But `mergedRoster`'s merge relies on id equality as a deliberate dedup mechanism (a batch confirmed twice — double-click, retry, re-render timing — should collapse to one copy, not duplicate). A composite id preserves that (same batch + same row → same id, deterministic); a random UUID would silently break it, trading today's silent-data-loss bug for a silent-data-duplication bug on re-confirm. `batchId` is already a real, server-generated id unique per upload, so it's the correct, already-available namespace — no new dependency, no new server-side change needed.
  - `src/components/ShiftUpload.tsx`: `onCommitted` now passes `batchId` alongside the rows (`onCommitted?.(rows, data.batchId)`), typed as `(rows: PreviewRow[], batchId: string) => void`.
  - `src/App.tsx`: `handleCommitted(rows, batchId)` now builds `Shift.id` as `upload-shift-${batchId}-${row.rowNumber}` instead of just `upload-shift-${row.rowNumber}`. `Employee.id` (`upload-emp-${employeeName}`) deliberately left untouched — it's intentionally name-scoped so the same real person across separate uploads correctly dedupes into one employee record, which is correct existing behavior, not part of this bug.
  - `src/engine/commitBinding.test.ts`: updated the local `toCommitted` mirror to accept and use `batchId` the same way (kept in sync with the real source, learned from last time that a stale hand-copied mirror silently masks regressions). Added a new dedicated test — two separate batches (`'batch-aaa'`, `'batch-bbb'`) each starting their own `rowNumber` at 1 (the exact collision precondition) — asserting the resulting shift ids differ and both shifts survive a sequential merge (`afterA` → `afterB`) with both employees and both shifts present. All 12 tests (was 11) pass.

- [x] EVALUATED IBM Docling (MIT-licensed, self-hosted PDF/table extraction — github.com/DS4SD/docling) as a candidate tier-2 parser, per live web research into production roster-parsing approaches. Installed into an isolated venv (`server/docling-sidecar/.venv`, Python 3.14 — full dependency chain incl. torch 2.13 has cp314 wheels, no compat issues) rather than the global environment. **Install/runtime facts:** 1.30 GB venv, 566 MB one-time model download on first conversion (505 MB layout+TableFormer weights from Hugging Face, 61 MB RapidOCR weights from ModelScope, both cached locally afterward — confirmed zero network calls on a warm-cache re-run). **Accuracy, tested against both permanent fixtures with `TableFormerMode.ACCURATE` forced on:** on Gattopardo (text-layer PDF, borderless positionally-laid-out grid — the deterministic pdfjs parser's own target case), Docling's layout model classified all 433 text spans as standalone `text` at 100% confidence and never detected a `Table` region at all — the markdown/grid output is a scrambled bag of names/numbers with zero row/column structure, strictly worse than the existing deterministic parser (which already handles this exact file correctly, 160 rows). On Bar des Pres (scanned, no text layer — currently only reachable via the 10-minute Ollama fallback), Docling detected one table region (0.86 confidence) and produced a genuinely well-structured 22x9 grid via OCR+TableFormer, correctly capturing `UL`, `OFF`, hyphen-chains (`10:30-4:00-8:00-12`), multi-segment slash shifts (`10am/3pm-7pm/12am`), and free-text times (`4pm to 2am`) — in ~47s warm-cache versus Ollama's 10+ minutes. **Conclusion, reported to and confirmed by the user before implementing:** Docling adds negative value on text-layer PDFs (one clear negative data point, no positive evidence anywhere) but strong value specifically on the no-text-layer/scanned path. Scoped accordingly, NOT as a universal deterministic-fails→Docling→Ollama tier.

User chose the long-running local HTTP sidecar over a per-parse spawned subprocess (keeps model weights resident in memory across requests, mirrors the existing Ollama integration's shape) over abandoning Docling, when asked how to bridge a Python-only library into the Node/Express backend (explicit prior decision this session to stay Node-only was flagged as a real conflict before proceeding). `server/docling-sidecar/service.py` — a small FastAPI app (`/health`, `POST /convert`), binds to 127.0.0.1 only. Does structure extraction ONLY: returns `table.data.grid`'s cell text merge-expanded across each cell's `row_span`/`col_span` into a plain 2D JSON array — the exact same convention `buildMergeExpandedGrid()` already uses for Excel's `!merges` — so zero shift/role/leave-code semantics are duplicated on the Python side; all of that logic stays in the existing, already-tested TypeScript grid interpreter. `server/src/parsing/doclingClient.ts` — `isDoclingAvailable()` (cheap 3s-timeout reachability check), `extractGridViaDocling()` (POSTs the PDF buffer, 90s timeout — measured warm-cache latency plus headroom), `parseScannedPdfViaDocling()` (the full attempt: reachability → convert → `parseExcelGrid(grid, weekStart)`, returns `null` on unavailable/no-table-found so the caller falls through to Ollama exactly like an unreachable Ollama host does today — never a hard failure). `server/src/routes/schedules.ts`'s PDF branch restructured into 3 explicit cases: deterministic grid result (unchanged) → `!hasTextLayer` (NEW: Docling, then Ollama on `null`/`DoclingUnavailableError`) → `hasTextLayer` but deterministic failed (UNCHANGED: text-list parser, then Ollama). `npm run docling:sidecar` script added (`server/docling-sidecar/.venv` python + uvicorn on port 8901 with `kill-port` first, matching `server:dev`'s pattern) — first attempt used forward-slash paths and hit a real Windows `cmd.exe` quirk (forward slashes work fine as *arguments* to an already-resolved binary like `tsx`, but not when cmd itself has to resolve the executable path), fixed with a quoted backslash path. `.venv`/`__pycache__`/`*.pyc` added to `.gitignore`. **Live-verified, not just unit tests:** new `server/src/parsing/doclingClient.test.ts` (gated — skips cleanly if the sidecar isn't running, matching the existing gated-live-Ollama-test pattern) ran the real Bar des Pres PDF through the real running sidecar twice (once started manually, once via the new `npm run docling:sidecar` script specifically to confirm the script itself works, not just my manual invocation) — both passed, ~39-59s each. Follow-up diagnostic run showed 62 resolved rows / 17 anomalies / 65 leave records for 12 of the fixture's 20 staff; the other 8 (e.g. Robert Orgovan, Aya Boutaieb) simply have zero clean-resolvable shifts this particular week in the source data itself (all-`UL`/all-shorthand-flagged weeks) — confirmed this is real roster content, not a Docling extraction gap, by cross-checking against the hand-transcribed `barDesPresReference.test.ts` fixture. A handful of `"70"`/`"10"` cells were correctly flagged as unresolvable rather than guessed — an undocumented shorthand code not in the venue's given definitions (UL/CL/IN), consistent with the "never silently guess" principle. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, full parsing suite 47/47 pass + 1 gated skip (0 regressions). Root cause traced precisely: `hasTitleColumn = firstDayColIndex >= 2` in `deterministicGridParser.ts` decides column meaning by position only, correct for Bar des Pres's own "Role, Name, days..." ordering but silently backward for the equally common "Name, Role, days..." reverse. Also found: multi-outlet files only read their first outlet block (title/header rows of outlets 2+ misread as staff-row garbage); legend-code cells correctly flagged as unresolved (never-guess held) but the legend table itself gets misread as a second shift grid; bilingual Arabic/English and days-as-rows both defeat the deterministic tier entirely and fall through to a live Gemini call — a 4th tier (Excel/CSV's own pre-existing `parseRosterGrid` fallback) not previously accounted for when "the three-tier pipeline" was described; uneven PDF column spacing can fuse adjacent columns. Ramadan-density split shifts themselves: clean, no bug. Published as an Artifact gap report (ranked findings, per-fixture breakdown, coverage matrix) — report only, no fixes applied per the ask. Explicitly flagged as untested this round: Docling/Ollama got no new coverage since none of the 7 fixtures were genuinely scanned/image-only (only Bar des Pres remains verified there).
- [x] FIXED the column-swap bug found above. Replaced the position-only `hasTitleColumn`/`nameColIndex`/`titleColIndex` assignment in `deterministicGridParser.ts` with a content-based check for the specific 2-leading-column case: sample every row where BOTH candidate columns are populated (a genuine per-row title+name pair — the most reliable signal, and excludes section-header-only rows like "FLOOR" from corrupting the sample, since a header's own column placement doesn't reliably indicate which column means what) and score each column against the known role vocabulary via a new `isRecognizedRoleAlias()` export (`resolveRows.ts` — a query helper only, the alias table itself untouched). A zero-vs-nonzero hit split on either side confidently picks that side; both columns having hits falls back to comparing rates with a 0.25 margin; both zero (no paired rows at all, or a file whose real vocabulary genuinely isn't in `ROLE_ALIASES` yet) is genuinely ambiguous — no signal to guess from either way.
  - **Ambiguous rows are surfaced via the existing `AnomalyRecord` mechanism** (`employeeName: null`, both raw column values in `rawText`, `rowNumber: null` since no row is created) rather than resolved as `Matched` — this IS the codebase's existing "needs manual review" signal (the upload preview's "⚠ Needs manager review" block), used instead of adding a new field since `ParsedShiftRow` has no `needsReview` boolean (only the VLM-path's transient `VlmCell` shape does). Deliberately does **not** early-return with a distinct bail-out `templateLabel` the way "no day-header row" does — it stays `'Deterministic Grid Parser'` (the header/day-columns really were found correctly; only the leading-column semantics are uncertain) so the ambiguity anomaly reaches the actual user in the upload preview instead of silently triggering fall-through to Gemini/Ollama, which would just guess anyway and defeat the entire point.
  - **Two real bugs caught and fixed during self-verification, not just claimed correct:** (1) an initial margin-based version regressed 4 Bar des Pres tests — root cause: Bar des Pres's own real per-row titles ("RM", "JAM", "Head waiter 1"...) mostly aren't in `ROLE_ALIASES` either, so its title column's hit *rate* was low (~1/20) and didn't clear a flat 0.25 margin; fixed by making a zero-vs-nonzero split decisive on its own rather than rate-margin-based, since a real name column has an ~0% false-positive rate regardless of the title column's absolute hit rate. (2) a first attempt at the paired-rows-only sampling caused one small synthetic unit-test grid (zero genuine paired rows, header + single-column-only rows) to always read as ambiguous even though it's a completely standard, unambiguous shape — fixed with a two-tier fallback: prefer paired-row signal when any exist (the common case — a real roster's staff rows almost always carry both title and name per row), fall back to a full-column scan (noisier, header rows included) only when zero paired rows exist anywhere on the sheet.
  - Verified against the same 6 stress fixtures via the real live API (not just direct function calls): fixtures 1/2/6 now correctly resolve `employeeName`/`role` in the right order (spot-checked via anomaly `employeeName` attribution for fixture 2, since its main-grid cells are legend-codes that don't produce clean rows — confirmed "Ahmed Ali" not "Waiter"). Fixture 5 (Dubai fine-dining titles) is genuinely ambiguous by design — none of Outlet Manager/Chef de Rang/Demi-Chef de Rang/Commis de Salle/Chef de Partie/Commis Chef are in `ROLE_ALIASES` (confirmed by grep) — correctly flagged (0 rows, 9 anomalies) rather than silently guessed, which is the intended outcome for a genuine vocabulary gap, not a bug to chase within this task's scope. Fixtures 3/4 (bilingual, days-as-rows) unaffected, as expected — neither reaches this code path at all. Full parsing suite: 47/47 pass + 1 gated live-Ollama skip, **0 regressions**, confirmed via 3 full re-runs across the debugging process (including the real Bar des Pres reference tests and the live Docling-sidecar integration test, which recovered automatically once the regression was fixed — same root cause, not a separate bug). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. Untouched, exactly as scoped: `ROLE_ALIASES` contents, multi-outlet handling, legend-table misread, the bilingual/Gemini fallback path.
- [x] ROOT-CAUSED and FIXED the Bar des Pres Ollama upload failure logged above (`Ollama request failed (400): Failed to load image or audio file`). Confirmed step-by-step before touching code, per explicit instruction: (1) `qwen2.5vl:7b` IS pulled and Ollama IS running (`GET /api/tags`) — not an environment/model issue. (2) `parseVisionOllama.ts` base64-encodes whatever buffer it's handed with no PDF-to-image conversion step anywhere; `schedules.ts` passes `req.file.buffer` — the raw uploaded PDF, confirmed via its literal `%PDF-1.3` header bytes — straight into it for every Ollama-fallback branch. (3) Reproduced the exact error **directly against Ollama's API, outside the app**, by sending the same base64(raw PDF) payload via curl. (4) Confirmed the fix direction by rasterizing the same PDF's first page to a real PNG (via `pypdfium2`) and sending that instead — got past image-loading entirely, failed on an unrelated already-solved issue (context size, only because the manual test omitted `num_ctx`). **Verdict: a bug in the app's own request-building code, not an Ollama/environment issue** — the PDF-to-Ollama fallback path was built assuming `req.file.buffer` is already a raster image, true for the direct-image-upload branch but false for the PDF-with-no-text-layer branch.
  - Fix: new `server/src/parsing/pdfRasterize.ts` (`rasterizePdfPageToPng`), called at both PDF→Ollama fallback sites in `schedules.ts` (not the direct-image-upload branch, which already has a real image). **Implementation pivot, reported honestly rather than silently changed:** the original plan (`pdfjs-dist` + `@napi-rs/canvas`, keeping this Node-only per the project's established preference) segfaulted reproducibly in `page.render()` — isolated via 3 separate tests (full-size canvas, a much smaller canvas ruling out a size/memory cause, and a version with try/catch that never caught anything since the crash is below the JS layer) — confirmed via live web search as a known, documented category of pdfjs-dist/canvas-backend incompatibility, not specific to this file. Per the user's explicit "whichever is the better fit" authorization, pivoted to the Docling sidecar's already-installed `pypdfium2`, invoked as a short-lived **subprocess** per call (`server/docling-sidecar/rasterize_pdf.py`) rather than routed through the sidecar's HTTP server — rasterization has no model weights to keep warm unlike Docling's table extraction, and routing through the sidecar would make the "last resort" Ollama fallback newly depend on the sidecar's uptime, an unwanted coupling between tiers meant to be independent. `@napi-rs/canvas` was installed then fully removed after the segfault was confirmed.
  - **Verified working two ways, not just claimed:** (1) sent the app's actual `rasterizePdfPageToPng()` output straight to Ollama with a cheap one-word prompt — correctly identified as `"Table/grid of text."`, no image-loading error. (2) Stopped the Docling sidecar and uploaded the real Bar des Pres PDF through the live API, forcing the genuine `!hasTextLayer → Docling unavailable → rasterize → Ollama` path end-to-end: result was a 10-minute timeout, not the old 400 — a timeout only happens after Ollama accepts and holds the connection open while working; an outright rejection returns in milliseconds. The one-word test's own timing (22s model load + 36s prompt eval, just for one word) explains why the full structured-extraction request takes far longer — this hardware's pre-existing, already-documented slow-inference characteristic (explicitly called out in `parseVisionOllama.ts`'s own comments as an accepted tradeoff), not a new problem. Full parsing suite: 47/47 pass + 1 gated skip, 0 regressions, `tsc --noEmit` clean. Docling sidecar restarted and confirmed healthy afterward.

Flagged this discrepancy plainly rather than assuming the premise. Still attempted live reproduction as asked: drove the real browser (`playwright-core`, system Chrome) through the actual `ShiftUpload.tsx` dropzone for 3 structurally different real files — Gattopardo PDF (160/160 matched, deterministic tier), `sample-roster.xlsx` (3 rows, standard template), Bar des Pres PDF (62 rows via Docling) — all three: HTTP 200, zero failed network requests, zero relevant console errors (one unrelated 404, almost certainly `favicon.ico`). Confirmed structurally, not just by luck, why CORS/wrong-host theories don't apply to this app's real upload flow regardless of timing: `vite.config.ts`'s `server.proxy` forwards `/api/*` to `:4000` server-side (Node-to-Node), so the browser only ever fetches from its own origin in dev — never a cross-origin request at all — and `server/src/app.ts`'s `cors()` has no origin restriction regardless. Reported to the user as safe to proceed with the Lovable merge, with the documentation gap surfaced explicitly rather than silently resolved either way. **Status: investigated and cleared — non-reproducible against real files through the live upload flow. No data is fabricated either way — the sheet already produces 0 rows and multiple explanatory anomalies regardless — left as-is rather than expanded beyond this task's explicit scope.
- [x] BUILT Floor Plan — Sections & Duties (Phase 1 + Phase 2, per `floor-plan-sections-kickoff-brief.md`), ran through the full build order in one pass with only Phase 3 (notifications) deliberately gated.
  - **Schema** (`prisma/schema.prisma`, migration `add_floor_plan`): `FloorPlanImage` (server-stored — the brief explicitly corrects the "stays on device" mockup copy; served via `express.static('/uploads', ...)` under `server/uploads/floor-plans/`, .gitignored) → `FloorSection` (freeform polygon, `polygon: Json` stored as **fractional (0-1) coords** of image width/height, not absolute pixels — deliberate so the DOM-overlay assignment screen never needs a resize-tracking recompute step, just CSS percentages) → `SectionAssignment` (`@@unique([sectionId, staffId, shiftDate])`, `status: DRAFT|PUBLISHED`). The `status`/`publishedAt` fields go beyond the brief's literal `section_assignments` column list — added because Phase 3's "Publish" action (explicitly asked to be built now, minus the push step) needs a persisted draft/published distinction to act on. Flagged as an additive, easily-reversible schema call rather than silently added.
  - **Backend** (`server/src/routes/floorPlan.ts`, mounted at `/api/floor-plan`): upload (rasterizes a PDF via the existing `rasterizePdfPageToPng` — same pypdfium2 step the Ollama fallback uses, per the brief's explicit reuse instruction — with a clear 422 if the sidecar's unavailable, not a crash), section CRUD, `GET /:locationId/assignments?date=` (sections pre-joined with that date's assignments so the frontend does zero client-side joining), assignment upsert (shared write path for both interaction modes, keyed on the same unique triple so re-assigning the same person/section/day updates instead of erroring), unassign, and `/:locationId/publish` (bulk DRAFT→PUBLISHED for a date, assignment writes only — no notification call anywhere in this path).
  - **Frontend** (`src/components/FloorPlan/`): `SectionEditor.tsx` is the only Konva consumer (`react-konva`+`konva`+`use-image`) — click-to-place-vertices polygon drawing, click-near-first-point-or-"Close shape" to finish, inline label+pax form, existing sections listed below with quick label/pax edit and delete. `AssignmentBoard.tsx` implements the brief's core architecture decision: the floor plan renders as a static `<img>`, each saved section becomes an absolutely-positioned DOM `<div>` (`SectionOverlay.tsx`) — **positioned/sized to the polygon's own bounding rectangle** (not the whole image) with `clip-path` remapped into that box's local 0-100% space, so dnd-kit's rect-based hit-testing actually targets each section's own area rather than the full image for every section (an implementation detail the brief didn't spell out but is necessary for the described bbox-not-exact-polygon limitation to mean anything). Both interaction paths write through the same `assignStaff` call: drag-and-drop via `@dnd-kit/react`'s `DragDropProvider`/`useDraggable`/`useDroppable` (current API, not legacy `@dnd-kit/core`, per the brief) with `PointerSensor.configure({ activationConstraints })` giving touch a 500ms delay and mouse an 8px distance threshold (a documented dnd-kit option, confirmed by reading `@dnd-kit/dom`'s actual type declarations rather than assumed); tap-to-pick via `SectionPicker.tsx`, a search-select modal, opened on section click (suppressed for ~200ms after a drag ends via an `onDragStart`/`onDragEnd`-tracked flag, since a completed drag can otherwise still fire a trailing native click on the same element). Pax-capacity warning is a pure frontend `useMemo` comparative signal (flags a section whose own capacity is above the sections' average AND whose assigned/pax ratio is under half the average ratio of sections that do have staff) — soft-only, never blocks, matches the brief's "high-capacity section looks understaffed relative to a low-capacity one" wording rather than a fixed headcount floor.
  - **Nav restructure required and done**: the brief assumed a Home/Scheduling/Floor Plan/People bottom nav already existed in the app — it didn't (`App.tsx` was one flat stacked page, confirmed by grep before starting: zero "Floor Plan" hits anywhere in the repo). Added a minimal `activeTab` state + fixed bottom nav reusing the existing 4 labels, splitting the prior single-page stack across them (Home=Dashboard, Scheduling=ShiftUpload+roster grid, People=StaffDirectory, Floor Plan=new). One regression caught and fixed before it shipped: gating `StaffDirectory` behind the People tab meant its mount-time fetch (which feeds `staffDirectoryByName`, the Scheduling tab's Management-tier cross-reference) would never run unless someone visited People first — fixed by adding an independent `fetchStaffDirectory` call in `App.tsx` on mount, separate from the component's own fetch.

Root `tsconfig.json` gained `esModuleInterop: true` — `use-image`'s `export =` CJS shape needs it for the `import useImage from 'use-image'` default-import form; a safe, common Vite-template default, not previously set.

Root-caused via a direct DB query (assignments existed under `2026-08-24` when the local/system date was `2026-08-25`), not guessed. Fixed to build the date from local `Date` components instead. Re-ran the full pass after the fix and confirmed assignments landed under the correct local date and the publish response echoed it back correctly (`"12 assignments published for 2026-08-25"`).

(2) added a `page.reload()` step to verify server-confirmed (not optimistic client) state, but this app's bottom-nav tabs are plain React state with no URL routing, so a reload resets to the Home tab — fixed by re-clicking "Floor Plan" after reload. A run that failed on this second bug (session ended before it could be fixed) was independently checked via direct DB query at the time and confirmed all 12 assignments + all 8 sections had in fact written correctly regardless — the failure was purely in the test script's own follow-up verification step, not a sign of a data-loss bug.

Section 8 (14 pax, below the average) was **also** left with 0 staff, deliberately, to prove the warning is comparative rather than "any empty section warns" — correctly `warned=false` despite being fully empty, because it isn't a high-capacity section relative to the others. Sections 3/4 (15/14 pax, below average, lightly staffed) also correctly never trigger regardless of their own ratio, since they're excluded from eligibility by capacity alone. Per-page header text moved to each route's `handle` (typed `RouteHandle`, read via `useMatches()`), defined in `router.tsx` rather than exported from route files — exporting them tripped `react-refresh/only-export-components` and would have broken Fast Refresh on all five pages. **(4) RadialDock index math fixed**: `STATIONS` was hardcoded `5` against a 4-tab array so the mod-5 wrap and mod-4 snap disagreed and the dial could park on a phantom station; now derived from `tabs.length`. **(5) `activeIndex` no longer lies** — was `Math.max(0, findIndex())`, so `/profile` rendered Home as focused with `aria-current="page"`; now `-1` when unmatched, with the dial's numeric spring target kept separate so it just rests. **(6) Six runtime-imported packages moved out of `devDependencies`** (`lucide-react`, `clsx`, `tailwind-merge`, `tailwindcss`, `@tailwindcss/vite`, `tw-animate-css`) — all are imported by shipped code or required to compile production CSS. **(7) `AppStateContext` value + its three handlers memoized** (`useMemo`/`useCallback`) so consumers stop re-rendering on unrelated provider state changes. **(8) AppShell header reads the real venue name** from `useAppState().config.name` for the eyebrow default and derives the avatar letter from it, replacing hardcoded `'Demo Venue'`/`'S'`. - [x] REPLACED the fixed demo week (`WEEK_START = '2026-08-17'` constant in `AppStateContext.tsx`) with a real, live-fetched current week. `src/engine/weekStart.ts` (`currentWeekStart()`) computes the real Monday of the real current week; `AppStateContext` now holds `weekStart`/`setWeekStart` as real state (seeded from `currentWeekStart()`, not a literal), a `weekShifts` array populated by `refetchWeekShifts()` (calls `GET /api/shifts/:locationId?weekStart=`, re-runs on every `weekStart` change), and `mergedRoster` folds `weekShifts` in alongside upload-committed shifts — synthesizing a minimal `Employee` for a real assigned user who has a RotaBuilder-assigned shift but no upload-derived record yet, so the roster grid/matrix render them anyway. Six real mutators added and exposed from `useAppState()`, all funneling through the same `refetchWeekShifts()` after writing so every consumer (RotaBuilder, Shift Editor, Team Matrix, Personal Rota) sees one consistent post-write state: `createRotaShift`/`updateRotaShift`/`deleteRotaShift`/`bulkCreateRotaShifts`/`publishCurrentWeek`/`fetchCurrentWeekPublishStatus`. `bulkCreateRotaShifts` threads `createdById` through so bulk-created shifts get a real actor in `AuditLog`. FIXED alongside: a failed `fetchWeekShifts` for a newly-selected week previously left the *previous* week's shifts rendered (looks like correct data for the wrong week, not an error/empty state) — `refetchWeekShifts` now clears `weekShifts` on failure. Also a sequence-number guard on refetches so a slow response for an older week can't overwrite a newer week's shifts during rapid week navigation. Verified: `npm run typecheck`, `server:typecheck`, `build`, `lint`, `npm test` all clean at each step; live-verified via the real API (see Task 12 entry below) that `weekStart` genuinely drives what `GET /api/shifts` returns.

Rows now come from the union of `mergedRoster`'s sections and the staff directory (fetched unconditionally on mount), grouped through the shared `roleGrouping` module so directory-only staff land in their real role section. The role catalog for new shifts now reads from the staff directory too (already returns `roleId` per Task 9's directory fix) instead of only the current week's shifts; `openNew` no longer silently falls back to the alphabetically-first role when a person's role can't be resolved — it leaves the field blank so the existing required-field guard makes the manager pick one.
