Every row created during this pass (2 shifts, 1 template) was deleted via the real API afterward; the attendance log (no DELETE endpoint exists for `AttendanceLog` by design, consistent with the Staff Directory/Shoutouts precedent) was deleted via a throwaway direct-Prisma script, then the script itself deleted. Pre-existing rows from earlier tasks' own review passes (2 already-published shifts on the real current week) were left untouched — confirmed present both before and after, unaffected. Dev server stopped and port 4000 confirmed closed (`kill-port 4000`) afterward. **One honest side effect, not fully reversible:** the real current week already had a `RotaPublish` record from an earlier task's own review pass (2 shifts already `status: published`); this task's publish-status check necessarily called the real publish endpoint too, which `upsert`s that record's `publishedAt`/`notifiedCount` — the timestamp was advanced to this task's real run time as a result. Not a data-integrity issue (the record's shape/meaning is unchanged, just its timestamp), but flagged plainly rather than silently left unmentioned. **NOT verified — explicitly, per the brief, no browser tool exists in this environment:** the drag-and-drop interaction itself, RotaBuilder's visual grid layout, and the Shift Editor's rendering all need a human with a real browser.
- [x] BUILT the Floor Plan AM/PM + notify-tracking + 86 List phase (9-task plan, `.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-27-floor-plan/`), each task built and reviewed independently. Kept the existing freeform-polygon/server-persisted section base untouched throughout — no task in this plan touches `SectionEditor.tsx` at all.
  - **`SectionAssignment` is now period-aware.** `prisma/schema.prisma` added `AssignmentPeriod` (`AM`/`PM`) as a required column on `SectionAssignment` (`@default(AM)`), folded into the row's own uniqueness (`@@unique([sectionId, staffId, shiftDate, period])`) — the same person can now hold one AM assignment and one separate PM assignment for the same section/date without collision, where before a same-day reassignment for the other half of the day would have silently overwritten the first. `server/src/routes/floorPlan.ts`'s list/create/publish endpoints all take a required `period` query/body param and filter/write by it.
  - **Per-person notify tracking replaced the old no-op "Publish day" button.** `SectionAssignment` gained `notifiedAt`/`publishedAt` columns. `PATCH /api/floor-plan/assignments/:id/notify` stamps `notifiedAt` for one assignment (an honest, manager-initiated tracking event — no real push/SMS/WhatsApp send exists anywhere in this codebase, consistent with how Shift/Attendance notify-equivalents already work). `POST /api/floor-plan/:locationId/publish` now flips every `DRAFT` row for that date+period to `PUBLISHED` and stamps both `publishedAt` and `notifiedAt` at once — publish IS the notify event for a freshly-published assignment, with the individual endpoint left for re-notifying one person later (e.g. after a reassignment). `AssignmentBoard.tsx`'s AM/PM toggle (`chip`/`chip-active` tab pair) drives which period's assignments load/write; its publish button now reads "Publish & notify" and shows an honest per-run result banner (assignment count actually published+notified) instead of the old silent/no-op action. `SectionDetail.tsx` shows a per-assignee notified/not-notified badge plus an individual "Notify" button sourced from the same `notifiedAt` field.
  - **REAL audit logging added to `floorPlan.ts`**, a route that previously wrote zero `AuditLog` rows for any of its actions. Assignment create/delete now write `SHIFT_ASSIGNED`; individual notify writes `ASSIGNMENT_NOTIFIED`; publish writes one summary `ASSIGNMENT_NOTIFIED` row (`entityId` = the locationId, since it covers many rows at once, not one) with the published+notified count in its `note`. Brings this route in line with every other mutation route in the codebase, all of which already audit-logged.
  - **FIXED a real duty-label-clobbering bug during Task 6's review**, verified again end-to-end in this task's own live check (see below): the assignment-upsert route couldn't originally tell "a plain drag-drop/tap reassign, no label typed" apart from "an explicit clear of an existing label" — both arrived as `dutyLabel: null/undefined` after a naive truthy-or-null coercion, so re-assigning an already-labeled staff member (the single most common daily action) silently wiped their label every time. Fixed by checking `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(req.body, 'dutyLabel')` — the row's existing `dutyLabel` is only touched when the request actually sent that key at all; a plain reassign omits it entirely and the upsert's `update` clause becomes `{}`. `SectionDetail.tsx` also gained the inline duty-label editor this fix was needed for (edit-in-place, save-on-blur/Enter).
  - **BUILT the 86 List** as a genuinely new, real, backend-persisted board — a shared out-of-stock list by station, not a per-user/local concept. New `EightySixItem` model (`itemName`, `station`, `status: EIGHTY_SIXED|BACK_ON`, `note`, `eightySixedAt`/`backOnAt` + who-actioned-it ids) and `server/src/routes/eightySix.ts`: `GET /api/eighty-six/:locationId` (active-only by default; `?includeResolved=1` also returns `BACK_ON` items — "what's out right now" is the default view, not a full history dump), `POST /api/eighty-six` (86 an item, writes `ITEM_86D` to `AuditLog`), `PATCH /api/eighty-six/:itemId/back-on` (writes `ITEM_BACK_ON`, 409s if already back on). Frontend: `src/components/FloorPlan/EightySixBoard.tsx` (items grouped by station, 86/back-on actions) mounted as a third tab in `FloorPlanTab.tsx` alongside the existing Assignment Board and Section Editor tabs. "Live" here means real-DB-backed and reloadable, same as every other "live" feature in this codebase (RotaBuilder, Hour Tracking, etc.) — explicitly NOT real-time push/websocket; a second manager's device only sees a change on its own next fetch. Known pre-existing gating condition this plan doesn't touch: the tab is unreachable until a floor plan image + sections already exist for the venue.
  - **Full verification, all clean:** `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` (0 errors, the same 1 pre-existing `AppStateContext.tsx` warning), `npm run build`, `npm test` (23/23), `npm run test:server` (60 pass / 2 skipped by design — gated live-Ollama/live-Docling, not failures).
  - **Live E2E against the real running server + real Postgres DB, real `seed-location` staff, nothing mocked** (fixture floor-plan image/section created via direct Prisma — multipart upload itself wasn't in scope for this check): created an AM assignment and a separate PM assignment for the same person/section/date and confirmed both persist independently, with `GET .../assignments?period=` correctly returning only the matching period's row each time. Set a duty label (`dutyLabel: "Expo"`) on the AM assignment, then re-sent a plain reassign with no `dutyLabel` key at all and confirmed the label survived — the Task 6 fix holds end-to-end, not just by code inspection. Individually notified that assignment and confirmed both `notifiedAt` on the row and a real `AuditLog(ASSIGNMENT_NOTIFIED)` row. Created a fresh `DRAFT` assignment on a second date/staff and published that date+period, confirming exactly the one newly-`DRAFT` row got both `publishedAt` and `notifiedAt`, plus a summary `AuditLog(ASSIGNMENT_NOTIFIED)` row with the correct count in its note. 86'd a test item, confirmed it appeared in the active (no-`includeResolved`) list grouped under its station with a real `AuditLog(ITEM_86D)` row, marked it back on, confirmed it left the active view but reappeared with `?includeResolved=1` showing `BACK_ON`, plus a real `AuditLog(ITEM_BACK_ON)` row. All fixture rows (FloorPlanImage, FloorSection, 3 SectionAssignments, 1 EightySixItem, 4 AuditLog rows) deleted afterward and cleanup independently re-queried to confirm zero remaining. **NOT verified — explicitly, no browser tool exists in this environment:** the AM/PM toggle's visual behavior, the drag-and-drop interaction, and the 86 List's on-screen layout all need a human with a real browser.

## Next Sprint Goals (Scheduling phase handoff)
- `weekStart` is now real app-wide state (`useAppState().weekStart`/`setWeekStart`, backed by `src/engine/weekStart.ts`'s `currentWeekStart()`), not a per-screen constant. Floor Plan/People/Identity (the next phase) should read the current week from `useAppState()` if they need one — do not reintroduce a fixed/hardcoded date the way the pre-Scheduling `WEEK_START` constant did; that's exactly the bug this phase fixed.
- RotaBuilder and Shift Editor share the same six `AppStateContext` mutators (`createRotaShift`/`updateRotaShift`/`deleteRotaShift`/`bulkCreateRotaShifts`/`publishCurrentWeek`/`fetchCurrentWeekPublishStatus`) against the same `/api/shifts`/`/api/rota-templates`/`/api/attendance` backend — no separate data path to keep in sync, no drift possible by construction. Any future screen that needs to read/write a shift should extend this same set rather than adding a parallel one.
- Known, documented technical debt from this phase's own reviews (parked deliberately, not silent gaps — see `.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-26-scheduling/progress.md` for the full ruling on each):
  - A published week's `locked` status doesn't clear when a shift is later deleted from it (`publish-status` only checks `Shift.updatedAt`; a delete leaves no row to bump). Needs a deliberate design decision on how deletions should affect publish tracking — not a quick patch.
  - Clock-in has a narrow TOCTOU race: two concurrent clock-in requests for the same user can both pass the "any open log?" check and create two open `AttendanceLog` rows. Low-likelihood (needs a genuine double-click race across devices within milliseconds), self-correcting on the next clock-out, parked rather than fixed — same bug class already fixed once elsewhere in this repo (swap-request approval, `bec8aea`) if a real fix is wanted later.
  - A handful of Minor findings, ledgered not fixed: `DraftShift.roleName` is write-only dead state in RotaBuilder; a same-cell drag-drop fires a no-op `PATCH`; the Shift Editor's edit path leaves a stale `roleId: ''` initializer (harmless, unused); `nameKey` doesn't collapse whitespace/diacritics (pre-existing, shared with `staffDirectoryByName`); `totalHours` in `rosterView.ts` is now fully dead code repo-wide since Hour Tracking switched to real attendance data.
- **Floor Plan phase handoff (for whoever picks up People/Identity/Onboarding next):** `SectionAssignment` is now period-aware — it has a required `period: AM|PM` column, folded into its own uniqueness key (`sectionId, staffId, shiftDate, period`). Anything that reads or writes `SectionAssignment` going forward (a People/Identity screen surfacing "today's assignment," a reporting view, an onboarding flow that pre-assigns a new hire) must account for that dimension — a query that only filters by `sectionId, staffId, shiftDate` will now silently match up to two rows (AM and PM) instead of one.
- The 86 List (`EightySixItem`, `server/src/routes/eightySix.ts`, `src/components/FloorPlan/EightySixBoard.tsx`) is real and backend-persisted, but "live" means real-DB-backed/reloadable-on-fetch, not real-time push — same convention as every other "live" feature in this codebase (RotaBuilder, Hour Tracking, etc.). A second manager's device only sees an 86/back-on change on its own next fetch; there is no websocket/SSE layer anywhere in this repo. Don't assume one exists when building the next feature that touches this list.
- Known, documented technical debt from the Floor Plan AM/PM phase's own reviews (parked deliberately, not silent gaps — see `.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-27-floor-plan/` review files for the full ruling on each):
  - Duty-label edits and real reassignments both go through the same assignment-upsert endpoint and both write the same `SHIFT_ASSIGNED` audit action with no field distinguishing "the person changed" from "only the label text changed" — the audit trail can't currently answer "who was working this section" vs. "who last touched the label" as two separate questions.
  - `AssignmentBoard` remounts/flashes on every mutation's loading state (assign, notify, publish all trigger a full section refetch+re-render rather than an optimistic/targeted update) — functionally correct, visually rough.
  - No Escape-to-cancel on `SectionDetail`'s inline duty-label editor — blur/Enter commit, but there's no way to back out of an in-progress edit without either saving it or clicking away.
  - A synthetic `upload-emp-<name>` actor id (an uploaded roster row that never matched a real `User`) now hits a live FK-500 path when passed as `createdById`/`actorId` on any of the Floor Plan phase's audit-writing calls — previously these calls omitted the actor entirely, so this risk didn't exist. Same profile as an already-accepted risk in `RotaBuilder.tsx`; worth a real fix (404 instead of a raw FK 500, matching the treatment `shifts.ts` already got for `roleId`/`userId`) if it's ever hit in practice.
  - The 86 List's "Back on" and `SectionDetail`'s "Notify" in-flight guards are both single-slot (track one in-flight id, not a set) — clicking two different items' action buttons in quick succession can let the first one's `finally` re-enable a button before its own request is confirmed done, in a narrow interleaving. Inherited from an already-reviewed-and-accepted pattern; fixing both would mean a `Set`-based guard in both places, out of proportion for what's actually a rare double-click edge case.
- **People + Identity phase handoff (for whoever picks up Confirm Roster next):** a real, if deliberately lightweight, identity/session system now exists — `Session`/`OtpCode` (phone+OTP only, no password/email/OAuth). Backend: resolved via `server/src/middleware/requireSession.ts` (`Authorization: Bearer <token>` → real `req.user: User`, or 401). Frontend: `src/state/IdentityContext.tsx`/`useIdentity()`, mounted in `App.tsx` (not `main.tsx`, which has no provider tree). Any future staff-facing screen that needs to know "who is logged in" should use `requireSession`/`useIdentity()` — do not invent a second identity concept or a parallel session store.
- Known, real, minor gap surfaced during Task 13's live check, parked deliberately rather than fixed: `DELETE /api/policy-documents/:id` (`server/src/routes/policyDocuments.ts`) removes the DB row but never unlinks the file from `UPLOAD_DIR` — a "deleted" policy document's PDF stays servable at its old `/uploads/...` URL until removed from disk by hand. Worth a one-line fix (an `unlink` call in the same handler) next time this route is touched.
- Known, documented technical debt from the People + Identity phase's own task reviews (see `.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-28-people-identity/progress.md` for the full ruling on each), none blocking, none touched in this pass: `JoinFlow`'s success redirect uses `window.location.href` (full page reload) rather than SPA `navigate()`; the onboarding invite fetch on the frontend uses an inline `fetch` rather than this codebase's established `request<T>()`/`ApiError` client convention (inherited from the plan's own brief snippet, not an implementer choice); an unused `'error'` Phase variant sits in `JoinFlow`; `MyShiftsRoute`'s "next 5 shifts" trusts the server's contract with no client-side slice as a backstop.
- **FINAL WHOLE-BRANCH REVIEW fix wave** for the Floor Plan phase closed 4 real gaps the per-task reviews didn't catch (only visible once the whole branch was reviewed together): the 86 List's CSS was entirely missing (added, mirroring existing `.fp-*` patterns); every audit row this phase wrote had a null actor despite full plumbing existing (now threads `useAppState().currentEmployeeId` into all 7 mutation call sites); the individual re-notify button was hidden once an assignment had already been notified once, making the endpoint's own justified use case (re-notify after a later reassignment) unreachable (now always visible, labeled Notify/Re-notify); and neither of this phase's two riskiest pieces (the duty-label preserve/clear contract, AM/PM key isolation) had automated test coverage despite an established real-server/real-DB test pattern already in this repo (`server/src/routes/shifts.test.ts`) — added `server/src/routes/floorPlan.test.ts` covering both. Independently re-reviewed with byte-identical build hashes to the fix report; all 7 findings confirmed addressed, no new blocking issues.

Fixed to also strip a single leading `0` after the `00`/`971` strips, in the right order (`58c5928`). Verified via all 3 formats normalizing identically, full test re-run, and no regression on the one legitimate edge case checked (a malformed `'0971...'` input).

- [x] **FINAL WHOLE-BRANCH REVIEW fix wave** for the People + Identity/Onboarding/Join phase — 1 Critical, 7 Important and 2 cheap Minor findings that only became visible once the whole branch was reviewed together, all closed in one wave (`f005cfb` server, `1f6ebb0` frontend).
  - **[CRITICAL] The dev-OTP echo was fail-OPEN.** Both `identity.ts` and `join.ts` echoed the real OTP whenever `NODE_ENV !== 'production'` — and nothing in this repo (no script, Dockerfile, Procfile or start command) ever sets `NODE_ENV=production`, so the echo was on by default everywhere. Combined with Join's auto-match-by-phone, that was a one-request impersonation of any staff member with a phone on file. Now fail-closed and opt-in: `process.env.ALLOW_DEV_OTP_ECHO === 'true'` gates both the HTTP `devCode` field **and** the plaintext `console.log` of the code (previously unconditional, so real user OTPs would have been written to server logs in a real deployment even with the HTTP echo off). The capability was deliberately kept, not removed — this codebase's entire OTP-delivery story depends on it being usable in dev/test without an SMS provider.
  - **[IMPORTANT] `phoneDigits()` can collide two different people into one identity.** It unconditionally strips a leading `971` and a leading `0` from any input, so an Indian mobile `9715551234` and a UAE `+971 555 1234` both normalize to `5551234` — and `User.phone` has no unique constraint. Worse, identity's login lookup ran `findMany({ where: { isActive: true } })` with **no location scope at all**, then took the first arbitrary match across every venue in the database. Both routes now return `409` on an ambiguous match instead of silently picking one, and `/request-otp`/`/verify-otp` both require a real, FK-validated `locationId` (matching what `join.ts` already did) so the lookup is venue-scoped. This is a request-shape change — `src/api/identity.ts`'s `requestLoginOtp`/`verifyLoginOtp` now take `locationId` first.
  - **[IMPORTANT] Sign-out never revoked the session server-side.** `logout()` only did a `localStorage.removeItem`; the `Session` row and its 30-day token stayed valid indefinitely — on the shared venue device this product actually runs on, "signing out" didn't end access. Added `revokeSession(plainToken)` to `server/src/lib/identity.ts` (a `deleteMany`, so it's idempotent — revoking an already-gone session is a no-op, never an error), `DELETE /api/identity/session` behind `requireSession`, and a `bearerToken(req)` helper exported from `requireSession.ts` so the handler can read the raw token rather than re-parsing the header. `IdentityContext.logout()` now calls it best-effort — deliberately not awaited, so a network failure still clears local state instead of trapping the user in a session they asked to leave.
  - **[IMPORTANT] The whole login path was unreachable from the UI.** `requestLoginOtp`/`verifyLoginOtp` had **zero call sites** anywhere in `src/` outside their own definitions — Join was the only way to ever get a session, and once one expired there was no way back in (`MyShiftsRoute` even told users to "log in with your phone number," pointing at nothing). `JoinFlow.tsx` now carries a join/login mode toggle: login skips the `fullName` field and the whole `pending` branch (login either issues a session or errors — it never files a join request), and switching modes resets to the phone step so a code minted for one purpose is never replayed against the other. `MyShiftsRoute` now clears a stale session on a `401` (rather than showing an error with no way out) and its not-signed-in state links to `/join`.
  - **[IMPORTANT] `/join`, `/my-shifts` and `/onboarding` were linked from nowhere** — reachable only by typing the URL. `/my-shifts` (the staff-facing home screen) now has a real destination in the `AppShell` header next to `/profile`, since the four-tab `RadialDock` is the manager-side nav and has no room for it; `/onboarding` is now a header action on `/people`, reusing the exact pattern `/scheduling` already uses for its Shift-editor link; `/join` is reached from `MyShiftsRoute`'s not-signed-in state (it's for people who aren't signed in, so it correctly stays out of the authenticated nav).
  - **[IMPORTANT] The onboarding invite's `?location=` param was silently ignored.** `onboarding.ts` mints `${baseUrl}/join?location=${locationId}` into both the QR code and the WhatsApp text, but `JoinRoute.tsx` hardcoded `locationId="seed-location"` — so every invite, from any venue, joined the same hardcoded location. Now read via `useSearchParams()`, falling back to `'seed-location'` so bare `/join` navigation is unchanged.
  - **[IMPORTANT] `availability.ts`'s POST/DELETE accepted an arbitrary `userId` with no session or ownership check** — the one staff-facing mutation route in this phase that never used the session system the phase itself built. `POST /` trusted `req.body.userId` outright; `DELETE /:id` never checked who owned the mark. Both are now behind `requireSession`: POST derives the user from `req.user.id` and **ignores any body `userId` entirely**, DELETE 403s on a mark the caller doesn't own. `GET /:userId` deliberately stays **open** — `RotaBuilder.tsx` is a manager screen with no session of its own and must read *other people's* availability to render its informational badges; gating it would have broken that. Clients updated to send the Bearer token (`setAvailability(token, …)`/`removeAvailability(token, id)`).
  - **[IMPORTANT] `terminatedAt` was never written or read anywhere**, re-introducing exactly the two-sources-of-truth problem the plan's own judgment call (reuse `isActive`+`terminatedAt` rather than add a parallel status enum) existed to avoid. `staffDirectory.ts`'s PATCH now keeps the pair in lockstep — terminating stamps a real date, re-activating clears it — and `toDto()` exposes it as a `YYYY-MM-DD` string, matching `hiredAt`'s existing serialization. Only a genuine transition writes it, so re-sending `isActive: false` doesn't overwrite the original termination date.
  - **[MINOR] OTP rows were keyed on the raw submitted phone string, not normalized digits** — it worked only by coincidence (the client happened to send an identical string at request and verify time). `createOtpCode`/`verifyOtpCode` now both key on `phoneDigits()`, consistent with how user-matching already treats phone numbers. Verified live: a code requested as `"+971 555 1234"` now verifies as `"05551234"`.
  - **[MINOR] `OnboardingWizard.tsx` overstated two steps' copy** — the `venue` step said "Confirm your venue details" while rendering no venue details at all, and `review` unconditionally claimed "Your roster has been imported" even when reached via the step's own "Skip for now" button. Both now accurate; review tracks whether a real import actually happened and says "You can import your roster later from the Scheduling page" otherwise.
  - **Verified, all clean:** `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings), `npm run build`, `npm test` (23/23), `npm run test:server` (67 pass / 2 skipped by design) — byte-identical to Task 13's baseline, no regressions. **Live E2E against the real running server + real Supabase DB, nothing mocked, in two phases** to prove the OTP flag actually gates behavior in both directions: with the flag unset, `devCode` was confirmed absent from both `/api/identity/request-otp` and `/api/join/request-otp` responses, the new required-`locationId` contract returned a real 400/404, and a deliberately-constructed colliding phone pair returned a real 409; with `ALLOW_DEV_OTP_ECHO=true`, `devCode` appeared and the server log line printed, a cross-format verify succeeded, and the full session lifecycle was exercised — `GET /api/my-shifts` 200 with the token, `POST /api/availability` 401 without one, a **spoofed body `userId` confirmed ignored by re-querying the DB** (the mark belonged to the session user, not the spoofed one), 403 on deleting someone else's mark with the mark confirmed still present afterward, 204 on deleting one's own, `GET /api/availability` confirmed still open, then `DELETE /api/identity/session` → 204, the `Session` row confirmed gone from the DB, and the same token confirmed 401 afterward. `terminatedAt` was round-tripped (set on terminate, not re-stamped on a repeat, cleared on re-activate) and the invite URL confirmed to carry `?location=seed-location`. All test rows (2 users, sessions, availability marks, OTP rows) deleted and independently re-queried to confirm zero remaining.
  - **Process hygiene.** Four stale `npm run server:dev` chains from earlier tasks in this plan were found still alive (one holding port 4000, three orphaned `tsx watch` supervisors whose child servers had already been killed by later `kill-port` runs) — all traced via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` parent chains to confirm they were this repo's own dev servers before being stopped, along with the one this fix wave started itself. Final `netstat -ano` shows **no LISTENING socket on :4000 or :5173** (only harmless kernel `TIME_WAIT` entries from closed client connections), and a `Win32_Process` command-line sweep confirms no ShiftSync server, vite or `tsx watch` process remains.

A human with a real browser still needs to look at all of these.

The user chose to skip it due to time and proceed to the next phase on the existing API/DB/code-trace verification alone (all test data was reverted, both dev servers stopped cleanly before moving on). **This is at least the third time in this project a phase has shipped without its interactive/visual behavior ever being looked at by a human or a real browser tool** (the same gap was flagged, unresolved, at the end of the Floor Plan phase, again at the end of this phase's own Task 13/fix-wave, and again at the end of the Confirm Roster phase — see that phase's own Task 3 entry above, which added its restructured review screen to this same gate rather than opening a second, separate note). No automated browser-driving tool is available in this environment (`browser_act` exists but is permanently `degraded: true` here — no `page-agent`, no configured OpenRouter/Ollama key) — closing this gap requires either a human clicking through directly (the script above is ready to reuse) or configuring that tooling. **Flag this explicitly again before any of this phase's UI — Join, onboarding, My Shifts, availability marking, RotaBuilder's badge, Confirm Roster's restructured review screen (needs-review/matched sections, sticky confirm bar, per-row reviewed toggle), and now the AI Voice Intent phase's mic button/recording flow and confirm-before-execute sheet (`VoiceCommandSheet.tsx`, the `RadialDock` mic wiring) — goes in front of a real manager or staff member.** The AI Voice Intent phase's own acceptance-gate task (Task 8) attempted no exception to this: the actual browser microphone-permission flow, `MediaRecorder` audio capture, and the confirm sheet's on-screen appearance were verified only by real backend API calls against a real running server (see that phase's own Completed Core Components entry above for exactly what those calls proved) — same "API/DB/code-trace only, no human/browser look" treatment every prior phase in this list has gotten, not a new or different gap. **Add, Actor Identity Enforcement + Sign-In Gate phase and Schedule Persistence phase:** the new `RequireSession` redirect-to-`/join?mode=login` flow (on `/scheduling`, `/floor-plan`, `/people`, `/onboarding`) and `SchedulingRoute.tsx`'s `?week=` URL sync (a hard refresh reloading the actually-viewed week, and a bare visit still correctly defaulting to today) were both verified only via API-level live testing and static trace-through of the fixed effect logic — explicitly not by a human or tool loading the actual page, confirmed by the user as remaining open rather than closed by either phase. Add both to the list that goes in front of a real manager before launch.
- **⚠️ STANDING SECURITY GAP — most mutation routes still derive the acting user from a client-supplied body field, not from session.** Confirmed by reading the real route files, not assumed: `server/src/routes/swapRequests.ts` (`requestedById`/`reviewedById`), `server/src/routes/join.ts`'s PATCH (`reviewedById`), `server/src/routes/staffDirectory.ts`, and `server/src/routes/floorPlan.ts` all take an actor/reviewer id as a plain string in the request body — none of them are gated behind `requireSession`, so any caller who knows or guesses a real user id can act as that person. The only routes that actually derive the actor from a verified session today are `server/src/routes/myShifts.ts` (GET) and, since the People/Identity fix wave above, `server/src/routes/availability.ts`'s POST/DELETE. **The AI Voice Intent plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-27-ai-voice-intent.md`) fixes this only for the brand-new `/api/voice/execute` endpoint it adds** — that endpoint derives every actor id from `req.user` and re-checks role server-side before executing anything, by design. It does NOT retrofit `requireSession` onto the pre-existing REST routes it calls into (`swapRequests.ts`, `join.ts`) — those keep accepting a client-supplied `reviewedById` on their own HTTP paths exactly as before; the voice layer only ever calls their extracted, shared mutation *functions* directly with a session-derived id, bypassing the insecure HTTP layer rather than fixing it. **This is a deliberate scope boundary, not an oversight — but it means the underlying gap is deferred, not resolved, and remains fully exploitable via the original REST endpoints regardless of whether the voice feature ships.** Whoever picks this up next: retrofitting `requireSession` onto `swapRequests.ts`/`join.ts`/`staffDirectory.ts`/`floorPlan.ts` (deriving `reviewedById`/`actorId` from `req.user.id` instead of the request body, the same way `availability.ts` was already fixed) is real, contained, and overdue security work — flag it again before this app is used by anyone outside this dev environment. **Update, AI Voice Intent phase now closed (Task 8):** `/api/voice/execute` shipped and was independently re-verified — 2 full adversarial fix-and-re-review rounds plus this closing task's own live E2E check, all confirming it correctly re-derives the actor from session, re-checks role server-side, and re-validates location/ownership on every entity it touches before writing. That makes it a second, independently-built example of the CORRECT pattern (session-derived actor + role re-check + location scoping) existing side-by-side with `availability.ts`'s POST/DELETE from the earlier People + Identity phase — and, exactly as predicted above, it did NOT retrofit `requireSession` onto `swapRequests.ts`/`join.ts` themselves; it only bypasses their insecure HTTP layer by calling the new `server/src/lib/actions/*.ts` functions directly with a session-derived id. Two independently-built correct examples now exist side-by-side with 4 still-unfixed insecure REST routes on the exact same underlying models — the fix is proven out twice over, the remaining retrofit work is unchanged in scope, and it is now more overdue than ever. **CLOSED — Actor Identity Enforcement phase (2026-08-31):** the retrofit predicted above shipped for real. `swapRequests.ts`, `join.ts`, `staffDirectory.ts`, and `floorPlan.ts` now all derive actor identity from `req.user` (via a shared `requireManager` middleware alongside `requireSession`), never from the request body, with location-scoping added everywhere it was missing (including two real pre-existing gaps this phase found along the way: `PATCH`/`DELETE /floor-plan/sections/:id` had zero ownership check at all, and `POST /floor-plan/assignments` checked its two referenced entities against each other but never against the caller's own venue). See the dedicated completion entry below for the full account, the 4 findings the mandatory final whole-branch review caught, and the live-test evidence.
- **AI Voice Intent phase handoff (for whoever builds the next feature that mutates a swap request, join request, or availability mark):** `server/src/lib/actions/{swapActions,joinActions,availabilityActions}.ts` now holds the actual create/decide/upsert logic for those three things, extracted out of `swapRequests.ts`/`join.ts`/`availability.ts`'s own REST handlers so `/api/voice/execute` could call the same logic directly with a session-derived actor id instead of re-inlining it a third time. **Call these shared functions, don't re-inline the logic again** — a third copy is exactly the mistake this extraction exists to prevent, and every future surface that needs to trigger one of these three mutations (a scheduled reminder, a manager-dashboard shortcut, a different channel entirely) should go through them. Two new `AuditAction` values exist as of this phase: `AVAILABILITY_MARKED` (written only by `/api/voice/execute` today — the REST `availability.ts` route still writes no audit row at all for its own POST/DELETE) and `SWAP_REQUESTED` (written by `/api/voice/execute`, alongside — not replacing — `swapRequests.ts`'s own separate audit write on its own POST). Both are real, additive, backward-compatible enum values; nothing reads or depends on their absence anywhere else in the codebase.
- Parked technical debt from Task 6's 2 adversarial fix-and-re-review rounds on `/api/voice/execute`, deliberately left unfixed by this phase's closing task (a verification/acceptance gate, not a new fix wave) — real, worth a follow-up, none blocking: (1) the voice-transcript `AuditLog` write on `APPROVE_SWAP`/`DECLINE_SWAP`/`APPROVE_JOIN`/`DECLINE_JOIN` is a second, separate `prisma.auditLog.create` call made *after* the shared action function's own transaction has already committed — not atomic with the mutation itself, so a crash between the two could leave a correctly-decided swap/join with no matching `[voice]`-tagged audit row (the underlying decision itself is still correctly recorded either way, by the action function's own audit write, just without the transcript). (2) Neither `/api/voice/transcribe` nor `/api/voice/parse-intent` has any rate limiting, despite both calling paid external Gemini endpoints behind only a session-gate. (3) Missing regression test coverage, named specifically rather than left implicit: `REQUEST_SWAP`'s shift-ownership check (a session user naming a shift they don't own — the manual live check confirmed the 404 behavior, but no automated test pins it), and `/transcribe`/`/parse-intent`'s `401` (no session) and `503` (`GEMINI_API_KEY` unset) paths.
- [x] **SCHEDULE PERSISTENCE ACROSS SESSIONS phase (2026-08-31).** Bug report: a published rota only displayed within the same browser session — reload, re-login, or a second device showed nothing. Investigated before building anything, per standing pattern.
  - **The reported cause was wrong, and the evidence is real, not assumed.** `GET /api/shifts/:locationId?weekStart=` (`server/src/routes/shifts.ts:54`) already returns every shift for that location/week — draft and published together, no status filter, no session gate — and `AppStateContext.tsx` already calls it unconditionally on every mount. Proved live: created and published a real shift for a future week via the real API, then queried `?weekStart=<that week>` — 1 shift, correctly `published`. Queried `?weekStart=<today's week>` (what a fresh mount always defaults to) — 0 shifts. Same data, two different weeks queried; nothing was ever lost.
  - **Actual root cause: `weekStart` (which week is being viewed) was plain `useState(currentWeekStart())` in `AppStateContext.tsx`, with zero persistence** — no URL param, no localStorage. `currentWeekStart()` (`src/engine/weekStart.ts`) is a pure function of today's real date. Every fresh mount — hard refresh, new login, new device — reset the view to "today's week." A manager building next week's rota (the realistic case) would see it vanish on reload, not because it was lost, but because the view silently pointed at a different, empty week.
  - **Confirmed with the user, not assumed, before building:** the draft-visibility model needed no change — a draft is already a real DB row the instant it's created (never buffered client-side), visible to any authenticated manager session at that location regardless of status, matching this product's shared-schedule, manager-assigns-for-others model.
  - **Fix: `src/routes/SchedulingRoute.tsx`** now syncs `weekStart` with a `?week=` URL param via `useSearchParams()` (same pattern as `JoinRoute.tsx`) — a mount-effect adopts an incoming `?week=` param into the shared context state; a second effect keeps the URL in sync with `weekStart` afterward (Prev/Next-week clicks, template application). A bare `/scheduling` visit with no param is untouched — still correctly defaults to `currentWeekStart()`. Deliberately scoped to `/scheduling` only, per explicit confirmation: `/schedule` (Shift Editor) has no week-navigation UI of its own and was left to just inherit whatever week Scheduling last set via the shared context, and a server-persisted "last viewed week" was explicitly rejected as solving a different, smaller problem than what was asked.
  - **Mandatory final whole-branch review caught a real race condition in the fix itself**, before it shipped: the mount-adoption effect and the URL-sync effect both fire in the same initial effects flush, in the same render's stale closure — the sync effect would read the pre-adoption `weekStart` and briefly overwrite a valid incoming `?week=` param with the old default, only self-correcting on a second render (and not guaranteed to, given `setSearchParams`'s async resolution). Fixed with a ref-gated skip on the sync effect's very first invocation — the mount effect alone owns reconciling the initial URL/state mismatch; the sync effect's job starts only once that's already settled. Also flagged by the same review, not fixed (real, pre-existing, out of this phase's scope): `VENUE_TYPES` duplicated verbatim between `server/src/routes/locations.ts` and `src/api/locations.ts` with no shared module; the `locationId !== req.user.locationId` check's continued lack of a shared helper (already known, already deferred last phase); and `swapRequests.ts` awaiting two independent Prisma queries sequentially instead of via `Promise.all` (pre-existing, not touched by this phase's diff).
  - **The same review also caught a real, separate bug in last phase's own work**, unrelated to schedule persistence: `/onboarding` was left out of the `RequireSession` sign-in gate on the reasoning "doesn't touch the four hardened routes" — wrong, since `/onboarding`'s venue step calls `/api/locations/:id`, which that same phase DID harden. An unauthenticated visitor hit a dead-end error with no path to sign in, unlike the other three gated routes which correctly redirect. Now gated; the flawed reasoning corrected in this file's own prior entry rather than left standing.
  - **Verified live, three ways, against the real running server + real DB + real sessions**, matching the bug report's own test plan exactly: built and published a real shift for a week two weeks out; (A) re-fetched with that week's URL param present (hard-refresh) — 1 shift, published; (B) revoked that session, logged back in fresh (log out and back in) — old token confirmed 401, same query with the new session — same 1 shift; (C) logged in a third independent time (a second session/device) — same 1 shift again. All three returned the byte-identical shift id. Separately confirmed querying today's week returns 0 for this same data — proving the fix's premise, not just its outcome. All test rows deleted and independently re-queried to zero.
  - **Found and fixed an unrelated, real, pre-existing test-suite race condition** while getting the full suite clean (a hard requirement for this phase): `voice.test.ts` (18 sites), `shifts.test.ts` (3), `floorPlan.test.ts` (1), and `persistShifts.test.ts` (1) all called `prisma.location.findFirst()` with no ordering — `node --test` runs test files concurrently against the same live Supabase database, and several files (`shifts.test.ts`, `floorPlan.test.ts`, `voice.test.ts` itself) also `create()`/`delete()` their own temporary "otherLocation" fixtures for cross-tenant tests. An unordered `findFirst()` could non-deterministically return another test's temporary location instead of the real seed one, and when that test's cleanup deleted it mid-run, any concurrently-running test holding a reference got a real FK-constraint violation or a false "seed data must exist" failure. Reproduced 3 times on the clean pre-fix baseline (different test failing each time, confirming a genuine race, not a fluke) before touching anything. Fixed by adding `orderBy: { createdAt: 'asc' }` to all 23 call sites — the real seed location is always the oldest row, so this is a correctness fix, not a workaround, and doesn't hardcode the `'seed-location'` id these files never assumed before. Re-ran `test:server` 5 times total after the fix (89/89 pass, 2 skipped by design, every time) — zero flakes.
  - **Verified, all clean:** `npm run typecheck`, `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint` (0 errors, same 3 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings), `npm test` (23/23), `npm run test:server` (89/89, 2 skipped by design, verified stable across 5 runs).
  - **Not verified — no browser tool exists in this environment:** the actual in-browser hard-refresh behavior (a real `location.reload()`, the real URL bar) and the bare-`/scheduling`-visit-defaults-to-today check were confirmed by API-level live testing and by static trace-through of the fixed effect logic (the race-condition fix specifically eliminates the only mechanism that could cause a wrong default to stick), not by a human or automated tool actually loading the page. Same standing gap as every other UI-facing phase in this file.
- [x] **CLOSED (2026-09-05) — Follow-up from the Actor Identity Enforcement + Sign-In Gate phase.** `JoinFlow.tsx`'s login success path always hard-navigated to `/my-shifts` regardless of why the user landed on `/join`. Fixed exactly as anticipated: `RequireSession` (`src/router.tsx`) now encodes the current `pathname+search` as a `returnTo` query param when redirecting an unauthenticated visit to `/join?mode=login`; `JoinRoute.tsx` reads it and passes it through; `JoinFlow.tsx`'s `handleVerify` (both its login and non-pending join-mode success branches) navigates there instead of the hardcoded `/my-shifts`, but only after validating it.
  - **`returnTo` is untrusted, attacker-craftable input (it arrives via a URL query param), so this is a real open-redirect surface, not just plumbing.** A first draft validated it with a blocklist (reject `//`, backslash, `://`) — a review pass caught that this class of check is fundamentally unsound: the WHATWG URL parser strips ASCII tab/CR/LF from anywhere in a URL before resolving it, so `/\t/evil.example` contains none of the blocked patterns as a raw string yet normalizes to `//evil.example` — a protocol-relative redirect to another host — the instant it's assigned to `window.location.href` (verified: `new URL('/\t/evil.example', 'https://x').host === 'evil.example'`). Replaced with the correct general fix for this vulnerability class: resolve `returnTo` against a fixed placeholder base (`new URL(path, 'https://internal.invalid')`) and require the resulting `.origin` to still equal that base — this asks "what will the browser's own URL parser actually resolve this to," using the identical parser that will process the string at real navigation time, so it can't be defeated by a parsing quirk a blocklist didn't anticipate. Covered by 7 new tests in the new `src/components/JoinFlow.test.ts` (also required widening `package.json`'s `test` script glob to include `src/components/*.test.ts`, previously only `src/engine/*.test.ts`) — including the exact tab/CR/LF case that broke the first draft, so it can't quietly regress.
  - **Verified**: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm test` (30/30, up from 23 — the 7 new tests) all clean. No frontend browser-automation tool exists in this environment (standing gap, named repeatedly elsewhere in this file), so the actual click-through (redirect → login → land back on the originating page) was hand-traced through the code logic for three cases — an unauth'd visit to `/scheduling` round-tripping back to `/scheduling` after login, a bare `/join?mode=login` with no `returnTo` still falling back to `/my-shifts` unchanged, and a malicious `returnTo` falling back to `/my-shifts` — not confirmed in an actual browser.
  - **New, minor, non-urgent notes from the same review pass, not acted on**: (1) `isSafeReturnTo` is a general-purpose, state-free open-redirect guard defined inside the `JoinFlow.tsx` page component rather than a shared `src/lib/` utility — fine today (no second consumer exists yet), worth moving if `SignupFlow.tsx` or another screen ever needs the same check. (2) A STAFF session redirected from a `managerOnly` page (e.g. `/people`) with no prior session takes one harmless extra redirect hop after login (lands on the `managerOnly` page first, gets bounced to `/my-shifts` by `RequireSession`'s existing STAFF-on-managerOnly check) instead of landing on `/my-shifts` directly — cosmetic only, not a bug, not fixed.
  - **A separate, unrelated, real, and more severe issue surfaced by this same review pass — flagged prominently, not fixed, not folded into this task:** see the new "SHIFTS.TS MUTATION ROUTES HAVE NO MANAGER GATE" entry immediately below.

(A) User stays scoped to exactly one `Location` (Option A1 — no schema change to that relationship; the many-to-many "restaurant group" model was explicitly rejected as out of scope for now). Follow-on from A1: `User.phone` should get a DB-level `@unique` (matching `email`) — the user confirmed **globally** unique (not per-Organization), reasoning that under A1 phone IS the login credential, not just a business rule. **Applied (`e3fe977`, 2026-08-31):** re-verified safe immediately before applying (23 users, 2 with phone set, 0 collisions), added as a nullable-safe unique index (`prisma/migrations/20260831063257_add_user_phone_unique`, hand-written + `migrate resolve --applied`, same drift-safe pattern as the SIGNUP migration below), then live-verified both directions against the real DB — a duplicate phone insert correctly throws `P2002`, two NULL-phone users still both succeed. Full suite re-run clean afterward (`test:server` 93/93, no regressions).
  - **New signup surface:** `POST /api/signup/request-otp` + `POST /api/signup/verify-otp` (`server/src/routes/signup.ts`, new `SIGNUP` `OtpPurpose` — hand-written migration `20260831000344_add_signup_otp_purpose`, reconciled via `prisma migrate resolve --applied` after `prisma migrate dev --create-only` produced no output against the drifted dev DB; same safe pattern used in prior phases). Verify-otp creates `Organization`+`Location`+`User(systemRole: OWNER)` in one transaction (409 if the phone already matches any existing active user, via `identity.ts`'s now-`export`ed, `select`-scoped `findPhoneMatches` — reused, not reimplemented), issues a real session, and hands off to `/signup` → `SignupFlow.tsx` → `useIdentity().login()` → `/onboarding`, reusing the existing 4-screen wizard verbatim against the real new `Location`. `/join` (join an EXISTING venue's roster) is deliberately untouched as a separate concept — both screens cross-link to each other for the wrong-flow case.
  - **Login became global-by-phone.** The original OTP identity system only knew "does this phone match an existing venue's roster" (location-scoped). Real multi-tenancy needs a third branch — "no Organization/Location matches this phone at all" → route to signup, not the pending-join flow — which is only well-defined if the existing-account check itself has no location scope. `identity.ts`'s `/request-otp`/`/verify-otp` and `src/api/identity.ts`'s `requestLoginOtp`/`verifyLoginOtp` had their `locationId` requirement removed entirely; this also fixed a regression the de-hardcoding sweep introduced (`RequireSession`'s `/join?mode=login` redirect has no venue to give a location-scoped login).
  - **De-hardcoding sweep** replaced every literal `'seed-location'` across frontend and backend with the real session-derived `locationId` (`AppStateContext.tsx` now exposes `locationId: string | null` as the one blessed source; `OnboardingRoute.tsx`, `FloorPlanRoute.tsx`, `PeopleRoute.tsx`, `RotaBuilder.tsx`, `Announcements.tsx`, `Shoutouts.tsx` all read from it or `useIdentity()` instead). Two guard shapes used consistently: read-effects clear their own state and return early on no `locationId`; write-actions throw inside the mutation wrapper, caught by the caller's existing try/catch. `router.tsx`'s `RequireSession` gained a `managerOnly` prop (redirects a valid STAFF session away from `/people`/`/onboarding` instead of letting them 403 page-wide).
  - **Mandatory final whole-branch review** (8 parallel angles) found and fixed: `swapRequests.ts`'s two sequential awaits → `Promise.all`; `IdentityContext`'s unmemoized context value re-rendering every consumer every render; `JoinFlow`'s mode-toggle dead-end (offering "Join" with no reachable `locationId`); `OnboardingWizard`'s stale roster→review handoff timer surviving a manual skip; `SchedulingRoute`'s two-effect week/URL sync collapsed into one (the two-effect version left the URL stale after in-app navigation away and back, capable of resurrecting the schedule-persistence bug from the phase before this one on a subsequent hard refresh).
  - **Live-verified end-to-end against the real running server + real Supabase DB, nothing mocked:** created "The Marina Grill" as a genuinely new, isolated Organization+Location+Owner via the real signup API with a real dev-OTP session; confirmed cross-tenant 403 isolation against `seed-location`'s data; confirmed the 409 duplicate-phone rejection with zero rows created; confirmed `seed-location`'s pre-existing 23 users and login are unaffected by any of this (it is now simply "Organization #1"). All test data deleted and independently re-queried to zero. Full suite clean and re-confirmed stable after the review's fix wave: `typecheck` (both), `build`, `lint` (0 errors, same pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings), `npm test` (23/23), `npm run test:server` (93/93, 2 skipped by design — run twice in this final round, byte-identical both times).
  - **⚠️ STANDING GAP surfaced by this phase, deliberately held rather than silently fixed — awaiting a product decision from the user (explained in full in-thread, not yet answered as of 2026-08-31):** `/` (Home — announcements/shoutouts/roster/approvals) and `/schedule` (Shift Editor) have never been behind `RequireSession`, on purpose, to serve a "walk up to the shared venue device, no personal login" use case (`router.tsx`'s own comments document this explicitly, and it matches AGENTS.md's product framing). Their backends (`shifts.ts`, `announcements.ts`, `shoutouts.ts`) were never part of the actor-identity hardening and remain genuinely unauthenticated. This phase's `AppStateContext.tsx` change — correctly deriving `locationId` from the session for every *signed-in* consumer — made the shared data-fetching both pages depend on uniformly conditional on that same session, so an anonymous visit to either page now renders blank (no announcements, no roster, no shift editor content) even though the backend would still serve the data if it had a `locationId` to ask for. Before multi-tenancy this never surfaced because "no session" and `'seed-location'` were accidentally interchangeable — there was only one venue, so the hardcoding WAS the kiosk-binding mechanism, invisibly. Multi-tenancy removes that accident: an anonymous device now has no way to say which of potentially-many venues it belongs to. **Three options presented to the user, none picked yet:** (1) require login even on the shared device (simplest, but kills the kiosk use-case, real UX regression from what the app already did); (2) an explicit venue-binding mechanism for anonymous devices — e.g. a bookmarkable `/?venue=<locationId>` kiosk URL persisted to `localStorage`/a long-lived cookie after first load — preserves the original UX but is new, unbuilt security surface (needs a real answer on whether a write-capable page like the Shift Editor should be reachable this way at all, not just a read-only one); (3) split the two routes — Home stays anonymous-friendly via (2), the Shift Editor (a write surface) requires login, on the reasoning that "glance at the board" and "mutate shift data with no auth" are not the same risk. Not resolved in this phase — flagged per the user's own explicit "hold on genuinely hard-to-reverse forks" instruction rather than picked.
  - **Follow-up tech debt, named explicitly (not just noted in a chat transcript) — 3 real duplication findings the mandatory review surfaced, not fixed in this phase (all pre-existing patterns this phase extended rather than introduced, all cheap to fix, none blocking):**
    - **`VENUE_TYPES` duplicated verbatim** between `src/api/locations.ts:12` and `server/src/routes/locations.ts:13` — the exact same literal array (venue-type category strings) hand-copied in both places with no shared source of truth. A venue type added/renamed on one side silently drifts from the other until someone notices. First flagged during the Schedule Persistence phase, still unfixed, `signup.ts:89` now references it in a comment without importing it — a third de-facto dependent on the two copies staying in sync. Fix: define once in a shared location both `src/` and `server/src/` can import (or generate one from the other), not a new independent effort.
    - **The `locationId !== req.user!.locationId` cross-tenant ownership check is hand-repeated ~16 times** across `floorPlan.ts` (10 sites), `join.ts` (2), `staffDirectory.ts` (2), and `swapRequests.ts` (2), with no shared helper — first flagged during the Schedule Persistence phase, still unfixed. Every call site is currently correct, but each one is a spot where a future edit could typo/omit the check with no compiler or test catching a missing tenant-isolation guard specifically (only a live cross-tenant test would). This phase's own live verification exercised this exact check (confirmed a real 403 reading across Organizations) — it works today, but a shared `assertOwnsLocation(req, locationId)` (or equivalent middleware) would make "every mutation route checks tenant ownership" a structural guarantee instead of a convention 16 places have to individually remember.
    - **`prisma.auditLog.create({...})` is hand-inlined at 18+ call sites** across `joinActions.ts`, `swapActions.ts`, `eightySix.ts`, `floorPlan.ts`, `signup.ts`, `swapRequests.ts`, `staffDirectory.ts`, `shifts.ts`, and `voice.ts` — each site re-specifies the same shape (`action`, `entityType`, `entityId`, `actorId`, `note`, sometimes `locationId`) with no shared writer function. `signup.ts`'s own audit row reuses the `STAFF_CREATED` action for lack of a dedicated `ORGANIZATION_CREATED`/`SIGNUP` enum value — a judgment call made explicitly in this phase's own code comment, not hidden. Newly surfaced by this phase (the review looked at `signup.ts`'s audit write and traced the pattern outward), not previously ledgered. A shared `writeAuditLog(tx, {...})` helper would remove the copy-paste risk (a field silently omitted at one of 18 sites) and would be the natural place to introduce that missing enum value if `AuditAction` is ever extended for a real organization-lifecycle event.
- [x] **KIOSK-ACCESS FORK RESOLVED (2026-08-31).** The standing gap two phases above (`/` and `/schedule` both anonymous, `AppStateContext.tsx`'s session-derived `locationId` leaving both blank post-multi-tenancy) was decided by the user, not guessed at: **Option 3** — split the two routes by risk, not treat them identically.
  - **This is a deliberate behavior change, not a bug fix, and the user asked it be recorded as such.** Pre-multi-tenancy, `/schedule` (Shift Editor) allowed fully anonymous shift mutation by design — there was only ever one venue, so "no session" and `'seed-location'` were accidentally interchangeable, and the missing-session gap was invisible. Post-multi-tenancy that stops being acceptable for two independent reasons: (1) "no session" no longer disambiguates *which* venue an anonymous write is even for, and (2) unattributed shift mutation directly conflicts with the audit-trail differentiator this product is built around (see `AGENTS.md` §5). `/` (Home) keeps the old anonymous-friendly behavior on purpose — it's a read-mostly glance board, not a write surface, and the "walk up to the shared venue device" use case is real and worth preserving there.
  - **Home's venue-binding mechanism** (`src/api/venueBinding.ts`): a bookmarkable `?venue=<locationId>` URL param, adopted into `localStorage` (`shiftsync.anonymousVenueId`) on first load and read on every later bare `/` visit — chosen over a cookie because it's the more consistent extension of two patterns already established in this codebase: URL-param-driven state (`SchedulingRoute.tsx`'s `?week=`, `JoinRoute.tsx`'s `?mode=`) and localStorage-backed persistence (`identity.ts`'s own session store uses the same mechanism, just for a real login instead of an anonymous venue). `HomeRoute.tsx` owns the adoption effect; `AppStateContext.tsx`'s `locationId` is now `session?.user.locationId ?? anonymousVenueId` — a real session always wins, by construction, so a bookmarked kiosk link opened in a browser where a manager is also logged in can never override their own venue (live-verified below, not just reasoned about).
  - **`/schedule` now requires the same phone+OTP session every other manager-dashboard route already does** (`src/router.tsx`'s `RequireSession`, no `managerOnly` — matches `/scheduling`'s existing staff-can-view/manager-sub-actions posture, since the user asked for a session requirement, not a new role rule). Client-side gating alone would have been security theater against a direct API caller, so this phase also retrofitted `server/src/routes/shifts.ts` — previously the **only** mutation-route file in this codebase with zero session check at all, not one of the four the earlier Actor Identity Enforcement phase hardened — to the same `requireSession` + session-derived-actor pattern as `swapRequests.ts`/`join.ts`/`staffDirectory.ts`/`floorPlan.ts`: `locationId` for `POST /`/`POST /bulk` now comes from `req.user!.locationId`, never the body; `createdById`/`actorId`/`publishedById` follow the exact `swapRequests.ts` on-behalf-of rule (`req.user!.systemRole === 'STAFF' ? req.user!.id : (body value) || req.user!.id`) so a manager's "Viewing" dropdown still works but a STAFF session can't spoof another actor; `PATCH`/`DELETE /:id` resolve the existing shift first and 404 (not 403) on a cross-tenant id, matching this codebase's established leak-avoidance convention — this phase's own first draft got that wrong (used a separate 403) and self-caught it before shipping, not in review. GET routes (`/:locationId`, `/:locationId/publish-status`) deliberately stayed open — Home's anonymous glance board depends on them.
  - **A related, real contradiction found and fixed while wiring this up**: `Announcements.tsx`/`Shoutouts.tsx` (both render on the now-anonymous-capable Home) had a save-guard that checked `!locationId`, not `!session`, while their own error copy already said "You must be signed in to post an announcement/give a shoutout." Pre-fork this was harmless (locationId implied session, always). Post-fork it would have silently let an anonymous kiosk visitor post an announcement/shoutout — contradicting both the error copy's own promise and the "no mutation on Home" framing this decision was made under. Both guards now check `session` (from `useIdentity()`) instead; the read-fetch stays gated on `locationId` so the anonymous glance-board view is unaffected. **Their backend routes (`announcements.ts`, `shoutouts.ts`) remain genuinely unauthenticated server-side**, same as `shifts.ts` was before this phase — the UI fix closes the normal path, not a direct API call. Flagged, not fixed: hardening those two route files was judged a separate, larger scope decision from the one the user actually asked for here, not folded in silently.

Whether that's intentional (kitchen kiosk device, same reasoning as pre-fork `/schedule`) or an oversight was never asked, so it wasn't guessed at or touched.

    - **The cross-tenant ownership check**: two new helpers in `server/src/middleware/requireSession.ts`, alongside `requireSession`/`requireManager` — `assertOwnsLocation(req, res, locationId)` for the ~11 sites where the id being checked comes straight from the request (URL param/body, no lookup ambiguity, 403 is the correct response), and `ownedOrNotFound(req, res, entity, notFoundMessage)` for the ~10 sites where an entity was looked up by id and a cross-tenant id must get the *same* 404 as a nonexistent one (deliberately not 403, so a cross-tenant caller can't distinguish "not yours" from "doesn't exist" — `ownedOrNotFound` is a TS type predicate, so a passing call narrows the entity to non-null for the rest of the handler, same as the `if (!existing) ...` checks it replaces). Applied across `locations.ts`, `staffDirectory.ts`, `swapRequests.ts`, `join.ts`, `floorPlan.ts` (all 10 of its sites), and the `shifts.ts` sites this phase added — every site that used to hand-write `!== req.user!.locationId` now goes through one of these two functions; `grep` confirms zero raw comparisons remain in `server/src/routes/`.
    - **`prisma.auditLog.create`**: a new `writeAuditLog(client, entry)` in `server/src/lib/auditLog.ts`, typed to accept either the top-level `prisma` client or a `tx` from inside `prisma.$transaction(...)` (so `signup.ts`'s and `swapActions.ts`'s atomic writes still commit atomically) — replaces all 20 hand-inlined call sites across `shifts.ts`, `floorPlan.ts` (4), `staffDirectory.ts` (2), `swapRequests.ts`, `signup.ts`, `voice.ts` (4), `eightySix.ts` (2), `joinActions.ts` (2), and `swapActions.ts`; `grep` confirms zero raw `.auditLog.create` calls remain outside the helper itself.

On Tasks 2 and 4, the controller independently re-queried the database after every "cleanup: done" claim and twice found real leaked rows (6 `RotaTemplate` + 14 `AuditLog` rows on Task 2; 4 `AuditLog` rows + 4 orphaned PDF files on Task 4) and a dangling dev server still listening on both ports — cleaned up directly by the controller each time rather than spending a further fix round on pure hygiene. **The lesson generalizes: an implementer's own "verified"/"cleaned up" claim is not evidence — independently re-querying the database and re-checking listening ports after every task is what actually caught these, not reading the report.**

    3. **The on-behalf-of target itself was never tenancy-validated in 4 of the 6 new sites** (`rotaTemplates.ts` create + apply, `schedules.ts` confirm, `policyDocuments.ts` upload) — only `attendance.ts` did this correctly from the start. A MANAGER/OWNER session could stamp `createdById`/`uploadedById` with a different venue's user id (or garbage, risking a raw FK-violation 500). Fixed at all 4 sites with the same `ownedOrNotFound`-on-the-resolved-target pattern `attendance.ts` already used, skipping the extra query in the common self-service case. **Same gap independently exists in `shifts.ts`'s POST `/`, from an earlier phase, out of this plan's scope — flagged by the reviewer as a fast-follow, not fixed here.**

  - **`server/src/parsing/`: confirmed clean by construction, not by inspection.** Grepped every file in the directory for `app.listen`/`createServer`/`express()`/any server-instantiation call — zero matches. Every file in this directory (parsers, extractors, normalizers — `parseWorkbook.ts`, `pdfTableExtractor.ts`, `deterministicGridParser.ts`, etc.) is a plain function module with no HTTP surface of its own; the only way any of them run is by being imported and called from inside an already-`requireSession`-gated route handler (`schedules.ts`). There is structurally nothing here for an anonymous-read sweep to find — not "didn't find anything," but "there is no listener to check."
  - **`server/docling-sidecar/` (the separate local FastAPI process): confirmed to have zero endpoints requiring `requireSession`-style auth, but for architectural reasons, not because it was overlooked.** Read `service.py` directly: exactly two endpoints exist, `GET /health` (returns `{"status": "ok"}`, no data) and `POST /convert` (accepts an uploaded PDF, returns extracted table structure — grid of cell text — and nothing else). Neither has any auth check. But independently verified, not just quoted from the file's own docstring: the actual startup command in `package.json`'s `docling:sidecar` script passes `--host 127.0.0.1` to `uvicorn` — confirmed as the literal, only way this process is ever started (no Dockerfile, no alternate launch script, no other `uvicorn`/host-binding reference anywhere in the sidecar's own `.py` source). Loopback-only binding means this process is unreachable from any network caller, full stop — not "unauthenticated but hard to find," genuinely inaccessible except from a process already running on the same machine. And even granting local access, there's nothing to leak: `/convert` is stateless (no database connection, no file storage of its own — `requirements.txt` confirms only `docling`/`fastapi`/`uvicorn`/`python-multipart`, nothing DB- or auth-related), so a local caller could only submit their own PDF and get its structure back, never retrieve another venue's previously-uploaded data. This is a categorically different risk shape than every HTTP route this session's sweeps covered (network-reachable, backed by the shared database) — `requireSession` wouldn't even make architectural sense here (the sidecar has no concept of ShiftSync's session/user model), and there is nothing session-gating would protect that isn't already protected by not being reachable at all.
