- [x] **AUDIT-LOG WRITE ATOMICITY (2026-09-04).** Prior to this, `writeAuditLog(prisma, {...})` ran as its own independent statement after its triggering mutation at every call site — a crash, dropped connection, or thrown error between the two could leave a real mutation committed with no audit row, a real gap for a product whose stated differentiator is a MOHRE-compliance audit trail. Every call site across `server/src/routes/{staffDirectory,shifts,rotaTemplates,attendance,floorPlan,policyDocuments,schedules,eightySix,voice,swapRequests}.ts` and `server/src/lib/actions/{swapActions,joinActions,availabilityActions}.ts` now wraps its mutation(s) + `writeAuditLog(tx, ...)` in one `prisma.$transaction(async (tx) => {...})`, tightly scoped around just the write pair, not the whole handler. Two call sites needed a signature widening to accept `tx`, not just `prisma`: `persistShifts` (`server/src/parsing/persistShifts.ts`, used by `schedules.ts`'s upload-confirm) and `markAvailability`/`createSwapRequest` (used by both `voice.ts` and their own REST routes) — each had an internal array-form `$transaction` of its own, which can't nest inside an outer one, so those became `Promise.all` (still atomic as a set, now inside the caller's transaction instead of their own). `swapActions.ts`'s `decideSwapRequest` was already correctly atomic and untouched. `voice.ts`'s second, transcript-only audit row on APPROVE_SWAP/DECLINE_SWAP/APPROVE_JOIN/DECLINE_JOIN has no mutation of its own to pair with (the real mutation already committed atomically inside `decideSwapRequest`/`decideJoinRequest`) — left as a bare write, correctly. `signup.ts`/`onboarding.ts`/`join.ts` were out of scope (parallel onboarding-UX work in progress) and not touched; `signup.ts` was independently confirmed to already use `tx` correctly, `onboarding.ts` calls no audit write at all, and `join.ts`'s own call into `joinActions.ts` benefits from that file's fix without `join.ts` itself changing. Verified: `npm run server:typecheck` clean, `npm run test:server` 94/94 passing (2 pre-existing skips, unrelated).
  - **New named follow-up, deliberately not acted on — on-behalf-of actor ids are trusted without a tenancy check.** Surfaced by this phase's own mandatory whole-branch review, not asked about, not fixed: the established "on-behalf-of" convention (documented above at the `eightySix.ts`/`shifts.ts` hardening phases) lets a MANAGER/OWNER session supply an arbitrary `actorId`/`createdById`/`publishedById`/`backOnById` in the request body — this correctly stops a STAFF session from spoofing another actor, but nothing validates that a manager-supplied id actually resolves to a real user in *that manager's own venue*. A garbage id hits a raw Prisma FK-violation 500 instead of this codebase's normal clean-404; a real cross-tenant user's id would misattribute the row to a stranger. Confirmed present in `eightySix.ts` (`POST /`, `PATCH /:itemId/back-on`) and `shifts.ts` (`PATCH /:id`, `DELETE /:id`, `POST /bulk`, `POST /:locationId/publish`) — the same ~11-site copy-pasted ternary this review also flagged as unconsolidated. This is a threat-model question (how much a manager session should be trusted against their own venue's other users) as much as a code fix, so left named rather than silently patched mid-scope.
- [x] **VOICE ROUTE RATE LIMITING (2026-09-04).** `POST /api/voice/transcribe` (audio upload + transcription) and `POST /api/voice/parse-intent` (Gemini intent parsing) — the two voice routes that call a paid external AI provider — are now rate-limited per authenticated session (`req.user!.id` via `express-rate-limit`'s `keyGenerator`, not raw IP, so one abusive session on a shared venue device/IP can't lock out the rest of that venue). New `server/src/middleware/rateLimit.ts`: `transcribeRateLimiter` (20 req/5min, the costlier audio+provider call) and `parseIntentRateLimiter` (30 req/5min, cheaper text-only call), both `skipFailedRequests: true` so a genuine provider outage or bad input doesn't burn a retrying user's budget, both responding 429 with this codebase's own `{ error: '...' }` shape rather than the library default. `POST /api/voice/execute` (applies an already-parsed intent to the DB) makes no external AI call and was deliberately left out of this pass — narrower than the user's own "Whisper/Gemini voice routes" framing. Covered by a new `server/src/middleware/rateLimit.test.ts` proving the threshold triggers a real 429 for one user while a second user's session on the same limiter is unaffected. `express-rate-limit` (already present in `node_modules`/`package-lock.json` as a transitive `devOptional` dep of unrelated tooling) was promoted to a real, declared `package.json` dependency — a one-line, alphabetically-placed insertion, verified not to touch the parallel onboarding-UX session's own uncommitted `package.json` additions (`@react-three/fiber`, `@shadergradient/react`, `three`, `@playwright/test`). The `npm install` needed to regenerate `package-lock.json` for that one dependency also wrote in the lockfile's already-overdue resolution entries for those same four onboarding-UX packages (they were already declared in `package.json` and already installed on disk days earlier — confirmed via `node_modules` timestamps and a second idempotent `npm install` producing a byte-identical diff — so this closed a pre-existing package.json/package-lock.json drift rather than introducing one), which is why this phase's `package.json`/`package-lock.json` commit necessarily carries both changes together — a lockfile can't be committed in a state inconsistent with its `package.json`, so partially staging just the rate-limiting line was not a safe option once express-rate-limit needed promoting to a real dependency.
  - **New named follow-ups, deliberately not acted on — surfaced by this phase's own mandatory whole-branch review:** (1) both limiters use `express-rate-limit`'s default in-memory store, so the stated budgets only hold for a single Node process — if this ever runs as more than one instance/replica, a client's effective allowance multiplies with the instance count; revisit with a shared store (e.g. Redis) before any multi-instance deployment. (2) both use the library's default fixed-window algorithm, which allows up to ~2x the stated limit for requests clustered across a window boundary (e.g. 20 at the end of one window + 20 at the start of the next) — acceptable for now given the actual threat model (cost containment against casual abuse, not a hard security boundary), but named so it isn't mistaken for a stronger guarantee than it is.
- [x] **FINAL WHOLE-BRANCH REVIEW OF THE THREE PHASES ABOVE (2026-09-04) — 8-angle multi-agent pass over the combined diff, run as the mandatory closing step before handing the branch back for the user's own review.** Four real issues fixed, all in files this branch had already touched:
  - **`persistShifts.ts` and `rotaTemplates.ts`'s `/:id/apply`**: the audit-atomicity phase had replaced Prisma's safe array-form `$transaction([...])` batch-create with `Promise.all(rows.map(tx.create))` run against the outer interactive transaction's `tx` client — an interactive-transaction client is bound to a single reserved DB connection, so firing creates concurrently against it doesn't parallelize and risks tripping the transaction's own timeout on a large roster upload or a big template apply (a real regression the old batch form didn't carry). Both converted to a plain sequential loop with `await` inside the `tx` callback — still one atomic transaction, no concurrency-on-one-connection anti-pattern. Live-verified against the real server + real DB after the fix: applying a 3-entry template still produces exactly 3 real `Shift` rows and exactly 1 `AuditLog` row, atomically.
  - **`rateLimit.ts`**: the two per-route limiters (`transcribeRateLimiter`/`parseIntentRateLimiter`) were verbatim-identical except for `limit` — collapsed into one `makeVoiceLimiter(limit)` factory so the five shared fields can't drift out of sync between the two routes.
  - **`staffDirectory.ts`**: `POST`/`PATCH` were passing a hardcoded `toDto(user, false)` literal instead of deriving `redactPersonal` from the caller's role — harmless today only because both routes are already `requireManager`-gated, but a landmine: if either route ever became STAFF-reachable, the hardcoded `false` would silently start leaking the four redacted fields again, reintroducing the exact bug the over-fetch sweep fixed. All three call sites (`GET`/`POST`/`PATCH`) now go through one `redactPersonalFor(req)` helper instead of a literal or a re-derived expression.
  - **A bug the fix for the above item itself introduced, caught by this phase's own live verification, not by review**: the first draft of `sessionKey`'s defensive `req.user` fallback used raw `req.ip` directly. Running the live rota-template-apply check surfaced `express-rate-limit`'s own `ERR_ERL_KEY_GEN_IPV6` validation warning — a raw IP string in a `keyGenerator` lets an IPv6 client bypass the limit by rotating the low bits of its address. Fixed by routing the fallback through the library's own `ipKeyGenerator` helper instead of `req.ip` directly, then re-ran the live check to confirm the warning is gone and the endpoint still works.
  - **A candidate fix considered and deliberately reverted**: narrowing `schedules.ts`'s upload-confirm duplicate-import race by moving `uploadCache.delete(batchId)` to run *inside* the transaction (right after `persistShifts`, before the audit write) looked like it would restore the exact pre-atomicity race-window width. Rejected on inspection: `uploadCache.delete` is a plain in-memory JS side effect, not part of the DB transaction — if the audit write then failed and the transaction rolled back, the shift creates would be undone but the cache entry would stay permanently deleted, making the batch unretryable with nothing actually persisted. Left as originally written (delete only after the transaction commits), which keeps a narrow but pre-existing duplicate-confirm race rather than trading it for a worse, newly-possible failure mode.
  - **New named follow-ups, deliberately not acted on** (real, but each either pre-existing and unrelated to what this branch's three tasks were asked to do, or a genuine scope-expansion decision for the user to make, not this session):
    - **TOCTOU races on three separate mutation guards, all pre-existing and unrelated to (not introduced or worsened by) the atomicity work**: `joinActions.ts`'s `decideJoinRequest` reads `existing.status !== 'PENDING'` before either its approve or decline `$transaction`, with nothing re-checking status inside the transaction — two concurrent decisions on the same request (double-approve, or an approve/decline race) can both pass the guard. `attendance.ts`'s clock-in reads its "already clocked in" guard before its `$transaction`, so two near-simultaneous clock-ins for the same user can both pass and both create an open `AttendanceLog`. `staffDirectory.ts`'s `PATCH` computes `terminatedAt` from a plain pre-transaction `findUnique` of `existing.isActive`, so two concurrent opposite-direction toggles can leave the isActive/terminatedAt pair — which the code's own comment says "must... stay in lockstep" — inconsistent. `swapActions.ts`'s `decideSwapRequest` already re-guards correctly inside its transaction and is the pattern to follow if/when these three are addressed.
    - **`server/src/routes/availability.ts` (the REST path for marking availability) still calls `markAvailability` with no audit-log write at all**, while `voice.ts`'s call site for the identical mutation now atomically writes one — a REST/voice parity gap for the same action, pre-existing (the REST route never audited this), left alone because adding a wholly new audit write is new logging behavior, not an atomicity fix to already-existing logging.
    - **No shared `withAuditedTransaction`/`runAuditedMutation(fn, entryFn)` helper exists.** The `prisma.$transaction(async (tx) => { ...mutate...; await writeAuditLog(tx, {...}); return x; })` shape was hand-written at ~13 call sites across this branch's files rather than factored into one wrapper — exactly the copy-paste-drift risk this file already named for the pre-`writeAuditLog`-helper era of `prisma.auditLog.create`. A shared wrapper (accepting an arbitrary mutate callback, since `swapActions.ts`'s transaction has real extra guard logic beyond the simple shape) would remove it. **Update (2026-09-05, from the TOCTOU-fix pass below): the SAME duplication now also applies one level up** — the optimistic-concurrency guard shape itself (a dedicated `Error` subclass thrown inside the transaction on a conditional `updateMany`'s `count === 0`, caught outside, translated to a conflict response) is hand-copied 4 times (`swapActions.ts`, `joinActions.ts` x2, `staffDirectory.ts`), a real pattern worth factoring alongside the plain audited-transaction wrapper if/when this is picked up.
    - **`server/src/routes/schedules.ts`'s `POST /upload`** (image/PDF roster parsing via `parsing/parseVision.ts`) calls Gemini directly and carries zero rate limiting — a third AI-cost-bearing route sitting right next to the two voice routes just rate-limited, with the identical risk shape. Out of scope here because the task was specifically "the Whisper/Gemini voice routes," not every Gemini-calling route in the codebase — named so it isn't mistaken for already covered.

- [x] **TOCTOU RACE FIXES, ITEM 1/4 OF THE 2026-09-04 FINAL-REVIEW FOLLOW-UPS (2026-09-05) — the three named "TOCTOU races on three separate mutation guards" closed, using `swapActions.ts`'s `decideSwapRequest` pattern as the template: a conditional `updateMany` re-asserting the exact prior state inside the transaction, `count === 0` → throw a dedicated error class → transaction rolls back everything else it was about to write → caught outside and translated to a clean conflict response.**
  - **`joinActions.ts`'s `decideJoinRequest`**: both the decline and approve transactions now use `tx.joinRequest.updateMany({ where: { id, status: 'PENDING' }, data: {...} })` instead of a plain `update`, throwing a new `JoinRequestAlreadyReviewedError` on `count === 0` — on the approve path this rolls back the just-created `User` row too, so a losing approval never leaves an orphan staff member with no matching approved request. Caught outside `$transaction` and translated into the function's own pre-existing `{ result: 'already_reviewed' }` return variant — no new result shape invented, so neither call site (`routes/join.ts`'s PATCH, `routes/voice.ts`'s APPROVE_JOIN/DECLINE_JOIN branch) needed any change; both already handled `already_reviewed` as a 409. No `findUnique` re-fetch was needed after the guard (unlike the task's own suggestion) — nothing read from the request after the guard depends on a value that could have changed (fullName/locationId are invariant across the race). **Guarantee: airtight** — the DB-level atomic `updateMany` makes a double-approve/approve-decline race structurally impossible to both succeed. Regression tests: `server/src/lib/actions/joinActions.test.ts` (2 tests — approve-then-approve, approve-then-decline — each decides once for real, then replays the original PENDING-based decision and confirms `already_reviewed` with exactly one `User` row created). Live-verified against the real running server + real DB: approved a real PENDING `JoinRequest` via `PATCH /api/join/:requestId`, then called it again — first call 200 (real `User` created), second call a clean 409 (`"This request has already been reviewed."`), exactly one `User` row confirmed via a direct Postgres query. Verification script written to a temporary `server/src/__livecheck_join.mjs`, run once, then deleted — not committed.
  - **`attendance.ts`'s `POST /clock-in`**: took the **fallback (app-level), not the schema-migration path** — the "already clocked in" `findFirst` guard moved from a plain pre-transaction `prisma` read to a `tx`-scoped read as the first statement inside the `$transaction`, immediately before the `create`, throwing a new `AlreadyClockedInError` on a hit (caught outside, translated to the pre-existing 409). Reasoning for not adding the DB-level partial unique index (`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... ON attendance_logs (user_id) WHERE clock_out_at IS NULL`): this repo's Postgres is a shared Supabase dev DB with other agents' work potentially in flight in the same session (per this session's own multi-agent context), and the task's own instructions were explicit — don't run a live migration against a shared DB unless confident it's safe; a `prisma migrate dev` run risks a drift-triggered reset prompt or lock contention against concurrent in-flight test runs, for a guarantee this fallback already substantially narrows. **Guarantee: narrowed, NOT airtight.** This is a CREATE (a new row), not an UPDATE of an existing row, so there is no existing row to key a conditional `WHERE` on the way the other two sites do — the race window shrinks from spanning the whole HTTP request down to two round trips inside one transaction, but under Postgres's default READ COMMITTED isolation, two genuinely concurrent transactions can still both run the `findFirst` and both see "no open log" before either commits its `create`. A real double-open remains possible under true simultaneous requests. Closing it fully needs the schema migration named above — left as a follow-up, not attempted here. Regression test: `server/src/routes/attendance.test.ts` (clocks in once for real, then replays the call with the original "no open log" expectation and confirms a clean 409 with exactly one open `AttendanceLog` row persisted) — this exercises the guard's logic correctly but, being sequential rather than genuinely concurrent, does not itself prove the residual race is closed (it isn't).
  - **`staffDirectory.ts`'s `PATCH /:userId`**: `tx.user.update` replaced with `tx.user.updateMany({ where: { id: userId, isActive: existing.isActive }, data })` — re-asserting the exact `isActive` value `data.terminatedAt` was computed from — throwing a new `StaffRecordChangedConcurrentlyError` on `count === 0`, caught outside and translated to a new, clean `409 { error: 'This staff record was changed by someone else — please refresh and try again.' }` (this route had no pre-existing conflict-response shape to reuse, unlike the join/swap sites). A `tx.user.findUnique` re-fetch (same `include` as the old `update`) was added after a successful `updateMany`, since `updateMany` doesn't return the row and the handler needs it for `toDto`. **Guarantee: airtight** for the isActive/terminatedAt-lockstep invariant specifically — a concurrent opposite-direction toggle can no longer land. **Testing note, worth recording**: this site could NOT be exercised the same "decide once, replay the stale call" way as the other two, because the route always re-reads `existing` fresh immediately before its own write — a second full HTTP PATCH just observes the new state and succeeds again harmlessly, it never replays staleness. A first attempt at proving this via two genuinely concurrent HTTP requests (`Promise.all` of two `fetch`es) was tried and **empirically failed to race at all** in this environment — both got 200, because this app's shared `prisma` client serializes interactive transactions against this DB (small connection pool / pooled-connection behavior) rather than letting them genuinely overlap, so the second request's own pre-transaction read only ever ran after the first had already committed. Replaced with a test that does one real PATCH through the live server, then directly issues the exact same guarded `updateMany` shape the route uses, scoped to the now-superseded snapshot, and confirms it matches zero rows — proving the literal mechanism without depending on unreliable real-world thread timing. Logged here so a future session doesn't re-attempt the `Promise.all` approach expecting it to work.
  - **Files touched**: `server/src/lib/actions/joinActions.ts`, `server/src/routes/attendance.ts`, `server/src/routes/staffDirectory.ts` (all three fixes) + three new/extended test files (`server/src/lib/actions/joinActions.test.ts` new, `server/src/routes/attendance.test.ts` new, `server/src/routes/staffDirectory.test.ts` extended with one new test). `routes/join.ts` and `routes/voice.ts` were checked but needed NO changes — both already handled `already_reviewed` correctly.
  - **Verified**: `npm run server:typecheck` clean; `npm run test:server` 103/103 passing (2 pre-existing skips, unrelated) — includes re-running `voice.ts`'s full APPROVE_JOIN/DECLINE_JOIN suite to confirm the `decideJoinRequest` return-shape change didn't ripple into the intent executor (it didn't; shape is unchanged).
  - **Remaining 2/4 deferred follow-ups from the 2026-09-04 final review, at the time this entry was written** (the `availability.ts` REST/voice audit-log parity gap named alongside these was closed separately, by a different task running concurrently with this one — see the AVAILABILITY REST/VOICE AUDIT PARITY entry above): the missing shared `withAuditedTransaction` helper — **closed 2026-09-05, see the `withAuditedTransaction` SHARED HELPER entry near the end of this file** — and `schedules.ts`'s unrate-limited `POST /upload`.
  - **A real overreach in this entry's own `staffDirectory.ts` fix, caught by the orchestrating session's review, not by this task's own verification**: the guarded `updateMany`'s `where` unconditionally included `isActive: existing.isActive`, even for a PATCH that never touches `isActive` at all (e.g. a plain phone-number edit). That would have made an entirely unrelated field update spuriously fail with the new 409 any time someone else happened to toggle `isActive` around the same time — a real usability regression this "fix" would have introduced, not a data-integrity bug, but a genuine one. Corrected to only include `isActive` in the `where` when `data.isActive !== undefined` (i.e. only when this specific request is itself the one computing `terminatedAt` from it). Covered by a new test confirming a fullName-only PATCH succeeds and leaves `isActive` untouched regardless of its current value.

- [x] **ATTENDANCE.TS CLOCK-IN RACE — MIGRATION COMPLETED (2026-09-04), CLOSING THE DEFERRED FOLLOW-UP NAMED IN THE TOCTOU-FIX ENTRY ABOVE.** The app-level `findFirst`-inside-transaction guard only ever narrowed this race (see that entry); this phase closed it for real with a DB-level partial unique index, following the exact safety-gate order the user specified (checks BEFORE any schema change, no `--force`, no reset risk against the shared, pool-strained dev DB).
  - **Pre-migration safety checks, both clean, both actually run (not just asserted):** (1) data-violation check — a throwaway script using `prisma.$queryRawUnsafe('SELECT user_id, count(*) ... GROUP BY user_id HAVING count(*) > 1')` against `attendance_logs` returned zero rows (no pre-existing double-open logs to block the index); script wrote to repo root (`__check_violations.mjs`, needed real `@prisma/client` resolution), run once, deleted immediately after.   - **Migration**: `prisma/migrations/20260904152729_attendance_one_open_clock_in_per_user/migration.sql`, hand-written (Prisma's schema DSL can't express a partial/conditional unique index):
    ```sql
    CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "attendance_logs_one_open_per_user" ON "attendance_logs" ("user_id") WHERE "clock_out_at" IS NULL;
    ```
    Table/column names confirmed from `AttendanceLog`'s actual `@map`/`@@map` directives in `schema.prisma`, not assumed. Scaffolded via `--create-only`, hand-edited, applied via a plain `npx prisma migrate dev` (no flags) — applied cleanly, no prompts, no drift/reset warning of any kind. A short comment was added directly above the `AttendanceLog` model in `schema.prisma` explaining this index exists via raw migration SQL and is intentionally not representable as an `@@unique` there.
  - **`prisma generate`'s trailing step hit a Windows file-lock (`EPERM` renaming `query_engine-windows.dll.node`)** — caused by other node processes already running in this shared session (confirmed via `tasklist`, ~15 node.exe processes), not by anything this migration did. Did NOT kill any other process to force it through, since the raw index isn't part of the generated Prisma Client's TS type surface anyway (no model field changed) — confirmed clean by running `npm run server:typecheck` immediately after, with no fresh `generate`, and it passed with no errors either way.
  - **Code change (`server/src/routes/attendance.ts`'s `POST /clock-in`)**: kept the existing `tx`-scoped `findFirst` guard as a fast-path pre-check (still gives the clean 409 for the ordinary sequential case without a wasted insert attempt), and added the real backstop — the `.catch` after the transaction now also matches `err instanceof Prisma.PrismaClientKnownRequestError && err.code === 'P2002'` and translates it to the same 409 `{ error: 'Already clocked in — clock out first.' }`, so a genuine DB-level unique-violation from the new index never bubbles up as a raw 500. The "narrowed, NOT airtight" comment above this block was rewritten to state the race is now CLOSED and explain the two-tier mechanism (DB index = real enforcement, `findFirst` = friendly pre-check only).
  - **Testing — both concurrency proof types attempted, transparently reported which one actually materialized:**
    - New test: `POST /api/attendance/clock-in — genuinely concurrent double clock-in leaves exactly one open AttendanceLog` fires two real `Promise.all`-concurrent `fetch`es against a running server for the same user. **Result, confirmed by temporary instrumentation during development (removed before finishing): 5/5 manual runs resolved via the ordinary fast-path `findFirst` guard (409), never via P2002** — this shared Postgres pool serializes interactive transactions on this route too, matching the exact prior finding on `staffDirectory.ts`'s PATCH named in the TOCTOU-fix entry above. Real HTTP-level concurrency does NOT reliably race in this environment for clock-in either. The test is kept (asserts loosely: never two 201s, never a raw 500, exactly one open `AttendanceLog` afterward) because it's still a legitimate, valid outcome proving the ordinary path behaves correctly, but it does NOT by itself prove the P2002 backstop fires under real concurrency.
    - New test: `DB-level partial unique index rejects a second open AttendanceLog for the same user with P2002` — issues two `prisma.attendanceLog.create` calls back-to-back under the same "no open log" precondition (simulating what two concurrent transactions would each see after passing the app-level guard). **This is the test that actually proves the mechanism**: the second `create` is rejected with `Prisma.PrismaClientKnownRequestError`, `code === 'P2002'`, and exactly one open log persists. Since real request-level concurrency doesn't reliably materialize here, this direct-mechanism proof is the load-bearing one, not the HTTP-concurrency test.
    - Existing sequential test (`rejects a second clock-in while one is already open`) unchanged, still passes via the fast-path guard — no observable-behavior change for the ordinary case.
    - **Isolated low-concurrency run first, as the user explicitly asked**: `node --import tsx --test --test-concurrency=1 server/src/routes/attendance.test.ts` — 3/3 passing. **Then, only after that passed**, full suite at normal concurrency: `npm run test:server` — 108/108 passing, 2 pre-existing unrelated skips, 0 failures.

No other file changed; no frontend/`src/` file touched, per scope.

    - **`POST /clock-out` has the exact same shape of race as clock-in did, unfixed.** `open`'s existence is read via a plain `findFirst` before the transaction, and `tx.attendanceLog.update({ where: { id: open.id }, data: { clockOutAt: new Date() } })` has no re-check that `clockOutAt` is still null — two concurrent clock-outs for the same open log would both succeed, producing two `CLOCKED_OUT` audit rows for one physical event and leaving `clockOutAt` set to whichever commits last. Unlike clock-in, this one is cheap to close with no migration needed at all — it's an UPDATE, so the exact `swapActions.ts`/`joinActions.ts`/`staffDirectory.ts` conditional-`updateMany`-plus-count-check pattern applies directly (`where: { id: open.id, clockOutAt: null }`). A natural, low-effort next pickup.

A manager reviewing the audit trail for a disputed shift via `AuditLog.shiftId` (indexed specifically for this) would find no attendance events linked to it. Minor completeness gap, not a security issue.
    - **Clock-in's `shiftId` body param is only checked for same-venue ownership (`ownedOrNotFound`), never that the shift is actually assigned to the effective target user** — a STAFF session could clock in against a coworker's shift id (or an unassigned one) at the same venue, linking the attendance record to a shift nobody assigned them. Pre-existing, unrelated to the race fix.
    - **The new migration's raw SQL has no defensive pre-check for pre-existing violating rows baked into the `.sql` file itself** — this matches, not deviates from, this codebase's own established convention (see the `User.phone` unique-index migration above in this file: its safety check was also a manual pre-apply step, not code embedded in the migration). Noted only so it isn't mistaken for an inconsistency; if this exact migration file is ever replayed against a different database that already has duplicate open logs, Postgres will refuse to build the index with a clear, loud error — the correct failure mode for hitting bad data, not a bug.

  - **`router.tsx`'s own comment above `RequireSession`** claimed `/schedule` was safe to leave `managerOnly`-unset because it "mix[es] staff-readable/staff-usable content with manager-only sub-actions that already fail informatively per-action rather than page-wide" — **that claim was false, verified by reading the actual route file, not just trusting the comment.** `shifts.ts`'s `POST /`, `PATCH /:id`, `DELETE /:id`, `POST /bulk`, and `POST /:locationId/publish` were ALL gated by `requireSession` ONLY — no `requireManager`, unlike every mutation route in `floorPlan.ts`. `ownedOrNotFound`/`assertOwnsLocation` only checked that a shift belonged to the caller's own VENUE, never that the caller had any special role or relationship to that specific shift.
  - **Concrete impact, now closed**: any signed-in STAFF session — not just a manager, any real employee's own login — could call `PATCH /api/shifts/:id` or `DELETE /api/shifts/:id` for ANY shift at their venue, including a coworker's, directly via the API (no UI needed — the endpoint was always reachable this way, and confirmed during this fix that the Shift Editor's own client renders these controls with no client-side role check either, so this was reachable through the real UI too, not just a crafted request). The `req.user!.systemRole === 'STAFF'` check on `createdById`/`actorId` only forced the AUDIT LOG's actor field to the caller's own id — it never blocked the mutation itself.
  - **Fix**: `requireManager` (already existing, used identically across `staffDirectory.ts`/`floorPlan.ts`) added to all 5 routes, right after `requireSession` in each middleware chain — `shiftsRouter.post('/', requireSession, requireManager, ...)` and equivalent for the other four. The `STAFF ? self : ...` on-behalf-of ternaries in each handler are now unreachable dead code (no STAFF session can pass `requireManager` to reach them) — left in place rather than simplified away, matching this codebase's existing defense-in-depth style at every other on-behalf-of site, and because the user's ask was specifically to add the gate, not refactor the business logic around it.
  - **Verified no existing test assumed STAFF could reach these routes** before touching anything: every session fixture in `shifts.test.ts` was already a MANAGER; adding the gate broke nothing pre-existing (confirmed by the full suite passing unchanged, not just assumed).
  - **New tests**: one proving all 5 routes reject a real STAFF session with a clean 403 (not a raw 500, not a silent success), with a follow-up check that nothing from any rejected call actually persisted; a companion test proving a real MANAGER session still succeeds on the three routes (`POST /`, `DELETE /:id`, `POST /:locationId/publish`) that had no prior manager-session test coverage of their own — added specifically so a middleware-ordering mistake (e.g. `requireManager` placed before `requireSession`) would fail a test instead of silently breaking shift creation/deletion/publishing for every real manager. Both isolated-run (low concurrency) and full-suite (normal concurrency) clean.
  - **Live-verified against the real running server + real DB, not just the test harness**: a real STAFF session's `POST`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`/`POST /bulk`/`POST /:locationId/publish` all returned a real 403 with the expected error body; a real MANAGER session's `POST`, `PATCH`, and `DELETE` all still succeeded normally end-to-end (a shift was actually created, updated, published, and deleted); confirmed zero shifts remained afterward, so none of the rejected STAFF calls left anything behind.

Whether `/schedule` now belongs in that same `managerOnly` bucket is a real product/UX decision (a broken-feeling page, not a security issue) left for the user's own call, not guessed at — this fix's own scope was specifically "add the server-side gate and fix the stale comment," not "redesign who can reach `/schedule`."

- [x] **ORCHESTRATOR'S FINAL WHOLE-BRANCH REVIEW OF THIS PHASE'S THREE TASKS (2026-09-05) — 3-angle multi-agent pass over the attendance migration + JoinFlow redirect + AI-audit combined diff.** Five real issues found and fixed; one already-flagged item (`shifts.ts`, above) reconfirmed as unrelated to this diff, not re-litigated:
  - **`isSafeReturnTo`'s `/join` loop-guard was case-sensitive; `react-router-dom`'s route matching is not.** Verified empirically both ways: `isSafeReturnTo('/JOIN')` returned `true` (the guard's `startsWith('/join')` missed it), and `matchPath('/join', '/JOIN')` from this project's own installed `react-router` genuinely matches (its default `caseSensitive` is `false`, confirmed by contrast with an explicit `true`). A link like `/join?mode=login&returnTo=%2FJOIN` would pass validation, then really route back into `JoinContent` on navigation — the exact loop the guard exists to prevent, just reachable via a differently-cased path. Fixed: the guard now lowercases before comparing. Covered by a new test.
  - **`returnTo` was honored on the JOIN (self-registration) success path too, not just login.** Invite links (`/join?location=<id>`, minted by `onboarding.ts`) carry no signature over their query string, so anyone could append `&returnTo=/floor-plan` and redirect a brand-new hire somewhere unexpected the instant they're auto-approved — a real, if minor, gap in a mechanism that was only ever meant to serve `RequireSession`'s login redirect. Fixed: `returnTo` now only applies inside the `isLogin` branch of `handleVerify`; the join-mode success branch always hard-codes `/my-shifts`, exactly as before this whole feature existed.
  - **`attendance.ts`'s P2002 catch matched on error code alone — a real latent trap, caught before it could bite.** The catch was written to assume any P2002 inside the clock-in transaction was the new partial-unique-index violation, but this transaction also writes an `AuditLog` row, and any future unique constraint added to either model would also throw P2002 and get silently swallowed as "already clocked in" instead of surfacing as the real bug it'd be. **The first fix attempt was itself wrong, caught by direct empirical verification, not assumption**: it checked `meta.target` for the literal index name (`'attendance_logs_one_open_per_user'`) — but a throwaway script against the real DB showed Prisma's actual `meta.target` for this specific raw index is `["user_id"]` (the column, never the index name), which would have made the check permanently false and silently broken the whole migration's app-level handling (every genuine race would then rethrow as a raw 500 instead of a clean 409). Corrected to an exact-match check on `["user_id"]`, re-verified against the real error, all 3 attendance tests still pass in isolation.
  - **The same migration's own concurrency test allowed `[201, 201]` as a hypothetical valid outcome — confirmed by two independent review angles as dead and self-defeating**: if the migration works, the DB can commit at most one open-log insert per user, so two genuine concurrent clock-ins can never both return 201; the test's own very next assertion (`openLogs.length === 1`) would independently catch that case anyway, so allowing it only weakened what the test proved. Narrowed to the one correct outcome, `[201, 409]`.
  - **`package.json`'s new `src/components/*.test.ts` glob (added for `JoinFlow.test.ts`) was non-recursive**, unlike `test:server`'s own `**/*.test.ts` two lines below it in the same file — a latent test-skipping trap for the next component test added one directory deeper (e.g. under `src/components/shiftsync/`), which would silently never run while `npm test` stayed green. Widened to `src/components/**/*.test.ts`.
  - **Also fixed, lower severity**: `RequireSession` now preserves `location.hash` when building `returnTo` (no live route reads a hash today, so this was latent, not exploitable — cheap to future-proof against the day one does). Test setup duplicated 3x across `attendance.test.ts` (1 pre-existing + 2 new) extracted into one `createTestStaff(label)` helper.

- [ ] **ROSTER-PARSING GAP SCOPING (2026-09-05) — investigation only, no code changed, awaiting the user's own design decision before any build.** Three real, long-logged gaps investigated in full: multi-outlet single-file rosters, legend-code shift systems, and bilingual Arabic/English layouts. Method: read the actual live pipeline end to end (`schedules.ts`'s branching, every parser file, `resolveRows.ts`, `types.ts`, the VLM prompt/schema) rather than assuming from the gap names.
  - **Multi-outlet — STRUCTURAL, genuinely large, needs its own design.** Not a "tag each row" fix: `locationId` is derived once from session before parsing even starts (`schedules.ts:87`), threaded as a single value through `resolveRowsAgainstDatabase`, `uploadCache`'s `CachedBatch`, and `persistShifts` — and `ParsedShiftRow`/`PreviewRow` (`types.ts`) have no field to carry a second venue's identity at all. The VLM schema's `venueTemplateNotes` is singular free text, not per-section outlet data. Closing this needs a new row-level field threaded through the whole pipeline, multi-section header detection in the deterministic parser (ambiguous against existing role-section headers), a way to resolve an outlet label to a real `Location` row (a new resolution problem — `locationId` currently comes from session, never the file), and schema changes to the cache/persistence layer.
  - **Legend-codes — smallest of the three, real gap confined to the deterministic/Docling path only.** The deterministic parser's only code vocabulary is a fixed, hardcoded `LEAVE_CODES` table (`OFF`/`AL`/`SICK`/etc.) — never derived from anything printed in the file; any venue-specific code (e.g. `A`=early, `M`=mid, defined by an in-file legend) falls straight to an "unresolved" anomaly. `parseExcelGrid` always returns `legend: []`; Docling reuses this exact same interpreter, inheriting the gap unchanged. The Gemini/Ollama vision path, by contrast, ALREADY has real, designed legend handling — `vlmPrompt.ts` explicitly instructs the model to detect a legend and resolve each coded cell using it, entirely inside the model's own single response (server code only carries the model's legend through as display metadata afterward, never re-applies it). So this is architecturally present and untested for the two VLM paths, and a genuine but bounded gap for the deterministic path specifically: needs a legend-block detection step (locate a small "Key: A=07:00-15:00" region) plus threading a per-file code→time map into `parseCellValue`, parallel to the existing `LEAVE_CODES` special-case but dynamic.
  - **Bilingual Arabic/English — the largest and most uncertain, confirmed for the reason the user suspected, PLUS a real correctness bug found along the way that isn't really an "Arabic support" feature request at all.** `normalizeHeader` (`templates.ts`) strips every non-Latin/non-digit character via regex — two DISTINCT Arabic names or role labels can normalize to the same empty-string key. Since `resolveRows.ts`'s `nameKey` IS `normalizeHeader`, and this function builds the `userByName`/`roleByName` lookup maps every one of the five ingestion paths (deterministic, Docling, Gemini, Ollama, workbook) feeds into via the single shared `resolveRowsAgainstDatabase` stage, this isn't a clean "no match" for Arabic content — it's a silent **collision**, where an Arabic-named row can resolve to the WRONG person/role rather than correctly falling into an `unmatched`/`new_employee` warning. This is a real, live correctness bug independent of any Arabic-specific feature work, confirmed to affect all five paths equally. On top of that: `deterministicGridParser.ts`'s structural role-header-detection fallback (`isRoleHeaderLabel`) is Latin-only by construction (checks `letters === letters.toUpperCase()` after stripping non-`[A-Za-z]` chars — empty for pure Arabic text, so it can never recognize an Arabic-only section header); day-name vocabularies (`DAY_OFFSET`) are English-only in two files; and PDF text-extraction (`pdfTableExtractor.ts`, glyph-position-based) has an unverified RTL glyph-ordering risk with no test fixture in this repo to check against. The existing fallback chain already routes SOME unrecognizable shapes to Gemini (which may already handle Arabic reasonably, being multilingual — genuinely unverified, not confirmed working), but a long-format Excel file with English headers/Arabic cell values succeeds at extraction and carries the (a)/(b) bugs downstream silently, so "does it gracefully degrade" is shape-dependent, not one clean answer. No safe minimal first step identified without the user picking which facet(s) to scope — explicitly left for their own design decision, per their own instruction, not guessed at.
  - **Not checked off** — this entry records a completed investigation, not a completed fix; left as an open checkbox on purpose so it isn't mistaken for "done."
- [x] **ORCHESTRATOR'S FINAL WHOLE-BRANCH REVIEW OF THIS BATCH (2026-09-05) — 4-angle multi-agent pass over the ShiftEditorLink + ROLE_ALIASES + Floor Plan Notes diff.** One serious, real bug found and fixed; two smaller ones fixed for consistency/robustness; several other findings reconfirmed already-flagged follow-ups (not re-litigated) or judged genuinely out of scope.
  - **The serious one: `resolveRowsAgainstDatabase` tried the alias-canonicalized role name BEFORE a venue's own raw exact match, so a venue that seeds a Role whose name coincidentally equals a `ROLE_ALIASES` key had every such row silently redirected to the wrong, generic role — matched, with no warning surfaced at all.** Concretely: a venue with its own distinct `"GRO"` Role (separate from `"Host"`) would have every `"GRO"`-roleName row silently reassigned to `"Host"`'s role id, because `canonicalRoleName('GRO')` → `'Host'` was tried first and matched before the raw `roleByName.get('gro')` lookup ever got a chance. This is exactly the domain this task's own vocabulary additions made MORE likely to hit in practice — `"Captain"`, `"Outlet Manager"`, `"Maitre D"`, `"Barback"`, `"Mixologist"` are all plausible names for a venue's own genuinely-distinct Role, not just generic synonyms. Fixed by swapping the lookup order: raw exact match first, alias-canonicalized name as the fallback only when the raw string isn't itself a real role at that venue — verified this doesn't weaken the intended synonym-matching case (no venue seeds a role literally named after its own synonym, e.g. `"Server"` when the real role is `"Waiter"`). Two new tests added to `resolveRows.test.ts`, one proving the venue's own role wins, one proving the alias fallback still fires when no such role exists.
  - **Additional real vocabulary gaps found by the same review and added**: `"FB Manager"` (a genuinely distinct one-token normalization from `"F&B"`'s two-token form — a real miss the original PR's own commit message claimed to cover exhaustively but didn't), `"Guest Relations Manager"` (a real one-grade-up GRO title), `"Commis de Cuisine"` (standard French-kitchen junior title, parallel to the already-added `"Commis Chef"`), and plural forms for the F&B/Guest-Relations entries (a section header grouping several people, e.g. `"Food & Beverage Managers"`, is a realistic sheet shape the singular-only entries missed).
  - **`SectionOverlay.tsx`'s new `truncateNote` helper (from the Floor Plan Notes fix above) had a real Unicode bug, fixed**: `.slice(0, maxLength)` operates on raw UTF-16 code units, so a note ending near the 80-character boundary with an astral-plane emoji could be cut mid-character (a lone unpaired surrogate — a mangled glyph) or split a ZWJ/combining-mark sequence (a family emoji, a flag, Arabic diacritics — all plausible in this app's Dubai/GCC context). Fixed with `Intl.Segmenter`-based grapheme-cluster splitting (standard in every evergreen browser this app targets), with a graceful fallback to the simpler code-unit slice if it's ever unavailable. Verified directly: a note engineered to put an emoji right at the naive slice boundary produces a mangled unpaired surrogate with the old code, an intact emoji with the fix.
  - **`RequireSession`'s `managerOnly` check flipped from a negative check (`=== 'STAFF'`) to a positive one (`!== 'MANAGER' && !== 'OWNER'`), matching `ShiftEditorLink`'s own fix earlier in this file** — for the identical reason: `loadSession()` (`api/identity.ts`) parses a `localStorage` blob at runtime with no validation that `systemRole` is actually one of the three expected values, so a corrupted/tampered session could carry an unexpected role string that a negative check would let fall through unredirected onto a `managerOnly` page, while `ShiftEditorLink` (already positive-checked) correctly hid the link for that same session — an inconsistency between "can navigate there" and "can see the entry point." Live-verified in a real headless browser with a deliberately garbled `systemRole` value: both the link-hiding and the page redirect now correctly fail closed together.
  - **Already-flagged follow-ups, reconfirmed by this review but NOT re-litigated or fixed here** (both already named as deferred in this file): `/people`'s own unconditional "Onboarding" link has the same shape of gap `/scheduling`'s link had before its fix; the MANAGER/OWNER role check is now written fresh in three places (`RequireSession`, `ShiftEditorLink`, `SchedulingRoute.tsx`) with no shared helper, and `deterministicParser.ts`'s independent `ROLE_HIERARCHY` table still disagrees with `ROLE_ALIASES` on overlapping terms (confirmed concrete, live-pipeline-reachable divergences: `'maitre d'` → `SUPERVISOR` there vs `'Management'` here; `'barback'` → `WAITER` there vs `'Bartender'` here).
  - **Judged out of scope, not fixed**: the pre-existing (not newly introduced) false-positive surface in `isRoleHeaderLabel` (a freeform day-cell note matching a single-word alias like `"Captain"` could misclassify a whole employee row as a section header) — this review's own count of newly-added single-word aliases (8: `captain`/`captains`/`barback`/`barbacks`/`mixologist`/`mixologists`/`gro`/`gra`) corrects this file's earlier, understated count of 3; the `title`-attribute tooltip's poor discoverability on touch devices (this screen's most likely real device class per the product's own stated venue-device context) — the aria-label remains genuinely accessible via screen reader regardless; and the `opacity-70` value on the new `StickyNote` icon not matching AGENTS.md's documented 87/60/38% text-opacity scale — a design-system nit on a small decorative icon, not a functional issue.
  - **Verified after every fix above**: `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build` all clean; isolated run of `resolveRows.test.ts`/`deterministicGridParser.test.ts`/`deterministicParser.test.ts` at low concurrency, 42/42 passing; full `npm run test:server` at normal concurrency, 124/124 passing (2 pre-existing unrelated skips); `npm test` (frontend) 31/31; live-verified in a real headless browser for both the emoji-truncation fix and the garbled-role-session fix.
- [x] **`withAuditedTransaction` SHARED HELPER (2026-09-05) — closes the "No shared `withAuditedTransaction` helper exists" follow-up named and deferred twice in this file (the 2026-09-04 final-review entry and the TOCTOU-fix entry above).** Pure mechanical refactor, no behavior change: the hand-copied `prisma.$transaction(async (tx) => { ...mutate...; await writeAuditLog(tx, {...}); return x; })` shape, independently re-written at every one of ~22 real call sites (more than this file's own earlier "~13" estimate — that number undercounted; the true count once every site was individually read was 22, e.g. `floorPlan.ts` alone has 4, not 1), is now one function.
  - **Shape**: `withAuditedTransaction<T>(client, mutate: (tx) => Promise<T>, buildEntry: (result: T) => AuditLogEntryInput | null | undefined): Promise<T>` in `server/src/lib/auditLog.ts`, colocated with `writeAuditLog` (whose entry-object type was pulled out into a newly-exported `AuditLogEntryInput` so both functions share one type). Internally: `client.$transaction(async (tx) => { const result = await mutate(tx); const entry = buildEntry(result); if (entry) await writeAuditLog(tx, entry); return result; })`. `buildEntry` returning `null`/`undefined` skips the write — used by `floorPlan.ts`'s publish route, which only ever audited when at least one row actually changed (`updateResult.count > 0`).
  - **The two order-sensitive DELETE sites kept their literal statement order, not flattened into the default post-mutate shape**: `shifts.ts`'s `DELETE /:id` and `floorPlan.ts`'s `DELETE /assignments/:assignmentId` both wrote their audit row BEFORE the delete, not after. Since the helper's own default always writes after `mutate` resolves, these two calls instead have `mutate` itself call `writeAuditLog(tx, ...)` directly (still the same shared low-level writer, just invoked inline in its original position) followed by the delete, and pass `() => null` as `buildEntry` so the helper doesn't also write a second row afterward — documented inline at both call sites so a future reader doesn't mistake it for an inconsistency.
  - **`swapActions.ts`'s `decideSwapRequest` — the one call site with real extra guard logic beyond the simple shape — converted with zero change to its actual mechanics**: the atomic `shift.updateMany` guard, the `count === 0` → `throw new ShiftAlreadyReassignedError()`, and the `.catch((err) => { if (err instanceof ShiftAlreadyReassignedError) return null; throw err; })` wrapped around the OUTSIDE of the `withAuditedTransaction(...)` call (not inside it) are all untouched — the helper doesn't know this error type exists, the thrown error just propagates out of `mutate` → out of `client.$transaction` → out of `withAuditedTransaction` uncaught, exactly as it did out of a raw `prisma.$transaction(...)` before. Same pattern applied to `joinActions.ts`'s two `decideJoinRequest` branches (`JoinRequestAlreadyReviewedError`) and `attendance.ts`'s clock-in (`AlreadyClockedInError`, plus its own P2002 backstop `.catch`) and `staffDirectory.ts`'s PATCH (`StaffRecordChangedConcurrentlyError`) — all four pre-existing guard-error call sites converted with their `.catch(...)` left wrapping the helper call, unchanged.
  - **All ~22 sites converted**: `staffDirectory.ts` (POST /, PATCH /:userId), `shifts.ts` (POST /, PATCH /:id, DELETE /:id — bulk-create and publish routes were deliberately left alone, they call no `writeAuditLog` at all), `rotaTemplates.ts` (POST /, DELETE /:id, POST /:id/apply), `attendance.ts` (clock-in, clock-out), `floorPlan.ts` (assignment create, assignment delete, notify, publish), `policyDocuments.ts` (upload, delete), `schedules.ts` (upload-confirm), `eightySix.ts` (create, back-on), `voice.ts` (MARK_AVAILABILITY, REQUEST_SWAP — the four bare-`writeAuditLog`-with-no-mutate branches, APPROVE/DECLINE_SWAP/JOIN, correctly left as direct `writeAuditLog(prisma, ...)` calls, matching the 2026-09-04 atomicity entry's own note that they have nothing to pair with), `swapRequests.ts` (POST /), `swapActions.ts` (`decideSwapRequest`), `joinActions.ts` (`decideJoinRequest`'s decline and approve branches). `availabilityActions.ts` needed NO change — `markAvailability` is a plain upsert helper accepting an externally-supplied `tx`, never wraps its own transaction; the actual `withAuditedTransaction` conversion for its one real call site lives in `voice.ts` (its REST counterpart, `routes/availability.ts`, was out of scope per this task's own boundary and left untouched, consistent with `signup.ts`/`onboarding.ts`/`join.ts`).
  - **Verification, given "no behavior change" was the entire point**: `npm run server:typecheck` clean before and after. - [x] **`normalizeHeader` SILENT-MISASSIGNMENT BUG CLOSED, AS ITS OWN URGENT FIX (2026-09-05) — separate from, and narrower than, full bilingual support (still deferred, see below).** `normalizeHeader`/`nameKey` widened from an ASCII-only `[^a-z0-9]+` character class to Unicode-aware `[^\p{L}\p{N}]+` — two different Arabic (or any non-Latin script) names/roles no longer both collapse to the same empty-string key and silently resolve to each other's DB record. `\p{L}`/`\p{N}` are strict supersets of `a-z`/`0-9`, so every existing ASCII/English/French-service match is unaffected. Live-verified against the real DB: two real Users seeded with genuinely different Arabic names (`محمد أحمد`, `فاطمة علي`) resolved from two roster rows to their own correct, distinct records — no collision, no cross-assignment.
  - **A second real instance of the exact same bug, found and fixed in the same pass**: `parseVisionOllama.ts`'s completeness-check safety net (`normalizeNameForCompare`) had its OWN independent, still-ASCII-only reimplementation of the identical stripping logic — meaning two different Arabic employee names collapsed to the same key there too, silently defeating the "did the model drop an employee" retry check for exactly the population this whole fix is about. Replaced with the shared, now-fixed `normalizeHeader` instead of a second copy. New test proves the check now correctly detects and retries when one of two differently-named Arabic employees goes missing from a structured extraction — with the old local copy, both names normalized to `""` and the check couldn't tell.
  - **A promising-looking follow-on fix was attempted and DELIBERATELY REVERTED after review — worth recording in detail so it isn't retried blindly.** To also fix the (lower-severity, safe-failure-mode) gap where the SAME Arabic name spelled with vs. without diacritics (tashkeel) produces different keys, `.normalize('NFKD')` + stripping all Unicode combining marks (`\p{M}`) was added — verified to correctly fold Arabic tashkeel AND, as a side benefit, fold Latin accents (closing a separately-documented "Maître d'Hôtel doesn't match its plain-ASCII alias" gap too). **But this was reverted** after review surfaced, and direct empirical verification confirmed, that a blanket `\p{M}` strip reintroduces the EXACT silent-collision bug this whole fix exists to close, for scripts where combining marks are load-bearing rather than decorative: two genuinely different Vietnamese names, `"Nguyễn"` and `"Nguyên"`, both normalized to `"nguyen"` under the NFKD+strip approach (verified: `normalizeHeader('Nguyễn') === normalizeHeader('Nguyên')` was `true`) — worse than the diacritic-matching gap it was fixing, since it's a false POSITIVE (wrong match) not a false negative (safe non-match). Reverted cleanly; `normalizeHeader` stays at the `\p{L}`/`\p{N}` widening only. The diacritic-mismatch gap (same Arabic name, different tashkeel) remains open, but fails SAFE (unmatched/new_employee, a visible manual-review prompt) — the correct tradeoff per the user's own stated standard ("if you can't safely support full normalization in a small fix, fail loud rather than fail silent"), since this already fails loud enough (a real 404/unmatched signal), not silent.
  - **A related, NOT-YET-FIXED, and NOT YET FULLY SCOPED risk found by the same review, worth its own attention before this area is considered fully closed**: even the accepted `\p{L}`/`\p{N}`-only approach has a collision risk for Brahmic/Indic abugida scripts (Devanagari, and likely Bengali/Tamil/Gujarati/etc. by the same mechanism) — dependent VOWEL SIGNS in these scripts are Unicode category Mark (`\p{M}`), not Letter, so they get stripped by the current filter same as any other mark, and unlike Arabic tashkeel (optional pronunciation aids) these vowel signs are load-bearing parts of the word. Verified empirically: `nameKey('रा') === nameKey('रि') === nameKey('र')` — three genuinely different Devanagari syllables collapse to one key. This is NOT something either the original widening or the reverted NFKD attempt introduced — it's present in the currently-shipped `\p{L}`-only fix too, and is a real, live gap for South Asian staff names entered in a native (not Romanized) script, which is plausible for this product's real Dubai/GCC hospitality workforce. Not fixed here — deliberately out of scope for what was asked as an "urgent, isolated, Arabic-focused" fix, and a real per-script investigation (which marks are load-bearing vs. decorative varies by script and can't be solved with one blanket rule) is exactly the kind of thing that belongs in the full bilingual-support design session, not bolted on here. Flagged prominently rather than silently left for a future session to rediscover from scratch.
  - **Verified**: `npm run server:typecheck` clean; full `server/src/parsing/` suite in isolation at low concurrency, 76/76 passing (2 pre-existing unrelated skips), including the two new Arabic-collision regression tests (`resolveRows.test.ts`) and the new Ollama completeness-check regression test (`parseVisionOllama.test.ts`).
- [x] **LEGEND-CODE SHIFT TABLES CLOSED for the deterministic/Docling path (2026-09-05) — closes the "Legend-code shift tables" gap logged earlier in this file and the matching facet of the ROSTER-PARSING GAP SCOPING entry above.** `deterministicGridParser.ts` previously had only a fixed, hardcoded `LEAVE_CODES` absence vocabulary — any venue-specific worked-shift code (e.g. `M`=Morning 07:00-15:00, defined by an in-file legend, not hardcoded anywhere) fell straight to an "unresolved" anomaly, and the legend block itself, when present, got misread as a second phantom shift grid. Docling reuses this exact interpreter (`parseExcelGrid`), so it inherited the same gap. New `detectLegend()` scans for a legend block sitting in a footer region below the main staff grid, separated from it by at least one fully-blank row (confirmed against the real reference fixture's own shape: blank row, "Shift Code Legend" caption, then one code per row). Two recognized shapes: multi-column (`"M" | "Morning" | "07:00-15:00"`, each in its own cell) and single-cell (`"M = Morning 07:00-15:00"`, the shape a PDF-text extraction of the same content tends to produce). `parseCellValue` now checks the file's own detected legend FIRST, before the fixed `LEAVE_CODES` table — a file-specific legend is more specific (literally printed by this venue) than the generic hardcoded vocabulary, and represents a real worked shift rather than an absence. Additive by design: when no legend is found (the vast majority of files), detection is a no-op and every existing code path is completely unchanged — proven by a dedicated no-legend regression test. `result.legend` (previously always `[]`) is now populated with the real detected legend, matching the shape the Gemini/Ollama vision path already surfaced. Also closed, per the same instruction, the untested-but-already-built legend-handling logic in the Gemini/Ollama path itself (`mapVlmResponseToResult`'s legend metadata pass-through, per-employee independent code resolution, and leave-vs-shift categorization for a legend-coded cell) — 4 new tests added, no bug found there, pure coverage.
  - **Documented, honest limitations, not silently glossed over**: a legend printed ABOVE the day-header row, in a side column running alongside the staff rows (not below a blank-row separator), or split across more than the two recognized cell shapes (e.g. code/`=`/time as three separate cells) are not handled. Narrow by design — the goal was closing the specific gap already logged, not building a fully general legend parser.
  - **Three real bugs found by the mandatory review, all fixed before commit — none of them were in the subagent's original diff review pass; found by re-testing after the review, not by the review itself catching them directly for two of the three**: (1) **Blank-row footer boundary was too permissive.** `detectLegend` treated the FIRST fully-blank row anywhere below the header as the permanent start of an excluded "footer" region, then scanned all the way to grid end for a legend-shaped match — which could walk straight through an entirely unrelated, later, blank-row-separated section (a role header + its own real staff rows), silently dropping those real staff rows from `result.rows` AND fabricating a bogus legend entry out of unrelated footer text (e.g. a totals line whose first cell happens to be code-shaped and a later cell happens to be a resolvable time range) — confirmed with a constructed real-shaped test case reproducing exactly that. Fixed by requiring genuine contiguity: once a real entry is found, the scan stops at the next non-matching non-blank row instead of skipping past it; more than one leading non-legend line (tolerating exactly one caption row, e.g. "Shift Code Legend") before any entry is ever found aborts detection entirely, leaving the file's staff rows completely unaffected — the same safe fallback as "no legend detected at all." (2) **The new `dataEndIdx` footer boundary wasn't applied to the column-order-disambiguation sampling loops** (the two-leading-column name-vs-role content-based guess), which still scanned into the excluded legend footer, feeding legend-shaped rows (code/label/time triples) into the isRecognizedRoleAlias hit-rate statistics used to decide which column means what — now correctly capped at `dataEndIdx` like the main staff loop. (3) **`lower in fileLegend` (and the pre-existing `lower in LEAVE_CODES`, found by extension) use the `in` operator on a plain object, which also resolves inherited `Object.prototype` property names** — a day-cell reading exactly `"constructor"` would silently "resolve" against the real Object constructor function (whose `.start`/`.end` are `undefined`) instead of correctly falling through to `'unresolved'`. Fixed via a `hasOwnKey` hasOwnProperty-based helper, applied to every plain-object key lookup in this file (`fileLegend`, `LEAVE_CODES` in all three of its call sites).
  - **A fourth, DEEPER instance of the same `Object.prototype`-collision bug class found while writing the regression test for (3) above — in `resolveRows.ts`, not this file, and NOT limited to legend codes: `canonicalRoleName`/`isRecognizedRoleAlias`'s `ROLE_ALIASES[key] ?? raw` / `nameKey(raw) in ROLE_ALIASES` had the identical hole.** A role/section label that normalizes to `"constructor"` (or `"toString"`, `"hasOwnProperty"`, etc.) made `canonicalRoleName` silently return the real `Object` constructor FUNCTION instead of falling through to the raw string — since a function reference is truthy, `?? raw` never fired — which is worse than the deterministic-parser instance: a non-string value could flow into `ParsedShiftRow.roleName`, a field every downstream consumer (DB writes, UI rendering) assumes is a real string. `isRecognizedRoleAlias` would also incorrectly report such a label as a known role alias. This is the same "silent misassignment via `\p{L}`-adjacent name-key handling" bug family the urgent `normalizeHeader` fix above exists to close, just via a different mechanism (prototype lookup, not Unicode stripping) — found only because writing a thorough regression test for the legend-code fix surfaced it empirically, not because either mandatory review pass caught it directly. Fixed identically: `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(ROLE_ALIASES, key)` in place of both the bracket access and the `in` check. Two new tests added to `resolveRows.test.ts` proving `"constructor"`/`"toString"`/`"hasOwnProperty"` all now correctly fall through / report `false`, instead of hitting `Object.prototype`.
  - **One finding from the review flagged but deliberately NOT fixed — future-drift risk, not a demonstrated bug**: the new `RANGE_SEP` (`(?:-|–|to)`) time-range separator and the pre-existing multi-segment split-shift parser's own inline separator (`[-–to]+`, a character class with genuinely different matching semantics) are two independent definitions of "what separates a time range" a few dozen lines apart. Unifying them risks changing the behavior of the older, already-tested, working split-shift parser for an edge case (repeated/chained separator characters) neither currently-passing test suite exercises — left as two separate patterns rather than risk a regression in unrelated working code for a maintainability nit with no demonstrated current failure.
  - **Two more real bugs found by the MANDATORY FINAL WHOLE-BRANCH REVIEW (run after the above was already fixed and committed-pending) — both in the contiguity fix itself, both fixed before commit.** (1) **The contiguity fix's own "stop at the first non-match once ≥1 entry is found" rule was order-dependent and silently dropped real entries.** A timeless/caption line (e.g. "OFF = Day Off", which never resolves — no time range) sitting BETWEEN two real codes, not just after all of them, permanently truncated the scan — a real venue's own legend has no guaranteed ordering (this file's own reference fixture happens to have OFF last, which is exactly why the original bug shipped unnoticed). Reproduced live: legend order M/OFF/N caused `N` to never enter the map. Fixed by replacing the leading-only tolerance with a consecutive-streak model: a non-matching line increments a streak counter and is tolerated (up to 1 in a row), a real match resets the streak to 0 — so a timeless/caption line anywhere in the block gets its own fresh tolerance instead of being a one-time allowance, while two non-matching lines in a row still aborts detection entirely (preserving the original fix's own protection against the unrelated-mid-sheet-section false positive). (2) **The contiguity fix didn't close the false-positive gap it was built for when the bogus row is the very FIRST thing after the separator.** A lone footer line directly below the blank separator (nothing before it to trip any streak) that coincidentally matches the legend shape — e.g. `["TOTAL", "Hours", "09:00-17:00"]` — was still fabricated into a bogus entry, since `CODE_TOKEN` (`[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9]{0,5}`, up to 6 characters) matched ordinary short English words as readily as a real short code. Fixed by capping `CODE_TOKEN` at 3 characters total — every real shift-code convention found in this session's own earlier research and the actual reference fixture is single-letter or a short abbreviation (`M`/`E`/`N`/`G`, `OFF`, `AL`), never a whole word, so this closes the gap without excluding any known real code (verified: no existing test uses a code longer than 3 characters).
  - **Verified**: `npm run server:typecheck` clean throughout (before and after all six fixes across both review passes). Full `deterministicGridParser.test.ts` + `parseVision.test.ts` + `resolveRows.test.ts`, isolated at low concurrency: 56/56 passing, including the 2 new tests from this final review pass (order-independence with OFF in the middle; the lone-"TOTAL"-row case) on top of the 3 regression tests from the first review pass and the subagent's own 8 new legend tests.

(2) Day-name vocabularies (`DAY_OFFSET` in both `deterministicGridParser.ts` and `parseText.ts`) are English-only. (3) `pdfTableExtractor.ts`'s glyph-position-based PDF text extraction has an unverified RTL glyph-ordering risk, with no real bilingual PDF fixture in this repo to test against. Also folds in the newly-found Devanagari/Brahmic-script vowel-sign collision risk named directly above, since it's the same class of "which normalization rule is safe for which script" design question. Not scoped further, not started — awaiting the user's own design decision.
- [x] **FULL BACKEND PERFORMANCE AUDIT (2026-09-05) — N+1 queries, missing indexes, unbounded queries, app-code computation across every route in `server/src/routes` and every Prisma query in the codebase, ahead of real pilot venues coming online.** Two parallel research agents covered the full route set (one per half, plus their direct call graphs — `lib/actions/*`, `parsing/persistShifts.ts`, `parsing/resolveRows.ts`), each producing a ranked, file:line-cited findings list; a third pass (this session) cross-referenced every field they flagged against the actual `prisma/schema.prisma` index coverage. **No N+1 loop patterns were found anywhere** (a real, notable clean bill — the codebase consistently uses batched `findMany({ id: { in: [...] } })`/`updateMany`/set-based transactions rather than per-row queries in a loop; `resolveRowsAgainstDatabase`, `floorPlan.ts`'s publish route, and `availabilityActions.ts` were specifically named as good patterns worth preserving).
  - **Fixed — safe, contained, no API/response-shape change:**
    - **3 missing indexes added** (`prisma/migrations/20260905191746_add_performance_indexes`): `AttendanceLog(userId, clockInAt)` — the weekly-hours screen's date-range query had zero index support on the date side, forcing a full per-user scan; `JoinRequest(phone, status)` — `myShifts.ts`'s "do I have a pending join request?" check (hit on every staff home-screen load) filters by `phone`+`status` with no `locationId` in the query at all, so the existing `[locationId, status]` index gave zero help; `PolicyDocument.fileUrl` promoted to `@unique` — the file-serve route looks documents up by this field alone, with NO index at all before this, across every venue's documents platform-wide, for a field that's already unique by construction (`randomUUID()`-generated at write time — confirmed zero existing duplicates via a live query against the dev DB before adding the constraint).
    - **`shifts.ts`'s publish route (`POST /:locationId/publish`)**: was `findMany`-ing every column of every shift in the week just to check non-emptiness and count distinct assigned users. Rewritten to a single `prisma.shift.groupBy({ by: ['userId'], _count: true })` — one query instead of a full-row fetch, giving both the total count (sum of `_count` across groups) and the distinct-assigned-user count (groups with non-null `userId`) from one round-trip. **First-draft note**: an initial rewrite used two separate queries (`count()` + a `distinct` `findMany()`) — closed the over-fetch problem but reopened a narrower one (two non-atomic reads instead of one snapshot, independently flagged by 3 of the review's 5 finder passes); caught and consolidated into the single `groupBy` before commit, which is strictly better than either draft.
    - **`swapActions.ts`'s `decideSwapRequest`**: was `include: { shift: true }` (every Shift column) for a function that only ever reads `.userId` (the TOCTOU guard) and `.locationId` (the audit entry) — narrowed to `select: { userId: true, locationId: true }`. Also reuses `SWAP_REQUEST_INCLUDE`'s own `requestedBy`/`targetUser` selects directly instead of retyping them, closing a constant-duplication risk the review's own convention-angle pass flagged.
    - New regression test (`shifts.test.ts`) proves the publish rewrite preserves exact `notifiedCount` semantics: 2 shifts for the same user count once, an unassigned shift doesn't count at all.
  - **Benchmarked, not just asserted — via `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` server-side execution time (not wall-clock, which a first attempt showed is dominated by network round-trip to this project's remote Supabase dev DB at these row counts and produced misleading/inverted numbers)**: seeded a representative dataset (50 users, ~39k attendance rows and ~40k join requests — simulating ~3 years of platform-wide accumulation, the timeframe the audit itself flagged as when "fine today" tips into "a real problem"), then measured each of the 3 newly-indexed queries with the index present vs. dropped (indexes restored after). Results: **AttendanceLog range query, Seq Scan 3.01ms → Index Scan 0.405ms (~7x); JoinRequest phone lookup, 2.77ms → 0.062ms (~45x); PolicyDocument fileUrl lookup, 2.82ms → 0.038ms (~74x)** — all measured on the real dev DB, indexes restored to their fixed state afterward, all benchmark data cleaned up.
  - **Flagged, NOT fixed — genuinely need a design/API decision, not a guess:**
    - **`findPhoneMatches` (`identity.ts`, used by `signup.ts` and every OTP request/verify)** fetches every active User with a phone number **across the entire platform** (deliberately unscoped by `locationId` — the venue isn't known yet at this point in login/signup), then filters in JS via a lossy `phoneDigits()` normalization no index can accelerate. Grows with total system headcount, not any one venue's — the one genuinely cross-tenant query in the whole audit, sitting on the hottest auth path. Real fix needs a persisted, indexed `phoneNormalized` column (computed at write time) plus a backfill migration — a schema/design decision, not a query rewrite. `join.ts` has a smaller, `locationId`-scoped instance of the identical pattern (low priority on its own, but should move in lockstep if the normalized column is ever built).
    - **Sequential per-row `shift.create` inside an interactive `$transaction`** in both `persistShifts.ts` (roster-upload confirm, `schedules.ts`) and `rotaTemplates.ts`'s `POST /:id/apply` — both already correctly documented as intentional (the `tx` is bound to one DB connection, so `Promise.all` wouldn't parallelize anyway), but both are exposed to Prisma's default ~5s transaction timeout at realistic import/apply sizes (a multi-week roster import, or a template applied across many staff/roles). A timeout here rolls back the whole action with no partial credit. Real fix needs a design decision (client-generated ids + a single `createMany` outside the transaction, chunking, or an explicit raised timeout) — each trades off the current "atomic with the audit-log write" guarantee differently.
    - **Three unbounded, ever-growing `findMany`s with no pagination**: `swapRequests.ts`'s `GET /:locationId` (every swap request ever made for the venue, PENDING or long-decided, joined through `Shift`) is the highest-priority of the three — realistically 130-650 rows at 6 months/50 staff today, unboundedly growing since nothing ages it out; `eightySix.ts`'s `includeResolved` branch (900-2,700 rows at 6 months, same shape); `shoutouts.ts`'s and `announcements.ts`'s location feeds (well-indexed, low volume today, but no cap ever). All need an API/UX decision (default status/date filter, or real pagination) that changes the response shape — explicitly out of scope per this task's own instruction not to guess at a frontend-consuming change.

  - **Real bug found, not just a style mismatch: the app never actually loaded Plus Jakarta Sans at all.** `tailwind.css`'s `--font-sans` token named it, but no `<link>`/`@font-face` anywhere ever fetched it — `index.html` had zero font tags, so every screen silently rendered in the `system-ui` fallback the whole time. Fixed by adding the same Google Fonts `<link>` (weights 400;500;600;700;800, matching the reference exactly) the reference itself uses, and pointing `global.css`'s `body` rule at `var(--font-sans)` instead of its own separate hardcoded `system-ui` stack — one source of truth instead of two independent font declarations that happened to almost agree.

  - **Highest-leverage single fix: `.btn`'s font-weight, 600 → 500.** `.btn`/`.btn-primary`/`.btn-ghost` is the de facto shared button class across BOTH the legacy-CSS-era screens and every Tailwind-era component built since (`NotificationSettings`, `NotificationBell`, `VoiceCommandSheet`, etc. all use it) — confirmed against the reference's actual shadcn `button.tsx` (`text-sm font-medium`, no variant ever uses 600) that every button in this app was rendering visibly heavier than intended, everywhere, in one place.
  - **Two raw-CSS panel/dialog titles gained the missing heading treatment** already used everywhere else (`.staff-directory-toggle`, `.fp-picker-head h3`, `.fp-86-header h3` — the last two were rendering as plain unstyled `text-base`/normal-weight text since Tailwind's preflight zeroes out heading defaults and neither rule set its own weight) — brought to `var(--text-lg)` + `font-weight: 600` + `letter-spacing: var(--tracking-tight)`, matching the reference's Dialog title (`text-lg font-semibold leading-none tracking-tight`) and every other panel `<h2>` in the app.
  - **Two genuine stray arbitrary-value drifts found and fixed** (everything else in this micro-tier already matched the reference): `SectionOverlay.tsx`'s pin-badge label was `text-[9px]` — the reference's own `FloorPlanCanvas.tsx` pin equivalent is `text-[8px]`, a 1px drift introduced during the earlier Floor Plan restyle port; fixed to match exactly. `MyShiftsRoute.tsx`'s availability-day status caption was also `text-[9px]`, a size the reference never uses anywhere (it only ever uses 8/10/11px) — snapped to `text-[10px]`, the reference's own "secondary tag under a slightly larger label" tier.
  - **~20 other one-off `global.css` font-sizes snapped onto the new token scale** (`.hint`, `.badge`, `.warnings`, `.cell`/`.cell .role`/`.cell.shift .split`/`.split .period`, `.roster-section-head`, `.dropzone-sub`, `.parse-issues`, `.anomaly-block`, `.chip`, `.preview-table`, `.overnight-tag`, `.preview-sticky-count`) — each mapped to its nearest real Tailwind scale step (e.g. 0.9rem→`--text-sm`, 0.62rem→`--text-2xs`), font-size only, no layout/spacing changes, so the fix carries zero regression risk to any of these components' actual structure. A row whose parsed role text happened to already match a canonical chip label (e.g. "Bartender", pre-highlighted since it's already a valid chip, just not yet a real `Role` for a brand-new venue) gave a manager no visual reason to re-touch it. Root cause: the edits-payload builder only sent a `role`/`employeeName` patch when the edited value differed from the original PARSED STRING, not when the row's role had never actually resolved server-side. Fixed by having "Looks right"/"Done" commit the row's *current* name+role into the edit maps unconditionally, and having the payload builder check map *presence* rather than diffing against the original value — re-verified via the real confirm response (`createdCount: 3, skippedCount: 0`) across 3 repeat E2E runs.
