  - **Deliberate scope-out, not a mocking shortcut**: WhatsApp message delivery itself is never verified (genuinely impractical — no test account, real external service). `onboarding.spec.ts` instead intercepts the `wa.me`/`api.whatsapp.com` popup navigation via `context.route(...).abort()` and asserts on the constructed request URL, so the suite never makes a real outbound call to WhatsApp's servers while still proving both the venue-wide Share link and an individual invite build the correct deep link (phone, encoded message).
  - **Two real, non-obvious things learned while building this that future sessions should know**: (1) a roster-confirmed row for a brand-new venue does **not** create a `User` — `schedules.ts`'s confirm handler only ever looks up an *existing* User by name (`userByName.get(...)  ?? null`, never a `.create`); a "new employee" only becomes a real `User` later, when they self-onboard via the invite link (already documented in `InviteScreen.tsx`'s own comments, easy to miss). The correct server-side proof that a Review edit reached the server is checking for the auto-created `Role` row (e.g. "Bartender" not previously existing for that venue), not a `User`. (2) deleting a test org (`cleanupTestOrgs`) while its browser page still has an in-flight request outstanding is a genuine race: `requireSession`'s async Express middleware has no error wrapper, so a Prisma "relation required, got null" error from a session/user row disappearing mid-request becomes an **unhandled rejection that crashes the whole Node dev-server process** (Node 15+ defaults to terminating on unhandled rejection), not just a 500 for that one request — every spec's `afterEach` now closes the `page` before running cleanup to make this rare rather than closing the gap in `requireSession` itself (a real robustness gap, logged separately below since fixing app error-handling was out of scope for "add tests").
  - **Known, accepted flake — mitigated, not chased further**: the very first test of a fully cold run (nothing warm-started) can occasionally still miss its 90s timeout during the initial `tsx watch` compile + first pooled-Postgres connection, even with `global-setup.ts`'s warm-up query (that query warms the e2e suite's own Prisma client, not the app server's separate one). Every later test in the same run is unaffected. Mitigated via `retries: 1` in `playwright.config.ts` rather than chased to a root cause — confirmed via 3 full cold-start runs that this is the only flake, self-heals on retry, and never recurs mid-suite.
  - **Verified the suite itself, not just the app**: ran the full suite cold-start 3 times (`npx playwright test`); final run: 5/5 passed (1 retried once on the known cold-start flake above, then passed). Ran `server/src/routes/staffDirectory.test.ts` alone (4/4 pass, including the new phone-conflict case) and the full `npm run test:server` suite (151/155 pass; the 2 unrelated failures are the pre-existing shared-dev-Postgres-pool `max clients reached, pool_size: 15` limit already documented above in the notification-broadcast entry, not caused by this change — confirmed by re-running the affected file alone, which passed cleanly). Confirmed zero leftover `__e2e-test__`-prefixed organizations in the real DB after every run via a throwaway check script (deleted after use). `npm run typecheck`, `npm run server:typecheck` (now includes `e2e/` + `playwright.config.ts` via `tsconfig.json`'s `include`), `npm run build`, `npm run lint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings only — `e2e/`/`server/` aren't in ESLint's `files` glob, matching this repo's existing convention of only linting `src/`) all clean.
  - **Follow-up RESOLVED (2026-09-09)**: `requireSession`'s async Express middleware (`server/src/middleware/requireSession.ts`) previously had no try/catch or async-error wrapper — ANY thrown error inside it became an unhandled promise rejection that crashed the entire Node server process for every connected user (Express 4 does not auto-catch a rejected promise from middleware), not just a 500 for the erroring request. Fixed by wrapping the body in try/catch and forwarding via `next(err)` to `app.ts`'s existing error handler — the same mechanism Multer's upload errors already use, so no new error-formatting path was introduced.
    - **Deterministic verification, not mocking**: the original race (a stale Session row concurrent with a User delete) isn't reliably forceable from outside the process — empirically confirmed via a throwaway script racing 15 concurrent real requests against a real concurrent delete, 30 trials, zero reproductions. Rather than mock anything, added a narrow, off-by-default dev flag (`ALLOW_DEV_ERROR_INJECTION`, same shape as the existing `ALLOW_DEV_OTP_ECHO` precedent) that makes `requireSession` throw for one specific 64-char-incompatible sentinel bearer token — a real throw, through the real middleware/try-catch/`next(err)`/error-handler, just deterministically triggered instead of relying on rare timing. Inert for every real token (a real session token is always 64 hex chars from `randomBytes(32).toString('hex')`; the sentinel isn't shaped like one).
    - **Manually verified first** against a real server instance (not just the automated test): the deliberate-throw request got a real `400 {"error":"Deliberate test-injected error..."}` response (not a connection reset), the exact same process PID kept listening afterward, and a concurrent real valid request (fired via `Promise.all` alongside the bad one) succeeded normally with real data.
    - **Permanent regression test added**: `e2e/require-session-error-handling.spec.ts` (real signup, real session token extracted from `localStorage`'s `shiftsync.session` key, real concurrent `Promise.all` of the sentinel-token request + a valid-token request via Playwright's `request` fixture) — asserts the bad request gets `400` with the injected-error message, the concurrent good request gets `200`, `/api/health` still returns `200` right after, and a follow-up request on the same connection still works normally (not a degraded/zombie state). `playwright.config.ts`'s backend `webServer` env now also sets `ALLOW_DEV_ERROR_INJECTION: 'true'` alongside the existing `ALLOW_DEV_OTP_ECHO`.
    - **Verified**: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run lint` all clean. Full `npx playwright test` run (all 6 specs, this one included) passed — one unrelated spec hit the already-documented cold-start flake on its first attempt and passed on the automatic retry, exactly as designed; the new test itself passed clean both times it ran. Zero leftover test data (checked via a throwaway script, deleted after).
- [x] **ADVERSARIAL EDGE-CASE AUDIT (2026-09-10) of the roster-parsing pipeline against 5 constructed fixtures** (`server/test-fixtures/edge-case-audit/`: ALL-CAPS-only roster, novel Title-Case section vocabulary, no-unlabeled-row-above-first-header, a simulated skewed/jittered scanned PDF rasterized via `pypdfium2` then re-embedded as an image-only PDF to force the true no-text-layer vision path, and a combined ALL-CAPS+novel-header worst case). Findings ranked by severity — full report given inline in that session's transcript, not duplicated here. Worst two: the combined fixture produced **total silent data loss** (0 rows/0 anomalies/0 issues — an HTTP-200-shaped "success" with the entire roster gone) and the ALL-CAPS-only fixture silently dropped 3 of 4 employees, both via the same root cause in `deterministicGridParser.ts`'s `isRoleHeaderLabel`: once one real staff row has been seen, EVERY subsequent ALL-CAPS row — real headers AND real staff names alike — satisfies the case-based header signal and gets misclassified as a new section header, silently eating that employee's whole week.
- [x] **FIXED both CRITICAL/HIGH findings from the audit above, same session, immediately following it.** Root heuristic fix: added `rowHasShiftShapedData()` to `deterministicGridParser.ts` — a row carrying real shift/leave/flagged data in ANY day column is structurally proof it's a staff row and can never be misclassified as a header, checked BEFORE the case-based signal at both call sites (`!hasTitleColumn` shape and the `columnOrderAmbiguous` shape) — survives ALL-CAPS names and novel-vocabulary section labels regardless of casing, no vocabulary list to maintain. Independent hard sanity gate, exactly as also requested: new `RosterExtractionAnomalyError`, thrown when an independently re-derived count of "rows in the raw grid that plainly carry real shift data" (`countRowsWithRealShiftData` — a from-scratch pre-scan sharing no state, not even `hasSeenAnyStaffRow`, with the classification loop) comes in higher than how many rows the loop actually routed into `processStaffRow` — a shortfall is proof something dropped real data, regardless of cause, and now surfaces as a loud `422 {errorCode:"roster_extraction_anomaly"}` in `schedules.ts` instead of a silent 200. Skipped for the `columnOrderAmbiguous` shape specifically (already surfaces every unclassifiable row as an explicit anomaly by design — the gate would trip on every such file for no new information).
  - **Two real false-positive/false-negative gaps in the new gate, both found by re-running the FULL existing suite (not just the new fixtures) and both fixed before shipping**: (1) the independent pre-scan didn't know about the main loop's own headcount-row/blank-label exclusions, so a per-shift headcount summary row (e.g. "3" | "2 2" | "1 1" ...) whose numbers coincidentally parsed as valid "start end" pairs via the SAME two-number-cell regex the real parser uses got counted as an expected staff row the main loop correctly (and rightly) skips — reproduced live on the real Gattopardo reference PDF (3 such rows), fixed by mirroring the exact same bare-number/blank-label exclusions in the pre-scan. (2) the pre-scan didn't check the trailing-notes-column leave-code fallback `processStaffRow` itself applies (an employee with blank day columns but a leave code in a side column, e.g. the real file's Tomas/Sintia coincidence, documented in the leave-annotation entry above) — underscounted by the same amount the main loop overcounts, netting out to a real, reproducible mismatch on that exact fixture; fixed by replicating that fallback check in the pre-scan too. Real Gattopardo fixture re-verified at exactly 160 rows/0 change throughout.
  - **One real regression from the structural fix itself, also caught by the full suite**: an existing test (`legend detection does not treat an unrelated mid-sheet blank-separated section as the legend footer`) has a deliberately adversarial footer row `["TOTAL", "Hours", "09:00-17:00"]` — the literal time-range cell now (correctly, per the new structural rule) proves it's not a caption... except this one genuinely isn't a real employee either. Fixed with a small, closed, universal vocabulary (`SUMMARY_ROW_LABELS`: total/totals/subtotal/sum/grand total/hours) checked before the structural override — deliberately NOT the same kind of hardcoded-word-list fragility as the original ALL-CAPS section-header problem, since tabular summary captions are a small closed set that can never collide with a real person's name or a real venue-specific section label, unlike open-ended hospitality role vocabulary.
  - **Re-verified against the 5 audit fixtures after the fix**: fixture 1 (ALL-CAPS) went from 5→21 rows (100% of both employees' full weeks recovered, roles correctly SUPERVISORS/RUNNERS, 0 anomalies/issues). Fixture 5 (combined worst case) went from 0→20 rows (all 4 real employees fully recovered) — the only residual gap is the well-understood, already-documented "no anchor first header" limitation (item 4 below), not data loss: the very first section's 2 employees get `roleName:""` (surfaced via warning), the second section's 2 correctly self-heal to the real header text. Fixtures 2/3 (novel-vocab, no-anchor) unchanged as expected — those are the lower-priority, explicitly-deferred findings, not touched this pass. Fixture 4 (skewed scan) untouched by this fix (vision-path, not deterministic-path) — see the infra-swap entry below.

- [x] **OLLAMA → HOSTED GEMINI/VERTEX AI VISION-FALLBACK SWAP.** The local `qwen2.5vl:7b` Ollama path (`parseVisionOllama.ts`) took 1124s (~18.7min) to process the skewed-scan audit fixture and hallucinated 3 of 21 ground-truth shifts (~86% cell accuracy) — confirmed live before removing it, not assumed. Turned out `parseVision.ts` already had a complete, previously-unused Gemini image-parsing path (`parseRosterImage`, same `ROSTER_VLM_SYSTEM_PROMPT`/`mapVlmResponseToResult` contract Ollama's path was built to mirror) that `schedules.ts` simply never called — the swap was routing 3 call sites to the existing function, not writing a new integration.
  - `parseVision.ts`'s `getClient()` is now Vertex-AI-capable: `GEMINI_VERTEX_PROJECT` + `GEMINI_VERTEX_LOCATION` (an EU region, e.g. `europe-west4`) set → Vertex AI via `@google/genai`'s `{vertexai:true, project, location}` mode, auth via Application Default Credentials (a service account key file or workload identity — no key material in code); otherwise falls back to the existing `GEMINI_API_KEY` Gemini Developer API path (dev/test convenience only, NOT the EU-data-residency-pinned production path). New `isGeminiConfigured()` helper (checks either var) replaces the old bare `GEMINI_API_KEY` presence checks so a Vertex-only deployment isn't mistaken for "not configured." Timeout dropped from Ollama's 600s(-900s-1200s, empirically all too short on this hardware) local-inference budget to `GEMINI_HTTP_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 30s) via the SDK's own `httpOptions.timeout` — a real hosted-API budget, not a local-GPU one. `schedules.ts`'s 3 vision-fallback call sites (direct image upload, PDF-no-text-layer, PDF-text-layer-unrecognized) now call `parseRosterImage` instead of `parseRosterImageOllama`; the 2 PDF branches also send the ORIGINAL PDF buffer directly (`mimeType: 'application/pdf'`) instead of rasterizing first — Gemini/Vertex accepts PDF natively, unlike Ollama's image-only loader, so `rasterizePdfPageToPng` is no longer called from the roster pipeline at all (kept — still used by the unrelated `floorPlan.ts` PDF-preview feature; its stale Ollama-specific doc comment was also fixed). `parseVisionOllama.ts` + its test file deleted outright (genuinely dead in the real request path once the swap landed, not deprecated-in-place).

The model's own `documentAnomalies` correctly flagged the page as skewed without needing to be told, and correctly read the per-row title-column abbreviations ("Sup"/"Run") over the section-header text, matching the deterministic parser's own "row title beats section header" precedence — consistent behavior across both paths on that design choice.

- [x] **ISSUES #12–15 FROM PR #11'S FOLLOW-UP REVIEW CLOSED (2026-09-15) — the lower-severity/data-model-drift tier, filed alongside the correctness/data-integrity findings #1-5 fixed in commit `28cae81`.**
  - **#12 — role auto-create didn't canonicalize.** `schedules.ts`'s edit-resolution role-create loop (confirm's `neededRoleNames`) used the raw trimmed chip label (e.g. "Manager") instead of `canonicalRoleName()`, so a fresh venue got a literal `Role` named "Manager" instead of the canonical "Management" `resolveRows.ts` already produces everywhere else. Fixed by canonicalizing before adding to `neededRoleNames` (still checked against both the raw and canonical `nameKey` before deciding a create is even needed, matching `resolveRowsAgainstDatabase`'s own raw-first-then-canonical lookup order).
  - **#13 — role lookup omitted `isActive`.** The same confirm-edit role lookup (`prisma.role.findMany({ where: { locationId } })`) had no `isActive: true` filter, unlike `GET /api/roles` — a deactivated Role (never a selectable chip) could be silently matched and reused. Adding the filter alone would have introduced a NEW regression: a deactivated role still occupies its name under `@@unique([locationId, name])`, so an edit naming that same role would now 500 on create instead of silently misbehaving. Fixed properly: `roleByName` (active-only, used for matching) is now built alongside a separate `inactiveRoleIdByName` map from one combined query; the create step reactivates (`isActive: true` update) an existing inactive row by that name instead of blind-creating, avoiding both the silent-reuse bug AND the P2002 crash.
  - **#14 — no sequential step gating on `/onboarding/:step`.** A manager could reach Invite via a stale bookmark/typed URL with Venue/Roster never run. Added client-side gating in `OnboardingStateContext.tsx`: a new `unlockedSteps` set (persisted in `sessionStorage`, same mechanism as the existing `uploadResult` mirror, scoped by locationId) tracks which steps a REAL `setStep()` call has ever reached; `resolveEffectiveStep()` honors the URL's requested step only when it's unlocked, otherwise falls back to the furthest step actually reached. Deliberately per-step-unlock (not "index <= max"), so Roster's existing "Skip → Invite, bypassing Review" shortcut still works — Invite gets unlocked directly without requiring Review specifically. Coexists with (does not replace) the 2026-09-08 step-in-URL reload/resume decision: a reload still resumes exactly where the manager left off, since the step they were on is always itself unlocked.
  - **#15 — `baseUrl` trusted from the client with no allowlist.** `onboarding.ts`'s invite-mint endpoint used `req.query.baseUrl` verbatim (falling back to the API's own host) — a compromised/forged client could mint a QR/WhatsApp invite pointing at an attacker-controlled domain. Fixed with a server-side allowlist: `FRONTEND_ORIGIN` env var (comma-separated for multi-environment, e.g. staging+prod), defaulting to `http://localhost:5173` when unset; the client-supplied `baseUrl` is honored only when it's an exact match in that allowlist, otherwise the first configured/default origin is used instead — never the unvalidated query param.
  - **Regression tests**: `schedules.test.ts` gained 2 new cases (canonicalization via alias chip "Manager"→"Management"; deactivated-role edit reactivates instead of silently matching or 500ing). `onboarding.test.ts`'s existing origin test was adapted to set `FRONTEND_ORIGIN` per-test (previously asserted the OLD, now-fixed trust-the-client behavior) plus 2 new cases (forged/unlisted origin is ignored and falls back to the allowlisted one; no-`baseUrl`-and-no-env falls back to the hardcoded default, never the API's own host). `OnboardingStateContext.test.ts` is a new file (pure-function tests for `isOnboardingStep`/`furthestUnlockedStep`/`resolveEffectiveStep` — no React/DOM needed) added under `src/state/`, which wasn't previously in `package.json`'s `test` glob — added `"src/state/**/*.test.ts"` to it.
  - **Verified clean, every stage**: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run server:typecheck`, `npm run lint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings only), `npm run build`, `npm run test` (49/49), `npm run test:server` full suite. The full-suite run showed 12 unrelated failures (shifts/signup/staff-directory 404/403 tests, voice parse-intent tests) — confirmed pre-existing, not caused by this change: the shifts/signup/staff-directory ones passed 14/14 clean when re-run in isolation (the shared dev-Postgres-pool 15-connection cap documented in AGENTS.md and multiple earlier entries above, tripped by the full suite's 13-17+ concurrent PrismaClients), and the voice ones are the documented Gemini free-tier daily-quota exhaustion (`429`/`503 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`), not a code defect.

- [x] **`findHeaderRows` STRUCTURAL GATE (2026-09-21, Issue #22) — a row with ≥2 date-shaped cells is only a header if it isn't really data.** `deterministicGridParser.ts`: new `isPlausibleHeaderRow` replaces the raw "≥2 cells resolve via `resolveHeaderDate`" count in BOTH places `findHeaderRows` used it (the first-header scan AND the "second header row" skip beneath it). Root cause: `resolveDayMonthDate` accepts numeric pairs, so a shift range ending ≤12h (`"9-12"`, `"8-11"`) reads as a calendar date (Dec 9, Nov 8). Verified on unmodified master with a 7-shape probe: (P1) an early shift-shaped row above the real header silently re-dated every shift to Dec 9 / Nov 8; (P2, **worse and more likely in practice**) a real first employee directly under the header with ≥2 such cells was swallowed as a "second header row" and **vanished with no anomaly** (the `RosterExtractionAnomalyError` count starts at `dataStartIdx`, so it can't see it either); (P7) same shift repeated ×3 above the header. Gate mirrors `rowHasShiftShapedData` next door: no date cell also parses as a shift → unambiguously a header; otherwise fall back to structure — dates must span ≥2 distinct days within `MAX_HEADER_DATE_SPAN_DAYS` (13). **Why not the plain "reject if shift-shaped" gate**: `"17/08"` is BOTH a date and a valid 17:00→08:00 shift, and numeric day-month headers are a supported (previously untested) layout — the naive gate would have rejected them; the structural tie-breaker keeps them. "Locked-in header can't be overridden" was already true by construction (the scan `return`s on first accept) — the flaw was purely what qualified. 6 regression tests (3 for Issue #22 — mutation-checked: fail on master, pass with fix — plus 3 guards: numeric-pair header, real second header row, `Name | Monday | …` label).
  - **Residual, documented in the code**: a data row whose shift ranges read as *consecutive* days (`"8-12","9-12","10-12"` = Dec 8,9,10) is indistinguishable from a numeric header by content or structure and is still accepted. A numeric-pair-only header spanning >13 days (monthly `1/09…30/09`) is now rejected → falls through to the Gemini grid path instead of parsing.
  - **Verified separately, NOT fixed here (P3)**: a row of bare numbers above the header (e.g. `Covers | 45 | 60 | 52`) is still taken for the header — `parseDateCell` treats ANY finite number as an Excel serial, so the file parses with `1900-02-13` dates, silently. Not shift-shaped, so this gate can't see it; fixing it means changing date semantics for small numbers (e.g. reject serials < 36526 = year 2000). The repo's only COVERS layout has the panel *below* the header in text, so it's unproven as a real layout — follow-up, not bundled.
  - **Verified**: `deterministicGridParser.test.ts` 33/33 (27 existing + 6), `pdfTableExtractor.test.ts` 13/13 (its `Name | Monday …` test runs through this parser), `npm test` 52/52, `test:server` 262/263 (0 fail, 1 skipped, 0 pool-cap errors) on local Postgres, typecheck ×2 / lint / build clean.
